Its catalogue prices thirty-two titles from **/۱۵ روپے** (فقر نامہ, p. 354) to **/۳۰۰ روپے** (فتوحات مکیہ and کتاب اللمع, pp. 353, 355), gives page counts for the Hujwiri and Ibn Arabi volumes (400, 616, 472, 800, 350, 250 صفحات — pp. 354–355), and names the trade structure: "ناشر: تصوّف فاؤنڈیشن، ۲۴۹/۴ سمن آباد لاہور | واحد تقسیم کار: المعارف، گنج بخش روڈ لاہور، پاکستان" (p. 353), with "شو روم: المعارف ○ گنج بخش روڈ ○ لاہور" (pp. 354–355) — **the sole distributor's address is named for the saint whose book heads the list.** The Foundation describes itself as "لائبریری ○ تحقیق و تصنیف و تالیف و ترجمہ ○ مطبوعات" (pp. 354–355), and frames buying as endowment: "تصوف فاؤنڈیشن کی زیادہ سے زیادہ کتابیں خریدیئے یہ صدقہ جاریہ ہے اِن کتابوں کی تمام آمدن صرفِ اشاعت کُتبِ تصوّف پر صرف ہوتی ہے" (p. 356). The edition's own catalogue line is the only place the annotator and the price appear together: "حدیقۃ الاولیاء | مُصنف: مفتی غلام سرور لاہوری، حواشی: محمد اقبال مجددی | قیمت مجلد /۱۵۰ روپے" (p. 353). **The provenance of the PDF, not of the edition:** "This book has been digitized by Maktabah Mujaddidiyah (www.maktabah.org)… Digitized by Maktabah Mujaddidiyah, 2012. Files hosted at Internet Archive [www.archive.org]", with a statement that the digitizer holds no copyright and an appeal for donations to ghaffari@maktabah.org (p. 361, English).

**How to cite it.** Any entry leaning on the footnotes should cite them as **Muhammad Iqbal Mujaddidi (the annotated edition)**, not as Ghulam Sarwar (p. 306, folio 294; p. 186, folio 184 as printed, حاشیہ 1; p. 159, folio 147, حاشیہ 1). The author himself is, on the book's own showing, **a Lahore mufti of Kotli Muftiyan, of a Qureshi family claiming descent from Baha al-Din Zakariya, of hereditary Suhrawardi practice, trained in the Ghulam Rasul–Ghulam Allah teaching line, writing about saints several of whom he had met** (pp. 211, 222–223, 232–234, folios 199, 210–211, 220–222). Two of the book's named sources are, on the edition's own showing, unverifiable — *Tuhfat al-Wasilin* (pp. 191–192) and *Tashrif al-Shurafa* (p. 212, folio 200, حاشیہ 1) — and that, rather than any single correction, is the edition's most useful property: **it tells the reader which of its own citations cannot be checked.**

## Figures and sites not in the archive

This is the book's acquisition list. *Hadiqat al-Awliya* runs to roughly 243–247 numbered
entries (the contents count **247** across seven *chamans*, p. 15–21, folios 3–9) against the
archive's 169 rows, so most of what follows has no row. Each line is one figure or site as the
book prints it, with the order, death year and burial place **only where the book gives them**;
`(not stated)` means the book does not say. `[PK]` marks a burial place in present-day
Pakistan — those are the candidate rows. Dates are kept exactly as printed, Hijri forms and
Urdu numerals included, and every `[OCR?]` / `[illegible]` flag stays on the word it was
attached to: a flagged name must be checked before it becomes a row. Where the 1875 author and
the 1974 editor (Muhammad Iqbal Mujaddidi) disagree, both readings are given (CLAUDE.md RULE 2).
"Appendix" = Mian Muhammad Din Kalim's table *مزارات لاہور کا موجودہ محل وقوع* (pp. 287–291),
which gives the 1974 street address for many Lahore graves.

### Lahore

**Qadiri and Gilani**

- [PK] **Mir Sayyid Shah Firoz / میر سید شاہ فیروز** — entry no. 1; died 933 AH; buried Lahore "ٹنڈی گراں کے تکیے میں", the takya of Tandi Garan; Gilani sayyid, chain to Ghaus al-Azam, sat on his grandfather Shah Alam's masnad; editor sources the grave to Khazina 1/118; appendix row 1 now "نزد باغ مہان سنگھ **نار [OCR?]** سینما چرچ روڈ". (pp. 41–42, folios 29–30; p. 287, folio 275)
- [PK] **Sayyid Mahmud Huzuri Lahori / سید محمود حضوری** — entry no. 3; died ۹۷۲ [OCR?] AH; Musawi Ghauri sayyid, father Khwaja Shams al-Din "Shams al-Arifin" of Ghur; settled Muhalla Haji Sawai; appendix row 2 → Allama Iqbal Road opposite Bain Bazar, Garhi Shahu. (pp. 43–44, folios 31–32; p. 287)
- [PK] **Sayyid Kamil Shah Lahori / سید کامل شاہ** — entry no. 16; died "in the year one thousand and five"; Qadiri, then faiz from Shaikh Ilah Dad داری [OCR?]; "پنجاب میں لوگ ان کو دیوان کامل کہتے تھے"; came in Akbar's time from بخارا [OCR?], settled and died at mauza باوساہو [OCR?]; appendix row 4 → موضع بابو صابو / Babu Sabu. (p. 54, folio 42; p. 287)
- [PK] **Shaikh Abu Ishaq Qadri Lahori / شیخ ابو اسحق قادری** — entry no. 11; died "nine hundred eighty-five Hijri"; **editor: 974 AH**, from the colophon of a 975 AH ms of his *Risala Natiqa* (Raza Library Rampur), and "متاخر تذکرہ نویسان لاہور کا مندرجہ سال وفات یعنی ۹۸۵ھ اہمیت نہیں رکھتا"; rauza at mauza Mozang, two miles south of Lahore, "زیارت گاہ خلق"; settled Muhalla Pir Aziz, Mozang منعل [OCR?]; khalifa of Shaikh Daud Kirmani; khanqah known as **Madrasa Ishaqiya**, ancestors from Bukhara; appendix row 3 → Rauza Abu Ishaq Street, Temple Road. (p. 50, folio 38; p. 287; p. 344, folio 332)
- [PK] **Shah Shams al-Din Qadri Lahori / شاہ شمس الدین قادری** — entry no. 18; died "one thousand and twenty-one Hijri", margin ۱۰۲۱ [OCR?]; khilafat from Abu Ishaq Qadri; illuminated rauza in Lahore; Jahangir's devotion recorded; appendix row 6 → "چمپہ ہاؤس **بلین [OCR?]** ریس کورس روڈ". (p. 57, folio 45; p. 288)
- [PK] **Sayyid Shah Bilawal b. Sayyid Usman b. Sayyid Isa Qadri Lahori / سید شاہ بلاول** — entry no. 22; died 28 Shaban 1044 AH, aged seventy; "مزار گوہر بار دروازہ دہلی شہر لاہور کے باہر ہے", outside Delhi Gate; khirqa from Khwaja Shams al-Din Lahori; editor: Shah Jahan and Dara Shikoh attended on him; a jagir and langar recorded; appendix row 10 → graveyard near Haji Camp Colony, Bagh Raja Dina Nath, Sultanpura Road. (pp. 62–63, folios 50–51; p. 287)
- [PK] **Khwaja Bihari Qadri Lahori / خواجہ بہاری قادری** — entry no. 25; died "one thousand and sixty Hijri"; khalifa of Mian Mir, buried adjoining Mian Mir's rauza; editor places his origin at Hajipur, Patna, early study with Shaikh Jalal Auliya at qasba کودہ پور [OCR?], Lahore study with Mulla Fazil. (pp. 65–66, folios 53–54; p. 287)
- [PK] **Sayyid Jan Muhammad Huzuri b. Shah Nur b. Sayyid Mahmud Huzuri / سید جان محمد حضوری** — entry no. 27; died 1065 AH; "لاہور میں متصل موضع گڈھی شاہو میں ان کا مزار ہے", adjoining mauza Garhi Shahu; sat on the masnad of guidance after father and grandfather; appendix row 13 → Allama Iqbal Road opposite Bain Bazar, Garhi Shahu. (p. 67, folio 55; p. 287)
- [PK] **Shah Raza Qadri Shattari Lahori / شاہ رضا قادری شطاری** — entry no. 32; died 12 Jumada al-Awwal 1118 AH; "مزار شہر لاہور کے اندر ہے"; editor, from his own *Irshad al-Ashiqin* (Akbar Nagar, 1068 AH/1657 CE): full name Muhammad Raza b. Shaikh Muhammad Fazil b. Shaikh Asad Allah Lahori Qadri, his father was also his pir, and "شاہ رضا بھی اپنے والد کی طرح لاہور کے قاضی تھے" — **qazi of Lahore**; appendix row 16 → near Tibbi Bazar police station, "**بچ [OCR?]** محمد لطیف سٹریٹ"; facsimile no. ۳۲ reproduces his hand. (pp. 73, 75; folios 61, 63; pp. 284, 288)
- [PK] **Shah Muhammad Ghaus Gilani Qadri Lahori / شاہ محمد غوث گیلانی** — entry no. 37; died 1152 AH, chronogram margin ۱۱۵۲; "مزار لاہور میں ہے"; **editor: "سال وفات ۱۱۵۲ھ غلط ہے"** — Dr Umm-e Salma Gilani proved 1173 AH/1759 CE from the contemporary طہماس نامہ [OCR?]; son of Sayyid Hasan Peshawari; five works of his own listed, one attributed tazkira dismissed as "بغیر کسی تاریخی شعور"; appendix row 18 → outside Delhi Gate near the Lahore Corporation library; row 60's graveyard is named for him; facsimile no. ۳۷. (pp. 77–78, 80; pp. 284, 288, 345)
- [PK] **Shaikh Abdullah Shah Baloch Lahori Qadri / شیخ عبد اللہ شاہ بلوچ** — entry no. 40; died 8 Jumada al-Awwal 1212 AH, chronogram "غریب"; "مزار بیرون شہر موضع مزنگ میں ہے"; murid and khalifa of Shaikh Sharaf al-Din Panipati; "ذات کے بلوچ", amassed wealth by camel-driving and founded **Kot Abdullah** at Mozang before renouncing; modern book: Muhammad Din Kalim, *Abdullah Shah Baloch*, Lahore 1971; **not the archive's Abdullah Shah Ghazi**; appendix row 19 → Kot Abdullah Shah, Mozang. (pp. 82–83, folios 70–71; p. 288)
- [PK] **Hafiz Ghulam Muhammad, known as Imam Gamun / امام گاموں** b. Muhammad Siddiq — entry no. 240; mazar by the **south wall of Masjid Wazir Khan**, inside the city; imam of the mosque "چند پشت سے", a hereditary imamate; bai'at to Abdullah Shah Qadiri Baloch; a poet whose "عاشقانہ ابیات اب تک زبان زد خلق ہیں"; the third appendix's family record gives his death as "۱۲۴۴ھ [OCR?]" and his son **Hafiz Imam Allah Bakhsh** as 11 Ramazan 1278 AH. (pp. 82, 275–276, folios 263–264; p. 337, folio 325)
- [PK] **Shaikh Mir Muhammad Yaqub Lahori / شیخ میر محمد یعقوب** — entry no. 148; died 4 Muharram 1160 AH — **the editor says that date belongs to a different man**; "مزار متصل موضع مزنگ کے ہے"; had built "اپنا قلعہ علیحدہ" at Mohalla Pir Aziz, Mazang and lived there; commanded by the *da'wat* of the divine names; eighteen-step pedigree to Abd al-Qadir Jilani through شاہ قمیص قادری; chain Sayyid Fazl Ali Lahori ← Shaikh عبد الرحیم جا اللہ [OCR?] ← Haji Muhammad Saeed Lahori; three sons Sayyid Muhammad Yusuf, Mir Sayyid Ali, Mir Ismail; appendix row 56 → Warris Road, Miani. (pp. 216–217, folios 204–205; p. 290)
- [PK] **Shaikh Haji Muhammad Saeed Lahori / شیخ حاجی محمد سعید** — entry no. 147; died 1166 AH in the text, **1162 AH in the editor's contemporary source**; "مزار گوہر بار لاہور میں روبرو نیلا گنبد پشت بازار انار کلی ہے" — opposite the Nila Gumbad behind Anarkali bazaar, the most precise Lahore address in the book; Qadiri khilafat from Sayyid Mahmud b. Sayyid Ali Husaini Kurdawi with bai'at at Medina, Shattari chain to Shah Muhammad Ghaus Gwaliari, **Naqshbandi ijazat from Hafiz Sa'd Allah Mujaddidi** — three orders in one man; his madrasa hosted Shah Wali Allah in 1143 AH; malfuzat *Qiran al-Sa'dain* by Akhund Muhammad Rafi Peshawari; the editor's own monograph on him was in press 1974; appendix row 55 → Bank Square. (pp. 212–215, folios 200–203; pp. 290, 293, 295)
- [PK] **Shaikh Nur Ahmad, famous as Nur Husain Qadiri / نور حسین قادری** — entry no. 155; died 1236 AH; khirqa of khilafat from Shaikh Abd al-Karim Bhawan Shah; sukr so overcame him that "برس برس روز تک ایک مقام پر بیٹھا رہ جاتا"; his khalifa Shaikh **Rasul Shah** "اس سال میں فوت ہوئے ہیں" — died in the year of writing. (p. 223, folio 211)
- [PK] **Shaikh Ladhe Shah "موئنہ ساز [OCR?]" Lahori / شیخ لدھے شاہ** — entry no. 161; died 1230 AH; mazar in Lahore; among Lahore's later elders, Qadiri bai'at; a named trade, a fixed charity rate and lending recorded; appendix row 60 → the graveyard of Shah Muhammad Ghaus outside Delhi Gate. (p. 229, folio 217; p. 290)
- [PK] **Miran Sayyid Ghulam Muhyi al-Din / میراں سید غلام محی الدین** — entry no. 159; died 1240 AH at **Kotla**, against the chronogram's 1244 [OCR?]; Qadiri, "اول چند پشت سے ان کا قیام لاہور میں تھا"; twenty-two-step pedigree to Abd al-Qadir Gilani through غوث محمد اوچی; his life is a sequence of rulers' summonses; five sons — Miran Sayyid Ghulam Ghaus (no. 169), Sayyid Ghulam "Shah Sahib" (no. 168), Sayyid Ghulam Rasul, Miran Hafiz Sayyid Muhammad Shah, Miran Sayyid Ghulam Gilani. **Keep distinct from Maulana Ghulam Muhyi al-Din Qasuri (d. 1270 AH).** (pp. 227–229, folios 215–217)
- [PK] **Shah Abdul Razzaq Makki / شاہ عبدالرزاق مکی** — entry no. 225; died "in the year one thousand and eighty-four" (era not stated); murid of Mauj Darya; came from شہر سبز دار [OCR?], took service with Akbar, then renounced; "بازار انارکلی میں ان کا مقبرہ برنگ [OCR?] نیلگوں مشہور ہے", with a grand mosque beside it re-repaired by the late Munshi Muhammad Najm al-Din. **Name overlaps an archive row — check.** (p. 269, folio 257)
- [PK] **Sharaf Shah Lahori / شرف شاہ** — entry no. 219; death year (not stated); died aged one hundred; Qadiri, murid of Muhammad Fazil Qadri (a great scholar whose old mosque still shows its traces); maqbara "پرانے ہزار دول [OCR?] کے درمیان لاٹ صاحب کی کوٹھی کے جنوب". (p. 267, folio 255)
- [PK] **Shah Dargahi Qadri / شاہ درگاہی قادری** — entry no. 220; date (not stated); murid of Shah Chiragh Gilani Lahori; "دن رات اہل حاجت کا ہجوم حضرت کے دروازے پر رہتا"; mazar beside that of **Shah Ismail Muhaddith**; a well, a cure and a Sunday ritual recorded. (pp. 267–268, folios 255–256)
- [PK] **Shah Ismail Muhaddith / شاہ اسمعیل محدث** — date, order (not stated); named only as the neighbouring mazar by which Shah Dargahi's grave is fixed. (p. 267, folio 255)
- [PK] **Pir Zuhdi Lahori / پیر زہدی** — entry no. 226; real name **Wajih al-Din**; died "in the year one thousand one hundred and forty" (era not stated); mazar near mauza Mozang; the book's most multiply-affiliated figure — faiz in the Naqshbandiya Mujaddidiya from Shaikh Sa'di بلخاری [OCR?] Lahori, the Suhrawardi *tariqa* from Shaikh Jan Muhammad Suhrawardi Lahori, the **khirqa** of the Chishtis from Miran Shah Bhikha, the **kulah** of the Qadiris from Shah Muhammad Ghaus Lahori, with Mecca, Medina, Bait al-Maqdis, Baghdad, Karbala and Najaf between. (pp. 269–270, folios 257–258)
- [PK] **Pir Burhan / پیر برہان** — entry no. 228; date (not stated); mazar outside the **Delhi Gate**; originally of Bukhara, came to Hind in Akbar's era; Qadiri faiz from Mian Mir and Shah Bilawal; demolished under Kharak Singh and rebuilt. (pp. 270–271, folios 258–259)
- [PK] **Shaikh Ghulan Shah Sarmast Qadiri / شیخ گھلن شاہ سرمست قادری** — entry no. 246; dates (not stated); mazar "موری دروازے کے باہر سرکاری باغ کے اندر"; murid of Shaikh Tahir Qadiri Lahori; "مستانے طریق پر رہا کرتے تھے". (p. 279, folio 267)
- [PK] **Sayyid Abdul Qadir Lahori / سید عبدالقادر لاہوری** — entry no. 236; death year (not stated); mazar east of mauza Mozang; son of the Gilani sayyid Sayyid Jalal al-Din Baghdadi, came in Akbar's era; Nawab Mir Kifayat Khan gave him his daughter; three sons — Sayyid Haji, Sayyid Sultan, Ghiyath al-Din "Sayyid Daulat Shah" — the line running to **Sayyid Shahsawar**, sajjada nashin of Shaikh Tahir Bandagi's mazar. **The editor rejects this genealogy.** (p. 274, folio 262)
- [PK] **Ayub Sabir Miran / ایوب صابر میراں**, descendant of Sayyid Mubarak Haqqani Gilani — entry no. 224; date (not stated); "باہر لاہور کے ان کا مزار گورستان میانی میں ہے" — the **Miani graveyard**; Qadiri, "اب تک فیض طریقت جاری ہے", with nine links printed from the living **Sayyid Pir Nizam al-Din, "لودیاں والہ"**, back to him. (p. 269, folio 257)
- [PK] **The Takiya Ambli Wala enclosure / احاطہ تکیہ انبلی والا** — entry no. 237; dates (not stated); two chahar-diwaris and three lime-plastered chabutras holding the graves of the descendants of **Sayyid Muhammad Ghaus Uchi Halabi Gilani**: Sayyid Sufi Ali (father Sayyid Badr al-Din b. Sayyid Ismail) with Sayyid Umar, Sayyid Hashim and Sayyid Abdul Qadir "Shah Gada"; Sayyid Qasim b. Sayyid Sufi and his descendants; Sayyid Miran Shah, Mir Miran and Sayyid Abu al-Barakat Shah; Sayyid Ismail; and, on a separate structure, **Pir Muhammad Shah Gilani**, whose son Pir Shah Sardar was alive at writing. (pp. 274–275, folios 262–263)
- [PK] **Mazar Chiragh Shah / مزار چراغ شاہ** — entry no. 238; outside the **Roshnai Gate**; **Sayyid Ali Gilani** came here "ملک دکن سے", from the Deccan, and died "in the year one thousand two hundred and twenty-seven" (era not stated); his janishin **Chiragh Shah** settled here and is buried in the same enclosure; Qadiri. (p. 275, folio 263)
- [PK] **Mazar Marg Naini / مزار مرگ نینی** — entry no. 239; location and date (not stated); real name **Muhammad Sultan**, titled Marg Naini for his very beautiful eyes; eight-link Qadiri shajra printed back to Shah Qamis Qadiri ساوھوی [OCR?]; "اور ان حضرت حال معلوم نہیں ہوا". (p. 275, folio 263)

**Chishti and Sabiri**

- [PK] **Shah Kaku Chishti Lahori / شاہ کاکو چشتی** — entry no. 51; died "eight hundred and eighty Hijri"; descendant of Baba Farid, khirqa from Shaikh Pir Muhammad Chishti Lahori; **mazar in Lahore outside Delhi Gate**; the editor gives him a different pir, a madrasa in present-day **Landa Bazar** next to the railway station, a famous scholar son, and **no certain death year at all**; appendix row 20 → "لنڈا بازار ، **اب یہ مزار نہیں ہے۔**" — this mazar no longer exists. (pp. 99–100, folios 87–88; pp. 288, 305)
- [PK] **Shaikh Jan Allah Chishti Sabri Lahori / شیخ جان اللہ چشتی صابری** — entry no. 54; died "one thousand and twenty-nine"; mazar in Lahore, editor: "شیخ جان اللہ باغ مہاران سنگھ [OCR?] لاہور میں مدفون ہیں"; khalifa of Shaikh Nizam al-Din Balkhi, "لاہور میں … مامور ہوئے", posted to Lahore; three pir-brothers also in Lahore — Sayyid Allah Bakhsh Bhakkari, Shaikh Allah Bakhsh Lahori, Shaikh Dost Muhammad Lahori; appendix row 21 → Nisbat Road. (p. 103, folio 91; p. 288)
- [PK] **Shaikh Haji Abd al-Karim Chishti Lahori / شیخ حاجی عبدالکریم چشتی** — entry no. 55; died 23 Rajab "one thousand and forty-five Hijri"; "مزار گوہر بار لاہور میں متصل باغ زیب النسا بیگم المشہور چوبرجی [OCR?] کے پہلو [OCR?]" — adjoining the garden of Zeb al-Nisa Begum, famous as **Chauburji**; father عماد الملک [OCR?] عبداللہ انصاری, a great amir; went into exile to the Haramain with his pir Nizam al-Din Balkhi when the king expelled him; four works survive in manuscript in Pakistani libraries; appendix row 22 → Nawan Kot, Qadir Park. (pp. 103–104, folios 91–92; pp. 288, 345)
- [PK] **Shaikh Abd al-Khaliq Lahori Chishti Sabri / شیخ عبدالخالق لاہوری** — entry no. 58; died "one thousand and sixty-eight Hijri"; mazar in Lahore; khalifa of Shaikh Jan Allah; a langar recorded; appendix row 23 → "عقب برکت علی محمڈن ہال نزد مندر بالمیکاں" — behind Barkat Ali Muhammadan Hall, near the Balmikan (Valmiki) temple. (p. 106, folio 94; p. 288)
- [PK] **Shaikh Muhammad Arif Chishti Sabri Lahori / شیخ محمد عارف** — entry no. 59; died 7 Zi al-Hijja "one thousand and seventy-one"; **mazar in Lahore**, no further location given; janishin of Abd al-Khaliq; a healing during sama recorded. (p. 107, folio 95)
- [PK] **Shaikh Muhammad Siddiq Chishti Sabri Lahori / شیخ محمد صدیق** — entry no. 60; died 1084 AH; mazar in Lahore — **the editor refuses to settle either the year or the grave**; "پنجاب کے ہزاروں لوگ اس کے ذریعہ سے فیض یاب دنیا وآخرت ہوئے"; appendix row 24 dittoes row 23's address. (p. 107, folio 95; p. 288)
- [PK] **Shaikh Muhammad Salim Chishti Sabri Lahori / شیخ محمد سلیم** — entry no. 67; died 3 Zi al-Hijja "one thousand one hundred and fifty-one Hijri"; mazar in Lahore; khirqa from Muhammad Siddiq; "ان کی مجلس کبھی سماع سے خالی نہ ہوتی"; the ulama's capital petition against him and its outcome is the book's fullest state-and-shrine episode; appendix row 25. (p. 114, folio 102; p. 288)
- [PK] **Shaikh Khair al-Din, known as Khair Shah Chishti Lahori / خیر شاہ** — entry no. 76; died 19 Zi al-Hijja "in the year 1228" — **three rival death years in play**; mazar in Lahore; khalifa of Shaikh Salim; "لنگر بھی ان کا فقرا اور درویشوں کے واسطے جاری رہتا"; appendix row 26. (p. 122, folio 110; p. 288)
- [PK] **Shaikh Haji Ramzan Lahori Chishti / شیخ حاجی رمضان** — entry no. 80; born Ramadan 1202, died 3 Ramadan 1282 AH, "عمر شریف اسی برس کی تھی" — eighty years, which the two dates support exactly; khalifa of Khwaja Sulaiman [Taunsvi]; resident in Lahore; went to the Haramain; appendix row 27 places him with the archive's Tahir Bandagi. (p. 125, folio 113; p. 288)
- [PK] **Shaikh Faiz Bakhsh Lahori Sabri Chishti / شیخ فیض بخش** — entry no. 81; died of "تپ محرق", a burning fever, 9 Rajab 1286 AH (۱۲۸۶ھ [OCR?]); khalifa of Shaikh Haidar Shah, who took inward faiz from Khair Shah; "گذارہ ان کا ریشم سازی کے کام پر تھا" — lived by **silk-making**, in Mahan Singh's garden; seventeen annual urs, a locust miracle, death in wajd; the author's own maternal great-grandfather's line (father of Shaikh Imam Bakhsh, "جد مادری مؤلف کتاب"); appendix row 28 blank in both columns. (pp. 82, 125–126, folios 113–114; p. 288)
- [PK] **Mian Amir Bakhsh the bookbinder / میاں امیر بخش جلد ساز** — died "سنہ بارہ سو اکیانوے", 1291; "لاہور کے مشائخ میں سے … ایک مرد صاحب ذوق و عبادت"; a named trade for a named Lahore shaykh. (p. 124, folio 112)
- [PK] **Pir Muhammad Shirazi Chishti / پیر محمد شیرازی چشتی** — entry no. 217; died "in the year one thousand one hundred" (era not stated); maqbara near mauza Mozang; a sayyid of the Khushab country with **Shahpur** his native place; "موضع مزنگ میں جو بلوچ کی قوم رہتی ہے، سب کی بیعت حضرت کی خدمت میں ہے" — the whole Baloch qaum of Mozang holds bai'at to him. (pp. 266–267, folios 254–255)

**Naqshbandi and Mujaddidi**

- [PK] **Khwaja Khawand Mahmud Lahori, famous as Hazrat Ishan Naqshbandi / حضرت ایشاں** — entry no. 84; born at Bukhara [OCR?], editor's year **971 AH**; died 12 Shaban 1052 AH; **mazar in Lahore** (no quarter given); descended from Khwaja Ala al-Din Attar through his father Mir Sayyid Sharif; outward bai'at from Khwaja Abu Ishaq Safid [OCR?] — editor: Khwaja Muhammad Ishaq Dahbidi — but an **Uwaisi** nisbat with Baha al-Din Naqshband; Kabul, Kashmir, Delhi, Akbarabad, then Lahore, where he stayed "شاہجہان بادشاہ کی التجا سے", at Shah Jahan's request — **which the editor rewrites as an order of banishment**; his son Khwaja Muin al-Din wrote *Kitab-e Rizwani* on him (copy at the J&K Cultural Academy, Srinagar); a demolition order was issued against his rauza; appendix row 29 → Begampura near the Yatim Khana Dar al-Furqan. (pp. 132–134, folios 120–122; p. 288)
- [PK] **Shaikh Sa'di بلخاری [OCR?] Mujaddidi Lahori / شیخ سعدی** — entry no. 89; died 3 Rabi al-Thani, a Wednesday, "in the year 1108" (the editor elsewhere records d. 1100 AH [OCR?]); "مزار بیرون لاہور موضع مزنگ کے پاس ہے"; seized at eight, brought through Maulana Haji Sa'd Allah Wazirabadi to Shaikh Adam Banuri, went with him to the Haramain, returned to Lahore when Adam died at Medina; the editor supplies his real name and doubts the nisba; appendix row 30 → "**ترنڈی [OCR?]** سٹریٹ سعدی پارک لٹن روڈ". (pp. 139–140, folios 127–128; pp. 288, 338)
- [PK] **Shaikh Mahmud Shah Naqshbandi Mujaddidi Lahori / شیخ محمود شاہ** — entry no. 100; died "in the year one thousand two hundred and eighty Hijri", 1280 AH, and buried **adjoining the maqbara of Jhulan Shah**, his illuminated rauza a place of pilgrimage; faiz in the Tariq-e Ahmadiya Mujaddidiya from Shaikh Abd al-Karim Mujaddidi, khirqa-holder from Shah Ghulam Ali; lived "مجردانہ", in celibacy; appendix row 31 → Ghore Shah Road, Sultanpura. (pp. 157–158, folios 145–146; p. 288)
- [PK] **Sayyid Munawwar Ali Shah Naqshbandi Suhrawardi Lahori / سید منوّر علی شاہ** — entry no. 163; died 1264 AH; tomb **inside Shaikh Tahir Lahori's chahar-diwari**; his bai'at chain runs to his father Mir Abd al-Razzaq ← Mir Abd al-Rahim ← Mir Sadr al-Din ← Mir Haidar ← **Baba Nasib al-Din Ghazi** ← **Baba Daud Khaki** ← **Shaikh Hamza Kashmiri** ← **Shaikh Jamal al-Din Bukhari** — the Kashmir Suhrawardi succession carried into nineteenth-century Lahore, though "رغبت حضرت کی سلسلہ نقشبندیہ کی طرف زیادہ تھی" and he had his murids practise in the Naqshbandi manner; two of his murids, **Sayyid Hasan Shah Bukhari and his son Sayyid Ahmad Shah**, were "لاہور میں موجود ہیں" as the author wrote; appendix row 61. (pp. 230–231, folios 218–219; p. 290)

**Suhrawardi, Qureshi and the Mufti family of Kotli Muftian**

- [PK] **Shaikh Abd al-Jalil, Qutb al-Alam Chuhar Bandagi Qureshi Harithi Hakari Lahori / چوہڑ بندگی** — entry no. 110; arrival dated by the editor to "۸۸۰ھ / ۱۴۷۵ء کے حدود میں", around 880 AH/1475 CE; buried **in his own khanqah**, with the emperor present at his ghusl; four intermediaries to Shaikh Hamid al-Din Abu al-Hakim, "بادشاہ کیچ مقران"; "پنجاب کے رہنے والوں نے خدمت حضرت کو اپنا فخر سمجھا"; his brother **Shaikh Jamal al-Din Abu Bakr b. Abu al-Fath** wrote a large life of him between 947–952 AH/1540–1545 CE, named by the editor *Tazkira-e Qutbiya*, "لاہور کی ثقافتی و مذہبی تاریخ کا ایک قابل قدر ماخذ", published Lahore 1952; appendix row 32 → McLeod Road opposite the Qila Gujjar Singh abadi. (pp. 172–173, folios 160–161; p. 288)
- [PK] **Sayyid Usman, famous as Shah Jhula Bukhari Lahori / شاہ جھولہ بخاری** — entry no. 111; died 8 Rabi al-Awwal 912 AH; came from Uch to Lahore; nine-step pedigree to Makhdoom Jahaniyan; **the laqab from a disease** — a tremor in the left arm, "جھولا پنجابی زبان میں رعشہ کو کہتے ہیں"; appendix row 33 → "تہ خانہ قلعہ اکبری لاہور" then **the Shahi Qila, Lahore**. (pp. 173–174, folios 161–162; p. 288)
- [PK] **Shaikh Musa Ahangar Lahori / شیخ موسیٰ آہنگر** (the blacksmith) — entry no. 113; died "سنہ نو سو پچیس ۹۲۵ھ" — **which the editor rejects outright**; editor's birth: in the environs of Multan, 27 Rajab 818 AH [OCR?] (*Manaqib-e Musawi* 15); mazar in Lahore "متصل قلعہ گوجر سنگھ المشہور بہ گنبد سبز" — adjoining Qila Gujjar Singh, famous as the **Gumbad Sabz**; first a murid of Shaikh Shahr Allah Multani, completed under Shaikh Chuhar; chief khalifas Mir Hashim Bukhari, Haji Ishaq Sindhi, Shaikh مہری [OCR?], Makhdoom Ilm al-Din b. Abd al-Salam Khokhar, Shah Nur, Shah Rizq Allah Niazi; appendix row 34. (pp. 174–175, folios 162–163; p. 289)
- [PK] **The small rauza of the newly-Muslim woman / روضہ خرد اس عورت نو مسلمہ کا** — undated; stands beside Shaikh Musa Ahangar's, from the spindle miracle. (p. 175, folio 163)
- [PK] **Sayyid Jhulan Shah, famous as Ghore Shah Lahori / گھوڑے شاہ** — entry no. 118; real name **Baha al-Din**; died 11 Rabi al-Awwal 1003 AH; "مزار لاہور کے باہر", outside Lahore, "در زمین حاجی نالہ"; grandson of Shah Jhula Bukhari, "مجذوب [OCR?] مادر زاد ولی"; the votive **clay horses** and the granting of wishes; the editor corrects his name and silsila; appendix row 35 (as سید قبولن شاہ المشہور گھوڑے شاہ) → Abadi Ghore Shah, Ghore Shah Road. (p. 179, folio 177 as printed; p. 289)
- [PK] **Sayyid Shahbaz b. Imadi al-Mulk** — died 7 Rajab 1041 AH — and **Sayyid کموی [OCR?] Shah b. Arif Shah b. Imadi al-Mulk** — died 22 Rajab 1050 — both buried "بہ پہلوئے مزار جھولن شاہ", at the side of Jhulan Shah's mazar; **Sayyid Imadi al-Mulk**, Jhulan Shah's real brother, resident in Lahore; the dates come from a genealogical register held by his descendants Sayyid Hakim Shah and Muhammad Shah. (p. 179, folio 177 as printed)
- [PK] **Shaikh Hasan Kunjadgar, famous as Hassu Teli Lahori / حسو تیلی** — entry no. 119; died "سنہ ایک ہزار بارہ", 1012 — **editor: 3 Shawwal 1011 AH at Lahore, aged 120**; a **grain seller** told by Shah Jamal Lahori "کم تولنا چھوڑ دو", who grew rich and then gave it all away; editor (from Surat Singh's 1054 AH tazkira): of **Makiwal on the bank of the Chenab**, father Shaikh چندو [OCR?], mother میلی [OCR?]; Abd al-Rahim Khan-e Khanan asked his prayer for the conquest of Thatta; in famine Akbar asked him to pray for rain and the prayer proved effective; appendix row 36 → Abbott Road near the زچہ بچہ hospital. (pp. 180–181, folios 178–179 as printed; pp. 289, 305)
- [PK] **Shaikh Jan Muhammad Suhrawardi Lahori / شیخ جان محمد سہروردی** — entry no. 125; died "سال ایک ہزار بیاسی ہجری", 1082 AH; "مزار باہر شہر لاہور کے متصل **مسجد قصاب خانے** کے ہے" — outside the city adjoining the butchery-quarter mosque; among the khalifas of **Shaikh Ismail, famous as Bara Mian Lahori**; likely the "میاں جان محمد قصاب پوری" the editor names among the Parvezabad teachers; a manuscript of his survives in the editor's own library; appendix row 40 → "**گاؤنگ [OCR?]** گراؤنڈ گڑھی شاہو نزد مسجد مولوی تاج دین". (pp. 186–187, folios 184–185 as printed; p. 289)
- [PK] **Shaikh Muhammad Ismail Lahori, famous as Mian Kalan — also Mian Wada / میاں کلاں، میاں وڈا** — entry no. 126; born **995**, died **1085 AH**; "مزار گوہر بار لاہور میں زیارت گاہ خلق ہے"; father Fath Allah b. Abdullah b. Sarfaraz, "قوم کے زمیندار **کھوکھر**" — one of very few statements of a saint's clan; kept the teaching of the Quran running his whole life; thirteen-step chain to Baha al-Din Zakariya Multani; students Mian Taimur Lahori and Mian Hamid Qari Lahori, "پورے پنجاب کے علما نے ان سے … فیض حاصل کیا"; editor, per Sayyid Sharafat Naushahi: mother **Mai فربد [OCR?]** buried at qasba **جوگا کلیان [OCR?], district Gujrat**, father at **mauza جیبہ چیمہ [OCR?] adjoining Ahmad Nagar, district Gujrat**; a 1909 Lahore biography *Sawanih Mian Wada Lahori*; appendix row 41 → inside the **Khanqah Mian Wada**. **Not to be merged with the author's ancestor Mian Kalan (no. 1 above).** (pp. 187–188, folios 185–186 as printed; pp. 289, 303)
- [PK] **Shaikh Jan Muhammad Thani Lahori Suhrawardi — Jan Muhammad Parvezabadi / جان محمد ثانی** — entry no. 127; died **1100** and buried in **Mohalla Parvezabad**, then **reburied three years later**; khalifa of Shaikh Ismail Mudarris Lahori, first murid of Shaikh Abd al-Hamid; "تمام پنجاب کے علما اس کے قول پر صاد کرتے تھے"; had a special hujra built at Parvezabad for his residence and teaching; appendix row 42 → the same address as Mian Wada. (p. 189, folio 187 as printed; p. 289)
- [PK] **Shaikh Karam Shah Qureshi Harithi ہنکاری [OCR?]** — entry no. 128; **martyred by bandits near Shahjahanpur** "سال ایک ہزار دو سو میں", the year 1200, with "۱۲۰۱ھ اس کا سال وفات ہے" in the same sentence — two years, see Doubts; Suhrawardi, seven steps to Abd al-Jalil Chuhar Lahori; first lived in Lahore, then went to **Lucknow** when "غارت گر ملا [OCR?] قوم سکھ" set the plunder of the Punjab going — the only displacement of a Lahore shrine family by the Sikh period in the book; three sons Sikandar Shah, Shah Murad and **Qalandar Shah** (d. **1228**), whose son **Shaikh Ghulam Muhyi al-Din Qureshi** died **1279**. (p. 190, folio 188 as printed)
- [PK] **Shaikh Muhammad Ismail Muhaddith wa Mufassir Lahori / شیخ محمد اسماعیل محدث** — entry no. 129; died **448 AH**, chronogram "متاب"; "مزار شہر لاہور کے جنوب کی طرف ہے"; of the great **Bukhari** sayyids, settled in Lahore in the time of Sultan Masud Ghaznavi; *Tuhfat al-Wasilin* makes him **the first of the callers to Islam to come to Lahore**, through whom "دین اسلام … پنجاب میں شائع ہوا" — a claim of Islamic priority against Data Ganj Bakhsh, who arrives seventeen years later on the book's own figures; **the editor demolishes all of this**; appendix row 43 → Hall Road. (p. 191, folio 189 as printed; p. 289)
- [PK] **Sayyid Ahmad Tokhta Tirmizi Lahori / سید احمد توختہ ترمذی** — entry no. 134; date (not stated) in this material; epithet from a Turkish word meaning "ایستادہ کھڑا", standing upright, given by his shaikh after he stood all night outside a shut door; his maternal grandson's line reaches Shaikh Hamid al-Din Abu al-Hakim, and the book promises his account "چھٹے باب میں"; appendix row 47 → **Muhalla Chihal Bibiyan inside Mochi Gate**. (pp. 167, 199–200; p. 289)
- [PK] **Sayyid Yaqub, addressed as Sadr-e Diwan Zanjani Lahori / صدر دیوان زنجانی** — entry no. 135; died **16 Rajab 604 AH**; mazar inside Lahore city, no quarter; came from **Zanjan**; father حامد [OCR?] Ali Musavi Husaini Zanjani, at whose hand his bai'at was taken; "ان کا توسل **سلسلہ سہلویہ [OCR?]** کے ساتھ تھا"; Tughril, subah of Lahore, was his murid; **he is Husain Zanjani's brother** per the quoted *Waqai-e Sialkot*; **the editor calls his whole Lahore chronology doubtful**; appendix row 48 → Hospital Road. (pp. 200–201, folios 188–189 as printed; p. 289)
- [PK] **Sayyid Shaikh Aziz al-Din Makki Lahori / شیخ عزیز الدین مکی** — entry no. 136; died **612 AH**; mazar in Lahore; of **Baghdad**, then twelve years' worship at the Bait Allah, entering Lahore in **574 AH**, the year Shihab al-Din Ghauri besieged it; prophesied to Khusrau Malik b. Khusrau Shah Ghaznavi inside the besieged city that he had "چھ برس … اور امان" before the Ghurids took the country; **a different death year appears for him in the author's other book — see Doubts.** (p. 201, folio 189 as printed)
- [PK] **Sayyid Mitha Lahori / سید مٹھہ** — entry no. 137 (contents: printed p. 189); died **661 AH**; mazar inside the city of Lahore; father **Sayyid Jamal al-Din** of **Khwarazm**, who took the road to Hindustan when Chingiz Khan tore up the Khwarazmian sultanate "جُڑ سے"; "بعد باپ کے مرنے یہ جانشین ہوئے" — father-to-son succession stated as such; the name from Punjabi, "مٹھا شیریں کو کہتے ہیں"; nineteen-step pedigree "باقوال صحیح" to Imam Husain; "لاہور کے بزرگوں میں سے یہ بزرگ آج تک مرجع خاص و عام ہیں"; appendix row 50 → **Sayyid Mittha Bazar**. (pp. 18, 189, 201–202; p. 290)
- [PK] **Shaikh Sayyid Abu Ishaq Gazruni, famous as Miran Badshah Lahori / میراں بادشاہ** — entry no. 139; died **786 AH**, chronogram "بسم اللہ الرحمن الرحیم" = 786; originally of **Gazrun**, murid of Shaikh Auhad al-Din Isfahani; buried inside Lahore, and "جب علم الدین حکیم المشہور نواب وزیر خان نے جامع مسجد اس جگہ تعمیر کی تو ان کے مزار کو مسجد کے صحن کے اندر ایک تہ خانہ میں رکھا" — his mazar was placed in a **basement inside the courtyard of Wazir Khan's Jami Masjid**; the persons index equates "میراں بادشاہ لاہوری" with him; a second death year appears in the author's other book; appendix row 51 (as "میلے بادشاہ") → inside the Wazir Khan mosque, in the tahkhana. (p. 203, folio 191 as printed; pp. 290, 331, 337, 340)
- [PK] **Sayyid Abu Turab, known as Shah Gada Husaini Shattari Lahori / شاہ گدا** — entry no. 144; died **14 Shawwal 1071 AH** — **no grave given in the text**; first lived at **Shiraz**, completed at **Gujarat** under Shaikh Wajih al-Din Gujarati, then settled in Lahore; thirty-seven-step pedigree to Imam Musa Kazim; six perfect khalifas — Qazi Muhammad Lahori, Shaikh Fazil, **Shah Jamal**, Lal Gada, Ahmad Gada, Shahbaz Gada, three of them sharing "Gada" as a lineage marker; appendix row 52 → Railway Colony near Garhi Shahu, near the Burt Institute. (pp. 209–210, folios 197–198; p. 290)
- [PK] **Khwaja Ayub Qureshi Lahori / خواجہ ایوب قریشی** — entry no. 145; died **Thursday 21 Jumada al-Thani 1155 AH**; mazar in Lahore, no quarter; author of *Masnavi Makhzan-e Ishq* and the ***Sharh-e Ayubi*** on Rumi's *Masnavi*, permission for which he took from Rumi himself, finding the licensing verses at the head of the opened page; Rumi told a student in a dream "خواجہ ایوب کو **اویسی ہماری روحانیت سے** پہنچا ہے"; the editor has seen manuscripts at the Panjab University Library and with Maulana Sayyid Muhammad Tayyib Hamdani of Qasur, gives the completion year as **1120 AH** against a rival **1111 AH**, and shows Mulla Abd al-Khaliq's 1155 AH *Sharh-e Masnavi* drawing heavily on it; **Mufti Ghulam Sarwar's own relation by marriage** — described as Mufti Muhammad Taqi's pupil and son-in-law, not his son. (pp. 24, 26, 210–212, folios 198–200)
- [PK] **Shaikh Fath Shah Shattari Lahori / شیخ فتح شاہ شطاری** — entry no. 146; died **1150 AH**; mazar in Lahore; khalifa of **Shah Latif Burhanpuri**, presented to him at seven years old and titled "**فتح شاہ سرمست**"; "بعد تکمیل لاہور کو مامور ہوا", posted to Lahore; a Ravi flood recorded; appendix row 54 → "**اب نشان نہیں ہے۔**" — **no trace remains now**. (p. 212, folio 200; p. 290)
- [PK] **Shaikh Muhammad Ashraf Lahori / شیخ محمد اشرف** — son of Shaikh Yunus; death year cut off in the transcription; "آپ لاہور کے نامور علماء میں سے تھے"; chain through Shaikh Farid Thani and Shaikh Bayazid Thani to Shah Muhammad Ghaus Gwaliari; a madrasa grant recorded; *Jami al-Fawaid*, which carries his own life as an introduction, and malfuzat compiled by Sulaiman b. Shaikh Sa'd Allah, both manuscripts owned by the editor. (p. 213, folio 201; pp. 293, 295)
- [PK] **Shaikh Hamid Qari Suhrawardi / شیخ حامد قاری** — entry no. 245; born "**سنہ ایک ہزار اکتر [OCR?]**", 1071 [OCR?], died **14 Jumada al-Thani 1066** — **a death before a birth, see Doubts**; "لاہور کے باہر مزار ہے", and where his grave now is, outside the city, "وہاں شہر آباد تھا" and there he taught; in Muhammad Shah's reign; a murid compiled his malfuzat and he himself wrote **a treatise on the unlawfulness of huqqa and tobacco**; editor: among the greatest ulama of the twelfth Hijri century, with four manuscripts listed. (pp. 278–279, folios 266–267)
- [PK] **Maulvi Timur Lahori / مولوی تیمور لاہوری** — date (not stated); Shaikh Hamid Qari's pir; "لاہور کے فقہا و مدرسین میں سے"; **signed the ulama's fatwa against Mujaddid Alf Thani** in Aurangzeb's reign. (p. 278, folio 266)
- [PK] **Mufti Shaikh Muhammad Mukarram Qureshi / مفتی شیخ محمد مکرم قریشی** — entry no. 243; dates (not stated); held **both the offices of ifta and qaza** in Lahore at the end of Chaghtai rule, and again under Ahmad Shah Durrani, whose special farman "محررہ ماہ رمضان سنہ ۱۱۲۰ھ [OCR?]" was **in the author's own possession** — **editor: "یہ سنہ غلط ہے"**, Abdali's Punjab campaigns began 1161 AH/1748 CE and he died 1187 AH/1773 CE; "ہم جدی" with the author; a decline of his descendants recorded. (pp. 276–277, folios 264–265)
- [PK] **Mufti Rahim Allah b. Mufti Rahmat Allah Qureshi / مفتی رحیم اللہ** — entry no. 154; died **1235 AH**; "مدفن لاہور میں موجود ہے"; **the author's own grandfather**; "طریق ان کا موروثی سہروردیہ تھا" — the family's **hereditary Suhrawardi** path, in which he gave students talqin; the Sikh-period destruction is recorded in his entry; editor: his father Hakim Mufti Hafiz Rahmat Allah b. Mufti Muhammad نقی [OCR?] b. Mufti Muhammad تقی [OCR?] died **1190 AH [OCR?]**; appendix row 58 → present location blank. (pp. 25–26, 222–223, folios 210–211; p. 290)
- [PK] **Mufti Rahmat Allah / مفتی رحمت اللہ** — died **1190 AH**; son, khalifa and successor of Hafiz Mufti Muhammad Taqi; expert in tafsir, hadith, fiqh, logic and medicine; **teacher at the ancestral mosque of Kotli Muftian**. (p. 26, folio 14)
- [PK] **Mufti Muhammad Taqi / مفتی محمد تقی** — died **1131 AH**; eldest son of Mufti Kamal al-Din Khurd; counted among the eminent ulama of Lahore; a second **Mufti Muhammad Taqi b. Mufti Muhammad Taqi, d. 1196 AH**, taught Quran, hadith and fiqh in the family's ancestral **Masjid Muftiyan**. (pp. 26, 211)
- [PK] **Hafiz Muhammad Taqi / حافظ محمد تقی** — died **۱۱۴۶ھ [OCR?]**. (p. 26, folio 14)
- [PK] **Mufti Ghulam Muhammad / مفتی غلام محمد** — the author's father; **two death years in the book**: "9 Rabi al-Awwal 1276 AH / 1859 CE" at p. 25, folio 13, and **1274 AH** in entry no. 166 at pp. 232–233 — give both; learned in the current sciences, "طبیب حاذق", a skilled physician who spent his whole day teaching and treating the sick; educated by his own father and by Maulana Ghulam Rasul Lahori; six sons and three daughters, the survivors Mufti Sayyid Muhammad (d. 1280 AH), Hafiz Ghulam Ahmad (d. "ایک ہزار نوے [OCR?]") and Ghulam Sarwar the author. (pp. 25, 232–233)
- [PK] **Makhdum Mufti Shaikh Muhammad Qureshi, "Mian Kalan" / میاں کلاں** — died **891 AH**; brought from **Multan to Lahore by Sultan Bahlul Lodhi**, appointed to the office of *ifta*, granted the area of **Haibatpur** as *madad-e maash*, and founded a muhalla. (p. 27, folio 15)
- [PK] **Shaikh al-Islam Mufti Abdul Salam Lahori / مفتی عبدالسلام** — appointed successor by his father Shaikh Mufti Inayat Allah in his lifetime in **1014 AH / 1604 CE**; died **1031 AH / 1621 CE**; the footnote distinguishes a contemporary namesake, **Mufti Abdul Salam b. Abdul Aziz Lahori**, author of *Nafi al-Muslimin*, **d. 1037 AH / 1628 CE**, citing Azhar Zahur Ahmad in *al-Maarif*, May 1970. (pp. 26–27, folios 14–15; p. 305)
- [PK] **Shaikh Ayaz Lahori / شیخ ایاز لاہوری** — appendix row 44; address per the book "اندر شہر لاہور", present location **Chowk Rang Mahal**. **Name overlaps the archive's Malik Ahmad Ayaz — check before drafting.** (p. 289)

**Lahore's late majzubs and ecstatics**

- [PK] **Mian Mongar Majzub Lahori / میاں مونگر مجذوب** — entry no. 177; died "**سنہ ۹۷۵ھ [OCR?]**"; grave (not stated); lived in Lahore; ordered Hasan Lodla out of the city in the Bazar سنخاس [OCR?] and he went at once; concordance row ۶۶ has both address columns **blank**. (pp. 238–239, folios 226–227; p. 291)
- [PK] **Masum Shah Majzub Lahori / معصوم شاہ مجذوب** — entry no. 185; died "**سنہ ایک ہزار دو سو اکیس**", 1221; "مزار لوہاری دروازے کے باہر ہے", outside the **Lohari Gate**; lived at the doorway of an old haveli in **محلہ سید مٹھہ [OCR?]** with "آگ ہمیشہ اس کے روبرو روشن"; the eyewitness is **Shaikh Wahab al-Din, "راقم الحروف کا ایک دوست"**; concordance row ۶۷: outside Delhi Gate in 1875 → **Anarkali, Makki Masjid [OCR?]** in 1974. (pp. 243–244, folios 231–232; p. 291)
- [PK] **Mustaqim Shah Lahori Faizpuri Majzub / مستقیم شاہ فیض پوری** — entry no. 186; died "**ایک ہزار دو سو چالیس**", 1240; "قبر موضع فیض پور میں ہے"; "قوم کا حجام", a **barber**, turned by a passing faqir's glance while shaving a zamindar at his well, after which he renounced and settled at mauza Faizpur; concordance row ۶۸: **Faizpur → Faizpur, unchanged**. (p. 244, folio 232; p. 291)
- [PK] **Faqir Taj Shah Majzub Lahori / فقیر تاجے شاہ مجذوب** — entry no. 187; died "**سال ایک ہزار دو سو اکسٹھ ہجری**", 1261 AH; "قبر لاہور میں باہر موچی دروازے کے ہے", outside **Mochi Gate**; "لاہور کے لوگ اکثر اس کے معتقد تھے"; his prophecies, Ranjit Singh's deathbed, and a promised son named "لوڈا" who "اب تیس برس کی عمر کو پہنچ چکا ہے"; concordance row ۶۹ → Gawalmandi, Chamberlain Road, near the old vegetable market. (pp. 244–245, folios 232–233; p. 291)
- [PK] **Bahle [OCR?] Shah Majzub Lahori / بہلے [OCR?] شاہ مجذوب** — entry no. 190; died "**سنہ ایک ہزار دو سو پینسٹھ**", 1265; grave (not stated); a very heavy turban, much talk that "اکثر سمجھا نہیں جاتا تھا", no fixed lodging in the streets and bazaars of Lahore. **The name itself is flagged.** (pp. 246–247, folios 234–235)
- [PK] **Mai Bhagi Lahori / مائی بھاگی** — entry no. 201; died "**سال ایک ہزار دو سو چونسٹھ**", 1264; grave (not stated); a woman, "مجذوبہ و مستانہ", formerly a **wine-seller** who "اس پیشے سے بہت سا روپیہ جمع کیا تھا", converted by a cup of wine from "ایک مرد خدا ذوالفقار نام", who then tore her clothes, threw off her jewellery, gave away her household and took up a torn quilt in a maidan by the **Akbari Gate**; building works recorded; concordance row ۷۶ → "**اب نشان مٹ چکا ہے (سلطان پورہ)**" — the trace has now been erased. (p. 255, folio 243; p. 291)
- [PK] **Nizam Shah Lahori Majzub / نظام شاہ مجذوب** — entry no. 188; died "**سال ایک ہزار دو سو انہتر**", 1269; "قبر گورستان میانی میں ہے", the **Miani graveyard**; refused a summons to the district court — "ہم کو فرنگی کی کچہری میں کون لے جا سکتا ہے" — spent the night in sama with qawwals, dismissed them at dawn and died; "ہزاروں لوگ جنازہ پر حاضر ہوئے"; concordance row ۷۰ → **Miani, تکیہ نظام شاہ**. (pp. 245–246, folios 233–234; p. 291)
- [PK] **Mastan Shah Lahori Majzub / مستان شاہ مجذوب** — entry no. 189; died "**سال ایک ہزار دو سو ستتر ہجری**", 1277 AH; "قبر لاہور میں ہے" with **no locality**; went naked in heat and cold, ignored offerings, ate tree leaves when hunger overcame him, and would fall to working the potters' and weavers' looms "نہایت خوبی کے ساتھ" when he passed their workshops; concordance row ۷۱ → **Miani, تکیہ مستان شاہ**. (p. 246, folio 234; p. 291)
- [PK] **Jali Shah Majzub Lahori / جلی شاہ مجذوب** — concordance row ۷۲, **both address columns blank**; no dates. (p. 291, folio 279)
- [PK] **Sain Qutb Shah Lahori / سائیں قطب شاہ** — entry no. 170; died **1290 AH** — the latest death year in the book — buried at **mauza Khoi Miran**; murid of Shaikh Ghulam Husain; "حصول قوت حلال کے واسطے جولاہوں کا کام کرتا تھا", did **weavers' work** for lawful sustenance; healed two of the author's sons; appendix row 65 → **کوٹھ خواجہ سعید نزد محراب قدیم**. (p. 235, folio 223; p. 290)
- [PK] **Shah کنٹھہ [OCR?] Naushahi / شاہ کنٹھہ نوشاہی** — entry no. 232; died **Rabi al-Awwal ۱۲۱۹ھ** — **the editor rejects the year**; "موچی دروازے کے باہر حضرت کا مقبرہ ہے", a chahar-diwari and dome; "اس خاندان کے مرید بھی اکثر لوگ شہر میں ہیں". (pp. 272–273, folios 260–261)
- [PK] **Fazl Shah Majzub Naushahi / فضل شاہ مجذوب نوشاہی** — **absolute year not stated**: "پچیس برس کا عرصہ ہوا ہے کہ یہ بزرگ مر گیا اور یہاں دفن ہوا" — twenty-five years before writing; entry no. 231; rose high "آخری سلطنت سکھوں" in the last Sikh reign; wandered intoxicated "لوگوں کو گالیاں دیتا"; Raja Dina Nath's patronage and a nazr recorded. (p. 272, folio 260)
- [PK] **Pir Dhal / Dhahl Majzub / پیر ڈہل، ڈھل مجذوب** — entry no. 241; death year (not stated); mazar inside the city of Lahore, and "وہ تمام محلہ اس بزرگ کے نام سے ڈھل محلہ کہلاتا ہے" — the whole mohalla is named after him; of the **Akbari era**; a Thursday Fatiha recorded. The heading spells ڈہل and the sentence ڈھل. (p. 276, folio 264)
- [PK] **Pir Bhola Lahori / پیر بھولا** — entry no. 210; date (not stated); "مزار … محلہ چہیلہ [OCR?] کے حمام کے اندر پختہ بنا ہوا ہے" — inside the **hammam of Mohalla Chahila**; "سوائے خرد سال لڑکوں کے کسی سے اس کو محبت نہ تھی" and he distributed thousands of rupees of offerings to small boys; a children's custom continues at the grave. (pp. 263–264, folios 251–252)

**Lahore's undated shrines, martyrs and unattributed sites**

- [PK] **Sayyid Suf Lahori / سید صوف لاہوری** — entry no. ۲۰۵; date (not stated); "مزار خاص لاہور میں میدان چوک روبروئے دروازہ مسجد وزیر خان ہے" — in the square facing the gate of the **Wazir Khan mosque**; a dome and a chilla recorded; *Risala Tuhfat al-Wasilin* makes him a contemporary of **Miran Badshah** and people say his real brother — "شاید ایسا ہی ہو مگر ثبوت جیسے کہ چاہیے نہیں ہے". (pp. 261–262, folios 249–250)
- [PK] **Sayyid Sar Buland / سید سر بلند** — entry no. ۲۰۶; "اصل زمانہ ان کا پایا نہیں جاتا"; mazar inside the city **north of the Wazir Khan mosque, inside the Tawila Kalan**, a pukka grave within a chahar-diwari, with an **annual urs**; a sayyid of the "متقدمین بزرگوں"; also said to be Miran Badshah's brother. (p. 262, folio 250)
- [PK] **Pir Zaki / پیر ذکی** — entry no. ۲۰۷; date (not stated); "مزار عین دروازہ ذکی کے درمیان ہے اور اسی بزرگ کے نام سے دروازہ شہر کا مشہور ہے" — in the middle of **Zaki Gate**, which is named after him; *Tuhfat al-Wasilin* has him **martyred in the Mughal fighting**, resisting them when the city fell; **two graves — the head's in the gate, the body's in a stable inside the city adjoining it**. (p. 262, folio 250)
- [PK] **Pir Balkhi / پیر بلخی** — entry no. ۲۰۸; "اصلی نام ان کا تحفۃ الواصلین میں تحریر نہیں"; mazar "کشمیری بازار کے سر راہ ایک مکان کے اندر پختہ چونہ گچ" — on the roadside of **Kashmiri Bazar**; of **Balkh**, came to Hind when Chingiz Khan took it, and was killed with his murids and students when the Chingizid army besieged Lahore hunting Prince Jalal al-Din Khwarazmi; **the editor reads a tablet in the Lahore Museum** that "پیر بلخی کا لوح مزار معلوم ہوتا ہے": *Abu al-Muhamid al-Husain b. Muhammad al-Husain Abu Bakr al-Dhikri al-Balkhi*, aged **98**, martyred **9 Zi al-Hijja 643 AH** (so born 545), and redates the martyrdom away from Chingiz Khan to the Mongol attacks under **Ala al-Din Masud Shah, 643 AH/1245 CE** — **the book's one shrine treated through its epigraphy, and among the strongest candidate rows here.** (pp. 262–263, folios 250–251; pp. 351–352, folios 339–340)
- [PK] **Pir Siraj al-Din, known as Pir Siraji / پیر سراجی** — entry no. ۲۰۹; date (not stated); mazar in **محلہ جوڑی [OCR?]**; came to Lahore **from Bukhara in the reign of Sultan Muhammad Tughluq**; refused the office of *qaza*, and when the king ordered him killed "لاہور کے عمائد و ارکان جمع ہو کر" got his life spared, after which he closed his teaching, dismissed his murids and lived in seclusion until he died. (p. 263, folio 251)
- [PK] **Mazar Shahid Ganj, Mohalla Sadhwan / مزار شہید گنج، محلہ سادھواں** — entry no. ۲۱۱; date (not stated); "اگرچہ قبر ایک ہے، مگر ہزاروں شہید دفنائے ہوئے ہیں" — one grave over thousands of martyrs; per *Tuhfat al-Wasilin*, when the Ghaznavid sultanate held the Punjab and Lahore was its capital, Raja Anangpal son of Raja Jaipal besieged the city for **six months** with no relief from Ghazni, and after it fell "بقدر دو ہزار نعش کے مسلمان اس جگہ پر دفنائے گئے" — some two thousand bodies; mosques pulled down and temples re-founded until the Ghazni army returned. **Name coincidence with two archive gurdwaras — see Doubts.** (p. 264, folio 252)
- [PK] **Dan Shahid / دان شہید** — entry no. ۲۱۲; date (not stated); "اصلی نام ان کا معلوم نہیں"; mazar in a corner of a roofed roadside house near **Shahnawaz's stable**; "ہر سال اعتقاد مند لوگ مل کر عرس کرتے ہیں" — an annual urs by his devotees. (p. 264, folio 252)
- [PK] **Shaikh Musa Khokhar / شیخ موسیٰ کھوکھر** — entry no. ۲۳۳; date (not stated); mazar "محکمہ جمی [OCR?] کے روبرو بھاٹی دروازے کے باہر", outside the **Bhati Gate**; faiz from **Shaikh Bahlul Daryai**, and Shaikh Lal Husain Lahori showed him "کمال ادب" as a pir-brother; "تمام عمر ان کی ریاضت و مجاہدہ میں گذری". (p. 273, folio 261)
- [PK] **Shaikh Muhtaram / شیخ محترم** — entry no. ۲۳۴; **year not solved**: a chronogram quatrain is printed inside the dome but not resolved ("قطب حق شاہ محترم ز جہاں / رفت در بزم اولیائے سلف …"); rauza west of **بدھو کے پڑاوے**, "گنبد بہت عمدہ پختہ بنا ہوا ہے" with Quranic verses and the durud inside; "اب غیر آباد ہے" and "معلوم نہیں ہوتا کہ کس خاندان کا یہ بزرگ تھا". (p. 273, folio 261)
- [PK] **Shah Rahmat Allah Qureshi (Multani then Lahori) / شاہ رحمۃ اللہ قریشی** — entry no. ۲۲۹; date (not stated); descendant of Baha al-Din Zakariya; descendants at **Lahore and mauza ڈھولن وال**, one of them, **Bahadur Shah**, personally known to the author; "ملتانی و لاہوری و امرتسری جولاہے دریائی باف اس خاندان کے مرید بہت ہیں" — many **daryai-weaving julahas** of Multan, Lahore and Amritsar are the family's murids; "یہ مزار فرشتوں کا مزار کہلاتا ہے", built in one night; nazr, demolition and rebuilding recorded. (p. 271, folio 259)
- [PK] **Shah Farid Naushahi / شاہ فرید نوشاہی** — entry no. ۲۳۵; death year **not stated in the text** and a year from elsewhere disputed by the editor; mazar "موضع ڈھولن وال کے قریب لاہور سے تین میل … جنوب کی طرف" — three miles south of Lahore near mauza Dholanwal; a sayyid, formerly "امرائے بادشاہی میں سے صاحب منصب و جاگیر", who gave everything away to become a murid of Shaikh Pir Muhammad Sachiar and came to Lahore with the khirqa of khilafat; "اب بھی اس سلسلہ کے مرید لاہور میں بہت ہیں". (pp. 273–274, folios 261–262)
- [PK] **Shah Zia al-Din Shirwani / شاہ ضیاء الدین شروانی** — entry no. ۲۲۱; date (not stated); rauza outside Lahore **north of mauza Mozang**; native of **ملک شیروان**, completed under Khwaja Shams al-Din Turk Chishti Panipati and settled at Lahore after that master's death. (p. 268, folio 256)
- [PK] **Pir Hadi Rahnuma / پیر ہادی رہنما** — entry no. ۲۱۸; died "**سنہ چھ سو اکیاسی**", 681 — **which contradicts the maqbara's stated construction "بابر شاہ کے عہد میں"**; a Shamsi sayyid of the descendants of Shah Shams al-Din Tabrizi, father **Sayyid Abdul Qadir**; the maqbara on the **Anarkali–Mian Mir road** holds three graves, his and those of his brothers **Muhsin Shah and Abdullah Shah**; stone stripping, a crypt and double graves recorded; "ان کی اولاد سادات ناروال [OCR?] وغیرہ سب اب شیعہ مذہب رکھتے ہیں". (p. 267, folio 255)
- [PK] **Pir Ghazi, known as Pir az Ghaib / پیر غازی المشہور پیر از غیب** — entry no. ۲۲۷; **name and date both not stated** — "اس بزرگ کا اصلی حال کچھ ظاہر نہیں ہوتا"; sited beyond Data Darbar on a high plinth; the grave's discovery at Zeb al-Nisa's wall, and a pebble cure; "اکثر لوگ اس بزرگ کی زیارت کو آتے ہیں". (p. 270, folio 258)
- [PK] **Haji Jamiat / Haji Jamil and the Qadam Rasul / مزار حاجی جمعیت و مزار قدم رسول** — entry no. ۲۳۰; date (not stated); "یہ مکان ریل کے پڑاؤ کے شمال کی طرف غیر آباد پڑا ہے" — north of the railway halt, lying unfrequented; allegiance to Shah [rang OCR?] Bilawal, khalifa of Lal Husain Lahori; his forebears came **from Iran** bringing "قدم رسول کا ایک پتھر سرخ", a red footprint stone, over which he built a dome; the inscription was copied by the author. **The heading says حاجی جمعیت and the text حاجی جمیل.** (pp. 271–272, folios 259–260)
- [PK] **Hazrat Shah Hasan Wali Kamil / شاہ حسن ولی کامل** — entry no. ۲۴۷, the book's last numbered entry; "**یہ نہیں معلوم کہ یہ بزرگ کس زمانے میں ہوئے ہیں**" — undated by the author's own admission; mazar in the courtyard of the **مسجد لوہڑ [OCR?] والی** in Mohalla Mochi Darwaza; "طریق اس بزرگ کا سہروردیہ سنا جاتا ہے", the order only *heard* to be Suhrawardi. (p. 279, folio 267)
- [PK] **Shaikh Ali Rangrez / شیخ علی رنگریز** — entry no. ۲۴۴; death year (not stated); mazar toward the gate-corner of **Sayyid Jan Muhammad Huzuri**; he and his brother **Shaikh Wali** came "تغلقیہ سلطنت کے وقت عرب سے لاہور میں" and opened a **dyeing shop**, and were buried here; the dyers' devotion and an annual urs recorded. (pp. 277–278, folios 265–266)
- [PK] **The relic collections / تفصیل زیارات** — entry-level material, undated as objects but with a full provenance: **twenty-nine relics in Lahore Fort** "بحفاظت سرکاری" and **forty-five (the sub-counts sum to 46) in the family of the late Faqir Aziz al-Din and Nur al-Din**, traced from Timur's siege of Damascus in 803 and the Constantinople envoy's gift of 805, to Babur's Delhi, the Chaghtai kings, Malika Zamani's flight to Kabul, plunder at Sialkot fort, a pawn and an 80,000-rupee deed, Rasulnagar, Gujranwala fort, Sada Kaur at Makerian, Chunda, Sher Singh, Raja Hira Singh's haveli — where "جس قدر موئے مبارک نلکیوں میں تھے۔ وہ سب گم ہو گئے اور نلکیاں خالی رہ گئیں" — and finally Jawahar Singh's removal to the Fort, "اب تک قلعہ میں موجود ہیں". (pp. 256–261, folios 244–249)

**Lahore figures the book names without a grave**

Spiritual masters, teachers and kin named in passing, useful as cross-references rather than as rows:
**Maulana Ismail Lahori**, teacher of Shaikh Daud Kirmani (p. 49) · **Shaikh Saad Allah Multani then Lahori**, d. 999 AH/1590 CE, Shah Husain's teacher and, on the editor's reading, the source of his Malamati leanings (p. 56) · **Shah Bahlul Daryai**, pir of Shah Husain, named with that attribute where entry no. 10 has only "Shaikh Bahlul Qadri" (p. 54) · **Shaikh Adam Banuri**, who walked from Banur to Lahore for Shaikh Tahir (p. 59) · **Shaikh Khizr Sistani**, Mian Mir's pir (p. 60) · **Hayat al-Mir Zinda Pir / Sayyid Jamal Allah**, found living in a cell at the **Miani graveyard** — the figure whose very existence the editor questioned at p. 45 (pp. 45, 64) · **Mulla Fazil** and his madrasa at Lahore (p. 66) · **Shaikh Hamid Lahori** (p. 78) · **Shaikh Muhammad Fazil Lahori** and **Shaikh Asad Allah Lahori**, Shah Raza's father and grandfather (p. 73) · **Shaikh Murad Bakhsh b. Shaikh Faiz** (p. 82) · **Mian Taimur Lahori, Mian Hamid Qari Lahori, Mian Muhammad Aqil Lahori, Mian Jan Muhammad Qassabpuri**, the four named *mudarris* of the Parvezabad madrasa (pp. 187, 189) · **Hafiz Qadir Bakhsh, madh-khwan**, called to Faiz Bakhsh's deathbed (p. 126) · **Maulvi Ghulam Rasul Fazil Lahori**, entry no. 160 — **no death year and no grave in the entry**, appendix rows 59 **and** 63 both → the **Miani graveyard** (pp. 229, 290) · **Maulvi Ghulam Allah Fazil Lahori**, entry no. 165, d. **1270 AH [OCR?]**, "مدفن مقدس لاہور میں ہے", who took over his brother Ghulam Rasul's teaching establishment (pp. 231–232) · **Maulana Jan Muhammad Fazil Lahori**, entry no. 164, d. **1268 AH [OCR?]**, mazar in Lahore, with the book's only explicit statement that a shrine family failed: "افسوس ہے کہ ان کی اولاد میں سے کوئی لائق پیدا نہ ہوا" (p. 231) · **Maulvi Ghulam Farid Lahori**, entry no. 153, d. **1216 AH**, "مزار پر انوار گورستان میانی میں ہے", appendix row 57 → Miani (pp. 222, 290) · **Maulana Muhammad Baqir b. Sharaf al-Din Lahori**, author of *Kanz al-Hidayat* and khalifa of Khwaja Muhammad Masum Sirhindi, reproduced in facsimile (pp. 284–285) · **Shah Chiragh Gilani Lahori**, Shah Dargahi's pir (p. 267) · **Shaikh Haidar Shah**, Faiz Bakhsh's pir (p. 125) · **Maulana Nur Muhammad داقی [OCR?] Lahori**, Haji Muhammad Saeed's classmate (p. 215) · **Hafiz Nemat Allah Lahori** and **Shaikh Nur Muhammad Mufti Lahori**, each the subject of an editor's article (p. 307).

**The author's own list of roughly fifty Punjab figures "documented only in this book"** (p. 32, folio 20) — the book's own acquisition list, mostly of Lahore, and the obvious starting point: Sayyid Kamil Shah Lahori, Shaikh Muhammad Arif Chishti Sabri Lahori, Shaikh Muhammad Siddiq Lahori, Shaikh Muhammad Salim Lahori, Shaikh Faiz Bakhsh Lahori, Sayyid Mittha Lahori, Khwaja Ayub Qureshi Lahori, Shaikh Fateh Shah Shattari Lahori, Maulvi Ghulam Farid Lahori, Mufti Rahim Allah Qureshi, Shaikh Nur Ahmad known as Nur Husain Qadri, Miran Sayyid Ghulam Muhi al-Din, Maulvi Ghulam Rasul Lahori, **شیخ لدھی شاہ [OCR?] لاہوری**, Maulana Jan Muhammad Fazil Lahori, Maulvi Ghulam Allah Fazil Lahori, Mufti Ghulam Muhammad, Miran Sayyid Ghulam Shah, Sayyid Ghulam Ghaus, Sain Qutb Shah Lahori, Masum Shah Majzub Lahori, Mustaqim Shah Faizpuri, Faqir Taje Shah, Nizam Shah Lahori, Mastan Shah Lahori, Jali Shah Lahori, Mai Bhag Lahori, Sayyid Sarbuland, **پیر فکی [OCR?]**, **سید صوف [OCR?]**, Pir Balkhi, Pir Siraj Din, Pir Bhola Lahori, Shaikh Fateh Shah Amritsari, Sharaf Shah Lahori, Shah Zia al-Din Lahori, Pir Burhan Lahori, Makhdum Shah Alam Sadr-e-Jahan, Shah Abdul Razzaq Makki Lahori, **پیر زہدی [OCR?] لاہوری**, Pir Ghazi Lahori, Shah Rahmat Allah Qureshi Multani then Lahori, Shaikh Musa Khokhar, Shaikh Muhtaram, Mufti Muhammad Mukarram Qureshi, **شیخ گھلن شاہ [OCR?]** and Shah Hasan.

### Multan

- [PK] **Shaikh Sadr al-Din Arif b. Baha al-Din Zakariya / شیخ صدر الدین عارف** — entry no. 102; died **23 Zi al-Hijja 684 AH** — **four death years are in play**; **mazar in Multan**; son and janishin, who sat on the masnad-e irshad after his father; the estate division is recorded; editor: he combined "درس و تدریس" with guidance, *Tasrif-e Jadwali* is his, and Shaikh Ziya al-Din collected his malfuzat as *Kunuz al-Fawaid* (extracts in *Akhbar al-Akhyar* 59–61, Meerut 1277 AH); a 1958 Faridi monograph exists. **He has no archive row though his mazar is at Multan.** (pp. 161–162, folios 149–150; p. 304)
- [PK] **Shaikh Ahmad Mashuq-e Ilahi / شیخ احمد معشوق الٰہی** — entry no. 104; died "in the year **723 [OCR?]**"; **mazar in Multan**; khalifa of Sadr al-Din Arif; his father **Mir Qandhari** "شہر قندھار میں دکان سوداگری کرتا" and turned him out with some capital, so he came from Qandahar to Multan and set up a trading shop, was seen in the street, summoned to the **khanqah**, given the Shaikh's own "پس ماندہ شربت", and that hour "دکان کا سامان … غربا و فقرا میں تقسیم کر دیا"; a confrontation with the ulama recorded; his ecstasy was cited in Nizam al-Din Auliya's assemblies (*Fawaid al-Fawad* 436, 439). **Also has no archive row.** (pp. 163–164, folios 151–152; p. 349)
- [PK] **Shaikh Hamid al-Din Abu al-Hakim Qureshi Hakkari Suhrawardi, Sultan al-Tarikin / سلطان التارکین** — entry no. 106; khalifa of Rukn al-Din Multani; thirteen links to Abu Sufyan b. Harith; his great ancestor Qutb al-Din was "خطہ کیچ و مقران کا بادشاہ", **king of Kech and Makran**, and his maternal grandfather **سید احمد تختہ [OCR?] لاہور**; "شیخ حمید الدین نے سلطنت ظاہری ترک کر کے خرقہ فقر کا پہنا"; his birth date, on Shaikh Shahr Allah's *Tazkira-e Hamidiya*, begins "بتاریخ بارہویں ربیع الاول" and **the entry breaks off**. (p. 167, folio 155)
- [PK] **The tail of entry no. 106, subject unnamed in the transcription** — born "**سنہ ۵۷۷ھ [OCR?]**", died **22 Rabi al-Awwal 737 AH**, **age 160**, "بزرگان خاندان سہروردیہ سے کسی بزرگ نے اتنی عمر نہیں پائی"; "مزار پر انوار حضرت کا موضع **موعلاۃ [OCR?] ملتان** میں زیارت گاہ خلق ہے". The entry heading falls in the previous chunk, so the name is not recoverable — **do not attach these dates to any Multan row without checking the printed page.** (p. 168, folio 156)
- [PK] **Shaikh Shahr Allah Multani / شیخ شہر اللہ ملتانی** — died **23 Zi al-Hijja 920 AH**; "آپ ملتان میں اپنے مشائخ کے پہلو میں مدفون ہیں" — **buried at Multan beside his own mashaikh**; b. Rahmat Allah b. ستاجی [OCR?] b. Kalu Langah Multani; a scholar and gnostic of his time; three sons Baha al-Din, Shaikh Muhammad Ismail, Muhammad Sadr al-Din; Shaikh Musa Ahangar's first pir; author of the *Tazkira-e Hamidiya*, written in Sultan Sikandar Lodhi's era (d. 923 AH/1517 CE) and translated into Urdu by Pir Ghulam Dastgir Nami, Lahore 1959. (pp. 167, 169, 173, 175)
- [PK] **Sher Shah Qadri Multani / شیر شاہ قادری ملتانی** — entry no. ۲۱۶; date (not stated); "مشہور ترین بزرگان ملتان سے"; Qadiriya, "خاندان قادریہ عالیہ", whose "پیری شجرہ" joins the **Gilani sayyids of Uch** and who took faiz from **Sayyid Hamid Ganj Bakhsh Uchi**; "ہزاروں لوگ اس خاندان کے مرید ہیں"; "**کنارے دریا پر ملتان سے پانچ کوس پرے** ان کا مقبرہ معلیٰ زیارت گاہ خلق اللہ ہے" — on the riverbank five kos beyond Multan; the **sajjada nashins** "ظاہری و باطنی عزت رکھتے ہیں". Whether he is the **Sayyid Sher Shah Multani** named as a perfect khalifa of Hamid Ganj Bakhsh at p. 48 the book does not say. (pp. 48, 266, folio 254)
- [PK] **Bibi Rasti / بی بی رستی** — entry no. 197; a named woman of the Multan Suhrawardi house, the material given under that shrine's own heading. (pp. 166, 252)
- Named without a grave: **Maulana Zahir al-Din**, the khalifa who prepared Rukn-e Alam's burial (p. 166) · **Shaikh Abdullah b. Yusuf Qurashi Multani**, from whom Abd al-Wahhab Bukhari drew faiz at Delhi (p. 176) · **Shah Murad Multani** and **Shaikh Dargahi Lahori**, links in Muhammad Saeed Sharaqpuri's eight-step chain (p. 121) · **Muhammad Anwar Multani**, one of Muhkam al-Din Uwaisi's nine khalifas (p. 220) · **Shaikh Husam al-Din Multani Muttaqi, Sayyid Shah Alam, Bahan al-Din Qutb, Makhdum Chin, Shaikh Milo, Makhdum Tayyib, Shaikh Abd al-Karim**, links in Mian Kalan's thirteen-step chain to Baha al-Din Zakariya (p. 188).

### Uch

- [PK] **Sayyid Muhammad Ghaus Uchi Jili Gilani / سید محمد غوث اوچی حلبی گیلانی** — died **923 AH**, buried **at Uch**; "اول پنجاب میں آئے" — the first of Abd al-Qadir Jilani's line to come to the Punjab; his forebear Sayyid Abul Abbas Ahmad b. Sayyid Sufi left Baghdad with his brother Sayyid Abu Sulaiman at the sack under Hulagu (the footnote dates it "۶۵۶ھ / ۱۲۵۸ء") and settled at **Aleppo**, where he was born; travelled, performed Hajj several times, stayed some years at Lahore, returned to Aleppo, and came by Khurasan to Multan and Uch; **Sultan Husain Mirza of Sind and Sultan Sikandar Lodhi** became his murids; four sons — Sayyid Abdul Qadir Sani, Sayyid Abdullah Rabbani, Mubarak Haqqani, Sayyid Muhammad Nurani — "from each of whom a separate faiz flowed"; a scholar and poet with the takhallus "Qadri", diwan in the Siraj al-Din Azar collection, Punjab University, no. **۸۱۴ [OCR?]**, and *Miftah al-Ikhlas*. (pp. 40–41, folios 28–29; p. 294)
- [PK] **Miran Sayyid Mubarak Haqqani b. Sayyid Muhammad Uchi Halabi Gilani / مبارک حقانی** — entry no. 4; died at Lahore in "نو سو چھبیس سال ہجری", **926 AH**, and "بدن کو اوچ میں لا کر دفن کیا" — **body carried to Uch and buried**; left habitation for the jungle, twelve years intoxicated, "کوئی انسان اس کے روبرو نہ آ سکتا"; only one man, **Maruf Chishti**, received khilafat from him. (p. 44, folio 32)
- [PK] **Sayyid Abdul Qadir Sani b. Sayyid Muhammad Halabi Uchi Gilani / سید عبدالقادر ثانی** — entry no. 2; died **17 Rabi al-Awwal 940 AH / 1533 CE**; **mazar at Uch**; taught the outer and inner sciences by his father; received the title "Abdul Qadir Sani" from Ghaus al-Azam "عالم غیب میں"; refused the Delhi court's grants. (pp. 42–43, folios 30–31)
- [PK] **Sayyid Hamid Ganj Bakhsh b. Sayyid Abdul Razzaq b. Sayyid Abdul Qadir Sani / سید حامد گنج بخش** — entry no. 8; died **978 AH**, sharpened by the footnote to **19 Zi Qada 978 AH**, chronogram "شیخ محبوبی"; **buried at Uch**, where he lived all his life; a great elder of the **Qadiriyya Azamiyya**; "بادشاہان وقت بھی ان کی آستان بوسی کو اپنا افتخار جانتے تھے"; perfect khalifas **Sayyid Sher Shah Multani** and **Shaikh Daud Kirmani**; after his death **Sayyid Jamal al-Din Musa** sat on the masnad-e mashikhat; a contemporary *Manaqib al-Asfiya* survives at Gujrat. (pp. 48, folio 36; p. 293)
- [PK] **Sayyid Sadr al-Din, known as Shaikh Rajan Qattal b. Sayyid Ahmad Kabir Bukhari Uchi / شیخ رجن قتال** — entry no. 108; died "بقول صاحب معارج الولایت بتاریخ سولہویں جمادی الآخر سن آٹھ سو ستائیس ہجری" — **16 Jumada al-Akhir 827 AH**; **mazar at Uch**; brother of Makhdoom Jahaniyan, khirqa "اپنے باپ اور بھائی دونوں سے"; Jahaniyan on him: "صدر الدین کو اپنی ذات کے عشق میں مستغرق کر رکھا ہے"; editor adds birth **20 Shaban 760 AH / 1359 CE**, a dispute over the laqab, his relations with **Firoz Shah Tughluq** and the village and cash he was given, and six further sources. (pp. 169–171, folios 157–159)
- [PK] **Sayyid Nasir al Din b. Makhdoom Jahaniyan Jalal al-Din Bukhari Uchi / نر ناصر الدین** — entry no. 109; died **847 AH**; "مزار گوہر بار اوچ میں زیارت گاہ خلق اللہ ہے"; called *Nar* Nasir al-Din for his offspring — his sons "ایک سو تک پہنچ گئی تھی", a hundred, of whom the long-lived were **twenty-one sons and two daughters**; engaged in guidance by his father's instruction although the sajjada went to Rajan Qattal; "لاکھوں طالبان خدا ان کی دستگیری سے مقام قرب تک پہنچے". (p. 172, folio 160)
- **Uch as a Qadiri Gilani cluster** — the book places Sayyid Muhammad Ghaus (d. 923), Mubarak Haqqani (d. 926, carried from Lahore) and Abdul Qadir Sani (d. 940) all at Uch, **distinct from the archive's Suhrawardi Bukhari rows at Uch Sharif**. (pp. 41–44, folios 29–32)
- [PK] **Makhdoom Nau Bahar Shah, sajjada nashin of Uch Bukhari** — the living custodian in whose hands the manuscript of *Mazhar-e Jalali* rests. (p. 169, folio 157)

### Satghara, Hujra, Jarwa, Chunian, Sharaqpur and the Bari Doab

- [PK] **Sayyid Muhammad Ghaus Bala Pir b. Sayyid Zain al-Abidin b. Sayyid Abdul Qadir Sani Uchi Gilani** — entry no. 5; died **5 Shawwal 959 AH**; murid of his grandfather, who raised him because his own father had been "کفار کے ہاتھوں" martyred young in his father's presence; fell out with his uncle Sayyid Hamid Ganj Bakhsh, left Uch, and settled at **Satghara**, "a famous qasba in Punjab", where he died **and where his rauza also stands**. (p. 45, folio 33)
- [PK] **Sayyid Baha al-Din Gilani, known as Bahawal Sher Qalandar Hujrawi / بہاول شیر قلندر** — entry no. 7; died **18 Shawwal 973 AH**, "buried at **Hujra**", the year also derivable from the chronogram phrase "عبدالقادر ثانی"; descendant of Sayyid Abdul Razzaq b. Abdul Qadir Jilani through the full printed chain; born at **Baghdad**, came to Hindustan with his father and paternal aunt and settled at **Badayun**, where his father died and his aunt, "جو رابعہ عصر تھی", trained him; the founding of Hujra is given as a legend. The **fourth appendix** independently names him "**سید بہاء الدین (بہاول شیر قلندر)**", builder of the hujra at **Hujra Shah Muqim** whose "بنیانھا الی الان قائمۃ", the building still standing, and works the numerical value of "عبدالقادر ثانی" out to **۹۷۳ھ**. (pp. 46–47, folios 34–35; pp. 342–343, folios 330–331)
- [PK] **Sayyid Muhammad Muqim, entitled Muhkam al-Din / سید محمد مقیم محکم الدین** — dates (not stated); the later elder of the same family "جن کی بزرگی اور صاحب کرامات ہونے کے باعث یہ علاقہ **حجرہ شاہ مقیم** کہلایا" — the man the place is named for; the source is an Arabic *Kifayat al-Nahw* by **Sayyid Inayat Allah Husaini** of the same family, dedicated to Muhammad Shah (1132–1161 AH / 1719–1748 CE), seen in Khalil al-Rahman Daudi's library, Lahore; the family "حضرت شیخ عبدالقادر جیلانی … کی اولاد امجاد میں سے تھے". (pp. 342–343, folios 330–331)
- [PK] **Sayyid Muhammad Nur b. Sayyid Bahawal Sher Gilani Jarwi / سید محمد نور** — entry no. 13; died "in the year nine hundred eighty-eight Hijri", **988 AH**; eldest son and khalifa of Bahawal Sher; his mother's father was **Shah Kamal Bukhari**, "whose mazar is in **qasba Chunian**" and who is famous as **Pir Jahanian**; **editor: the contemporary *Tazkira-e Muqimi* writes the name نور محمد, "جو درست سمجھنا چاہیے"** — which should be considered correct. (p. 51, folio 39)
- [PK] **Sayyid Muhammad Muqim Muhkam al-Din b. Shah Abul Muali b. Sayyid Muhammad Nur b. Sayyid Bahawal Sher Gilani Jarwi** — entry no. 23; death given as "سال ایک ہزار پچپن ہجری", **1055**, with "مزار پر انوار بمقام **جرہ** زیارت گاہ خلق اللہ ہے" — **the editor prefers 9 Shawwal 1050 AH** from the near-contemporary *Tazkira-e Muqimi* (ms p. 23); murid and khalifa of Sayyid Jamal Allah Hayat al-Mir Zinda Pir; his *Durr al-Ajaib* on marifat and tariqat published "سجادہ نشینان جروہ", by the **sajjada nashins of Jarwa**; Maulana Abdul Hakim Sialkoti came to him over passages of the *Fusus al-Hikam*. **This entry's father is a different Shah Abul Muali — see Doubts.** (p. 64, folio 52)
- [PK] **Pir Sayyid Kamal, known as Pir Jahanian / پیر سید کمال المشہور پیر جہانیاں** — entry no. ۲۱۴; date (not stated); "اس بزرگ کا مزار **قصبہ چونیاں** میں زیارت گاہ خلق ہے۔ مکان نہایت متبرک ہے اور **قبر بہت لمبی ہے**" — a very long grave; "سادات بخاری میں سے کمال سید"; "ان کی اولاد بھی قصبہ چونیاں میں رہتی ہے" and "دور دور سے لوگ ان کی زیارت کو آتے ہیں". **Name resemblance to an archive row — see Doubts.** (p. 266, folio 254)
- [PK] **Shaikh Ilm al-Din Chuniwal / شیخ علم الدین چونیوال** — entry no. 112; died **916 AH**; "روضہ حضرت کا اسی علاقہ میں ہے", in the Chunian area; khalifa of Abd al-Jalil Chuhar Lahori, to whom "خدمت کپڑے دھونے کی سپرد تھی" — the service of **washing clothes**; posted to qasba Chunian by his murshid's order; "**دھوبی لوگ حضرت کا عرس کرتے ہیں**" — the **dhobis perform his urs**; the editor reports a different affiliation for him. (p. 174, folio 162)
- [PK] **Shaikh Muhammad Saeed Chishti Sabri Sharaqpuri / شیخ محمد سعید شرقپوری** — entry no. 74; died "باقوال صحیح", by correct reports, in the year **1214 AH**; **mazar in Sharaqpur**; resided at "قصبہ شرقپور متعلقہ ضلع لاہور"; "چونکہ یہ بزرگ قوم کے **خوجہ** تھے۔ پہلے اپنے ہم قوم خوجوں کے ساتھ محنت مزدوری سے گذارہ کرتے تھے" — a **Khoja** who lived by manual labour with his fellow Khojas until divine longing seized him; an eight-link chain given by name through Shah Murad Multani, Shaikh جیوی [OCR?] Shah Gujrati, Shaikh Zakariya, Shaikh Haji Qutb, Shaikh Dargahi Lahori and Haji Abu Said Hanafi Gangohi to Shaikh Nizam al-Din Balkhi; a grain-cart miracle. **Sharaqpur holds an archive row for a different saint — see Doubts.** (pp. 121–122, folios 109–110)
- [PK] **Sayyid Abd al-Karim, famous as Pir Bhawan Shah b. Shah بلاق [OCR?] Lahori / پیر بھاون شاہ** — entry no. ۱۵۲; died **1213 AH**; **buried at مرلوپور [OCR?]**; "بارہ کے سادات میں سے", of the **sayyids of Barha**, "ہر ایک سلسلہ میں سے ان کو بہرہ حاصل تھا" with a Qadiri chain to Mian Mir; three successive residences — mauza **Mazang**, then twelve years' worship in the jungle of **پٹھو پور [OCR?]**, then **مرلوپور [OCR?]**, "ایک قصبہ دامن کوہ میں"; "قوم **کھکہ [OCR?]** تمام و کمال حضرت کے مرید ہوئے"; a raid by **Sahib Singh Bedi** recorded. (pp. 221–222, folios 209–210)
- [PK] **Hazrat Ghulam Muhi al-Din Qasuri Mujaddidi / غلام محی الدین قصوری** — entry no. 97, the book's longest entry; **born 1202, died 1270, aged sixty-eight**, with an unusually elaborate chronogram; "**ان سب بزرگوں کے مزار قصور میں ہیں**" — **the mazars of all these elders are at Kasur**; madhhab "حنفی اعظمی"; held conviction toward **both** Ghaus al-Thaqalain and the Mujaddid; first murid of his paternal uncle **Hafiz Shaikh Muhammad**, with whom he read the Mujaddid's *Maktubat* lesson by lesson, then bai'at with Shah Ghulam Ali at Delhi; father **Hafiz Shaikh Murtaza** in the text, **Shaikh Mustafa** by the editor's correction; grandfather **Shaikh Ghulam Murtaza**; his son **Shaikh Abd al-Rasul** described as living; a standing miracle of the platform. (pp. 150–154, folios 138–142)
- [PK] **Shaikh Ghulam Murtaza Qasuri / حافظ غلام مرتضیٰ قصوری** — the text gives his death as the year **1202** and the ijazat of all four orders, as a murid of the khalifa of Haji Fath Ali Muttaqi Sialkoti; editor: "مفصل حالات نہیں ملتے"; "آپ نے پنجاب میں **سکھ گردی** سے تنگ آکر **پشاور** ہجرت فرمائی", he migrated to Peshawar wearied by the Sikh *gardi*; a contemporary of **Ahmad Shah Abdali**, among the ulama who answered his question in the Punjab; "آپ پشاور میں فوت ہوئے۔ اور وہاں سے آپ کی نعش **قصور** لا کر دفن کی گئی" — died at Peshawar, corpse brought to **Kasur** and buried. (pp. 152, 208, folio 140)
- [PK] **Haji Hafiz Qari Abd al-Malik / حاجی حافظ قاری عبدالملک** — the family's great ancestor, who "ملک سندھ سے آکر قصور میں سکونت پذیر ہوئے", came **from Sindh and settled at Kasur**, and was foremost of the age in the science of *qirat*; buried with the rest at Kasur. (pp. 151–152, folio 139)
- [PK] **Maulana Ghulam Muhyi al-Din Qasuri / مولانا غلام محی الدین قصوری** — **d. 1270 AH** on the editor's own book title; a manuscript collector whose *zakhira* is at the **Ganj Bakhsh Library, Rawalpindi**; biography *Anwar-e Muhi al-Din*, Lyallpur 1966; the colophon of his *Risala Ilm Mirath* is reproduced in facsimile. **Keep distinct from Miran Sayyid Ghulam Muhyi al-Din of Lahore, d. 1240 AH.** (pp. 187, 284, 302, 307)
- [PK] **Abdullah Khweshgi / Khushgi Qasuri / عبداللہ خویشگی قصوری** — author of *Ma'arij al-Wilayat* (1096 AH) and *Akhbar al-Awliya* (1077 AH), one of the book's ten-times-cited authorities and the subject of the editor's own first monograph, *Ahwal wa Athar-e Abdullah Khweshgi Qasuri*, Lahore 1972. (pp. 293, 303)
- [PK] **Shaikh Nur Qasuri** and **Shaikh عطو [OCR?] Baghban** — both murids of Alim al-Din; Baghban's descendants are at **فیض پور [OCR?] باغبانان**. (p. 175, folio 163)
- [PK] **Sayyid Fazl Shah of mauza Sanda** — murid and host of Qalandar Shah in the food-multiplication miracle at **mauza Sanda**. (p. 190, folio 188 as printed)
- Also named as located places with a grave or a grant attached: **qasba Sheikhupura, Shahdara, Garhi Shahu, Jarra/Jarwa, Bhalwal** and Lahore's **Delhi Gate** (pp. 62–67, folios 50–55) · **Shahpur** and "the old dome of the Madrasa Aliya" on the Sharaqpur–Lahore road (p. 121, folio 109) · **Kot Kahror / Kot Karor** and **Pak Pattan** (pp. 158, 160) · **Lakhi Jangal**, where Shah Maruf Chishti took the Qadiri khirqa (pp. 50–51).

### Gujrat, Gujranwala, Wazirabad and Sialkot

- [PK] **Pir Muhammad Sachiar Naushahi / پیر محمد سچیار نوشاہی** — entry no. 36; died "in the year one thousand one hundred and fifty-two", **1152** — **editor: "پیر محمد سچیار نوشاہیؒ کا سال وفات ۱۱۲۰ھ صحیح ہے اور مفتی صاحب نے ۱۱۵۲ھ غلط لکھا ہے"**, 1120 AH correct and 1152 wrong, with birth **۱۰۱۳ھ [OCR?]**; true khalifa of Nausha Ganj Bakhsh, who came to him as a child, titled "Sachiar" for having no equal in truth; settled at **mauza Nausherah**, said in the text to be **six miles** east of Gujrat and by the editor **۱۴ [OCR?] miles**; so given to wajd and sama that "لوگوں کو حضرت کے مر جانے کا شبہہ ہو جاتا تھا". (pp. 76–77, folios 74–75)
- [PK] **Sayyid Shah Ismat Allah Hamza Pahalwan Naushahi / عصمت اللہ حمزہ پہلوان نوشاہی** — **d. 1137 AH**; Nausha Ganj Bakhsh's grandson, to whom Shah Muhammad Ghaus went for twelve years. (p. 77, folio 75)
- [PK] **Shaikh Ghulam Husain of Walian Wali / شیخ غلام حسین والیاں والی** — entry no. 41; died **1260 AH**; editor locates the mazar at **Walian Wali, district Gujranwala**, on Sayyid Sharafat Naushahi's own communications, and gives his descent b. Bahram Khan b. Zar Bakhsh b. Rasida, of the **قوم تور [OCR?] alias داوال [OCR?]**; "آخری زمانے میں اگر ان کو قطب وقت لکھا جائے تو بجا ہے"; his son **Shaikh Qaisar Shah** benefited many after him — **editor: "اصل نام کبیر شاہ تھا۔ سال وفات ۱۲۸۱ھ"**, real name Kabir Shah, d. 1281 AH, born through the prayer of **Sayyid Ilahi Bakhsh Naushahi Sahnpalvi (d. 1253 AH)**. (p. 83, folio 71 as printed)
- [PK] **Sayyid Ahmad Shaikh al-Hind Gilani / سید احمد شیخ الہند گیلانی** — entry no. 33; died **1136 AH** and **buried at the place Kotla**; came from Baghdad to Hind and "متصل وزیر آباد کے ایک گاؤں کٹلہ نام آباد کر کے سکونت اختیار کی" — founded and settled a village named **Katla adjoining Wazirabad**; full genealogy to Abd al-Qadir Jilani; "ان کی اولاد موضع **خان پور** میں سکونت رکھتی ہے اور سلسلہ پیری کا اب تک جاری ہے". (p. 75, folio 63)
- [PK] **Maulana Haji Sa'd Allah / Asad Allah Wazirabadi / حاجی سعد اللہ وزیر آبادی** — **d. 1102 AH [OCR?]**; a great scholar with authority to guide and a khalifa of **Shaikh Adam Banuri**; "سلسلہ مجددیہ کے تذکروں میں آپ کا نام **اسد اللہ** بھی لکھا ہوا ہے" and in contemporary books it is Asad Allah that is written, with a letter of Khwaja Saif al-Din addressed to him; "ان کا مسکن و موطن **سرائے وزیر خان (وزیر آباد ضلع گجرات پاکستان)** تھا", and in **Peshawar, Lahore and Wazirabad** he propagated the **Mujaddidiya Adamiya** silsila; it was he who took Shaikh Sa'di Lahori to Shaikh Adam Banuri. (pp. 139, 213, folios 127, 201)
- [PK] **Makhdum Shah Alam Sadr Jahan / مخدوم شاہ عالم صدر جہاں** — entry no. ۲۲۲; died "in the year one thousand one hundred and thirty-six", **1136** (era not stated), "اور مخدوم شاہ عالم مادہ تاریخ وفات ہے" — **his own name is the chronogram**; active "دہلی کے علاقے میں"; "مزار ان کا **قصبہ وزیر آباد** میں زیارت گاہ خلق ہے". (p. 268, folio 256)
- [PK] **Shaikh Abd al-Hakim Sialkoti / شیخ عبدالحکیم سیالکوٹی** — entry no. 142 (contents: printed p. 196); died "باقوال صحیح" **1068 AH** — and **no grave is given**; "اکابر علما و اعاظم فضلائے پنجاب سے"; held great honour at the **Chaghtai** court and "بادشاہ کی اجازت سے اس نے لاہور میں درس جاری کیا", his written fatwa accepted by all the ulama of Hind; **Shaikh Ahmad Mujaddid Alf-e Thani titled him "آفتاب پنجاب"**, Sun of the Punjab, and he wrote *Dala'il al-Tajdid* in the Mujaddid's support; he translated Abd al-Qadir Jilani's *Ghunyat al-Talibin* into Persian "حسب الایمائے حضرت **شاہ بلاول لاہوری**"; among the few honoured in the Mughal era with the title *Allami*; sons **Maulvi Abdullah Lahori, Maulvi Rahmat Allah, Maulvi Rahman Quli**; a 1969 Panjab University PhD thesis and Muhammad Din Fauq's *Malik al-Ulama Sialkoti*, Lahore 1924. **A Sialkot figure, not the archive's Imam Ali-ul-Haq.** (pp. 19, 196, 208–209, folio 196; pp. 296, 302, 344)
- [PK] **Haji Fath Ali Muttaqi Sialkoti / حاجی فتح علی متقی سیالکوٹی** — dates (not stated); the shaykh whose khalifa initiated Shaikh Ghulam Murtaza Qasuri in all four orders. (p. 152, folio 140)
- [PK] Two parental graves in **district Gujrat**, recorded only by the editor from Sayyid Sharafat Naushahi: Mian Kalan's mother **Mai فربد [OCR?]** at **qasba جوگا کلیان [OCR?]** and his father at **mauza جیبہ چیمہ [OCR?] adjoining Ahmad Nagar**; and the editor's own **موضع کلاسکے [OCR?] چیمہ ضلع گجرات پاکستان**. (pp. 187, 242–243)
- Also named: **Sayyid Sharafat Naushahi**, resident of Gujrat and the editor's living informant (pp. 83, 187, 242–243) · **Muhammad Salih Kunjahi** (*Silsilat al-Awliya*, 1276 AH, autograph, owned at Gujrat), **Ghulam Muhyi al-Din Kunjahi** and **Sadaqat Muhammad Mah Kunjahi** (*Sawaqib al-Manaqib*, 1127 AH) — the Kunjah scholarly family (pp. 293–295) · **Chhani Lak [OCR?], district Gujrat** and **Pindi Bahauddin** as imprint places (pp. 292, 303, 333).

### Peshawar and the Frontier

- [PK] **Sayyid Hasan Peshawari Qadri Gilani / سید حسن پشاوری** — entry no. 31; died "in the year one thousand one hundred and fifteen", **1115**, and **is buried at Peshawar**; murid of his father Sayyid Abdullah Gilani; his ancestor Sayyid Mahmud migrated from Baghdad to "مٹھہ کے ملک"; "تمام عمر انہوں نے عبادت و ریاضت و تلقین طلبا میں صرف کی"; **the editor corrects him on three points** — it was **Sayyid Abdullah, known as Sahabi**, not Sayyid Mahmud, who came from Baghdad to Mittha; Sayyid Abdullah never went to Peshawar but "مٹھہ" and died there; and "یہ شجرہ نسب مفتی صاحب نے **غلط** لکھا ہے", the genealogy is wrongly written, the correct chain being the one Shah Muhammad Ghaus himself recorded (Amir Shah Qadri Gilani, *Tazkira-e Abdullah Sahabi*, Peshawar 1971; *Tazkira-e Mashaikh-e Qadiriya Hasaniya* 26); a *Khawariq al-Adat* (1189 AH) on him survives with Maulana Amir Shah Qadri, Peshawar. (pp. 72–73, folios 60–61; p. 294)
