- [PK] **Sayyid Shah Husain, known as Bhohre Wala / سید شاہ حسین بھوہرے والا** — died **1224 AH**, a year Imam Ali Shah derived from the phrase "مرشد محذو دمی [OCR?]"; lineage to the Prophet; "آپ نے مروجہ علوم کی تحصیل **پشاور** میں کی تھی"; khirqa of khilafat from **Hazrat Haji Ahmad, resident of Qalat**, and "پنجاب کو فیض یاب کیا". (p. 156, folio 144)
- [PK] **Khwaja Muhammad Siddiq Peshawari / خواجہ محمد صدیق پشاوری** — dates (not stated); the narrator of the mule miracle, named by the editor among Muhammad Masum's "محبوب ترین خلفا" with **eighteen letters** in volume one of the *Maktubat-e Masumiya* addressed to him. (p. 136, folio 124)
- [PK] **Akhund Muhammad Rafi Peshawari** — compiler of *Qiran al-Sa'dain*, the malfuzat of Haji Muhammad Saeed Lahori and Akhund Muhammad Masud Peshawari, 1162 AH. (pp. 215, 295)
- [PK] **Haji Ahmad, resident of Qalat** — dates (not stated); the shaykh from whom Shah Husain Bhohre Wala took the khirqa. (p. 156)
- [PK] Repositories that would hold more: the **Bhana Mari library** and the **Islamia College Library, Peshawar**, and Maulana Fazl Samdani's library at **مہمانہ ماڑی [OCR?] پشاور** (pp. 139–140, 295, 302).

### Bahawalpur, Maharan and the south

- [PK] **Shaikh Nur Muhammad Chishti, famous as Nur Muhammad مجھیل [OCR?] Maharvi / نور محمد مہاروی** — entry no. 72; died **1205 AH**; "مزار پر انوار قریہ **تاج سرور** متصل قصبہ **مہاراں** کے ہے" — at the village Taj Sarwar adjoining qasba Maharan; **greatest khalifa** of Fakhr-e Jahan, whom he went "پنجاب سے دہلی" to for bai'at, returning to settle "قصبہ مہاراں علاقہ **نواب بہاول خاں** میں"; four chief khalifas — Khwaja Thani titled **Khalifa Sahib**, Maulana Qazi Muhammad Aqil, Khwaja Muhammad Jamal, Khwaja Sulaiman — "اور خلیفے بھی آپ کے بہت ہیں"; editor: his ancestral name was **مجھیل [OCR?]** and Shah Fakhr al-Din changed it to Nur Muhammad; a ten-link paternal genealogy from Hindal b. Tatar; "آپ کا تعلق قوم **کھرل** سے تھا", of the **Kharral** tribe; **born 14 Ramadan 1142 AH at qasba Chotala**, east of Mahar towards Bahawalpur; bai'at **1165 AH**; three sons Nur al-Samad Shahid, Nur Ahmad, Nur Hasan; "جن میں سے **تیس** کے نام محفوظ ہیں" — thirty khalifa names preserved. (pp. 119–120, folios 107–108)
- [PK] **Nur Muhammad Thani Naruwala / نور محمد ثانی ناروالا** — the "خلیفہ صاحب" of the text, identified by the editor, who "۱۲۰۴ھ میں وصال فرمایا" — **d. 1204 AH**, a year before his own pir on the book's dates. (p. 120, folio 108)
- [PK] **Khwaja Hafiz Abd al-Khaliq Uwaisi / خواجہ حافظ عبدالخالق اویسی** — entry no. 150; died **7 Zil-Hijja 1185 AH**; "مزار گوہر بار قصبہ **مبارک پور متصل بہاولپور** میں زیارت گاہ خلق ہے"; "خاندان اویسیہ کے یہ بزرگ **موجد** ہیں" — **the founder of the Uwaisiya family**, whose faiz came from the spirituality of **Uwais Qarni**; *Lata'if-e Nafisiya fi Faza'il-e Uwaisiya* has him living **on the bank of the Sutlej**, fainting at "Allahu Akbar" and not coming to himself "جب تک گانے والے نہ گاتے"; a journey with Bulleh Shah and three nights of Uwais Qarni's visits; three sons — **Khwaja Qutb al-Din**, who as a child flew away in ecstasy; **Khwaja Arif**, "جن کا مزار بریلی میں ہے"; and **Shaikh Muharram**, "جو قصبہ **لیّہ** میں مدفون ہیں"; khalifa **Khwaja Muhkam al-Din Sahib al-Sair**. (pp. 217, 219–220, folios 205, 207–208)
- [PK] **Shaikh Muhkam al-Din Sahib al-Sair Uwaisi b. Hafiz Muhammad Arif / محکم الدین صاحب السیر** — entry no. 151; died **5 Rabi al-Thani 1197 AH**; mazar at **کوٹ لجنت [OCR?] adjoining Bahawalpur** — **editor: "خاندانی روایت کے مطابق … سال وفات ۶ ربیع الآخر [OCR?] ۱۱۸۸ھ [OCR?] ہے"**, by family tradition 6 Rabi al-Akhir 1188 AH; real nephew, murid and khalifa of Abd al-Khaliq; "اخیر زمانہ میں یہ بزرگ اپنے وقت کے **قطب** تھے"; **nine perfect khalifas** — Hafiz Qamar al-Din, Muhammad Salim Qureshi, Shah Abu al-Fath, Khwaja Sulaiman, Muhammad Anwar Multani, Allah Dad, Diwan Muhammad Ghaus, Dost Muhammad, Hafiz Abd al-Karim; his Persian *Talqin-e Ludanni* printed at the Abu al-Alai press, Agra; one cited source, Ahmad Din's *Chahar Bagh*, is **in Saraiki**. (pp. 220–221, folios 208–209)
- [PK] **Sultan Bala Din Uwaisi / سلطان بالا دین اویسی** — entry no. 157; died **1241 AH [OCR?]** (the numeral repeated in the margin as ۱۲۴۱ [OCR?]); khalifa of his father **Khwaja Salih Muhammad b. Abd al-Khaliq Uwaisi**, who himself took faiz from Muhkam al-Din; "بعد وفات اپنے باپ کے یہ مسند ارشاد پر بیٹھے"; two sons **Shaikh Shihab al-Din and Ghulam Uwais**, "موجود ہیں", living at the time of writing — **the Uwaisiya's third generation, succeeding father to son**. (pp. 226–227, folios 214–215)
- [PK] **Sohan Majzub Ajodhani Pakpattani / سوہن مجذوب اجودھنی پاکپٹنی** — entry no. 172; died "سنہ سات سو اٹھائیس ہجری", **728 AH**; **grave (not stated)**; "مرد صاحب حال و جذب و سکر", a Hindu converted by Baba Farid's grandson, who became majzub, and "کبھی دو دو تین ماہ کچھ نہیں کھاتا اور کبھی چار چار پانچ پانچ سیر طعام کھا جاتا"; a lime-eating miracle recorded. (pp. 236–237, folios 224–225)

### Kashmir

- **Muayyid al-Din Bulbul Shah Kashmiri / مویدالدین بلبل شاہ** — entry no. 138; died **727 AH**; buried in the city of Kashmir, and "مکان مسکن مدفن ان کا اب تک **لنگر بلبل شاہ** مشہور ہے"; per *Tawarikh-e Azami* his real name was **Sharaf al-Din**; came to Srinagar, gave Islam currency, and by his attention "راجہ کشمیر مسلمان ہوگیا" with all his officers; editor: a murid of **Shah Ni'mat Allah Farsi**, "جن کا تعلق سلسلہ **سہروردیہ** سے تھا", who came to Kashmir **from Turkestan with a thousand refugees** in Sahdeva's reign in fear of the Mongol attack. (pp. 202–203, folios 190–191)
- **Shaikh Nur al-Din Wali Kashmiri / شیخ نور الدین ولی کشمیری** — entry no. 140 (contents: printed p. 192); born **757**, died **842 AH**; "روضہ منورہ ملک کشمیر میں زیارت گاہ خلق اللہ ہے"; a quantified **twenty-six-year fast** — twelve years in a wilderness jungle without food or sleep on "تھوڑی سی کائی کی پتی", twelve years on one bowl of milk a day, two and a half on the water of boiled grain, "غرض چھبیس سال تک حضرت نے روٹی نہ کھائی"; bai'at with **Sayyid Mir Muhammad b. Sayyid Ali Hamdani**, then attendance on Mir Sayyid Husain Samani, Shaikh Baha al-Din, Shaikh **سلطان کملی [OCR?]** and Baba Haji Adham, receiving the title **Qutb al-Afaq**; the *rijal al-ghaib* and **Bibi Lal Ded** foretold him to his mother; **six khalifas** became guides after him; chronogram "ولی با صفا نور علیٰ نور **۸۴۲**". (pp. 204–205, folios 192–193)
- **Baba Zain al-Din Kashmiri / بابا زین الدین** — **d. 850 AH**; originally of **Kishtwar**, "**زیا سنگ [OCR?]** نام تھا", named Zain al-Din on becoming Muslim; one of Nur al-Din Wali's six khalifas. (p. 205, folio 193)
- **Bam al-Din / بام الدین** — buried in Kashmir; his name before Islam was "**لوہر سادی [OCR?]**" and "طبقہ **برہمن** سے نسبی تعلق تھا", a lineal connection with the Brahmin class; repented at the end of his life. (p. 205)
- **Baba Latif / بابا لطیف** — buried in Kashmir; pre-Islamic name "**لدی رینہ [OCR?]**". (p. 205)
- **Baba Nasir al-Din / بابا ناصر الدین** — "صحیح نام **نصر الدین** تھا"; attached himself in childhood and was buried "اپنے مرشد کے جوار میں", in his murshid's vicinity. (p. 205)
- **Baba Qiyam al-Din / بابا قیام الدین** — buried in Kashmir; "بحکم مرشد **پرگنہ دیلہ [OCR?]** میں سکونت اختیار کی". (p. 205)
- **Makhdoom Shaikh Hamza Kashmiri / مخدوم شیخ حمزہ کشمیری** — entry no. 116; **editor: born 900 AH**; died **984 AH**; "مزار ملک کشمیر میں ہے" — so 84 lunar years on the two figures; his dwelling was "**موضع تجراز [OCR?]، پرگنہ زینہ گیر**" and his station "پہاڑ کے ایک غار میں", a cave in the mountain; "چونکہ **مرشد ظاہری** ان کا کوئی نہ تھا" he sought leave from the Prophet's *ruhaniyat* and was told "تیرا مرشد خود تیرے پاس آئے گا", whereupon Sayyid Jamal al-Din Suhrawardi came from Delhi; reached *fana fi'l-rasul* by an **Uwaisi** nisbat; **Baba Daud Khaki wrote his manaqib in *Wird al-Muridin***; "اہل تشیع کشمیر آپ کے سخت مخالف تھے"; a *Sultani* on him printed Lahore 1923. (pp. 177–178, folios 175–176 as printed; p. 297)
- **Baba Daud Khaki Kashmiri Suhrawardi / بابا داؤد خاکی** — entry no. 117; died **994 AH** and "شہر **سری نگر** میں مدفون ہوئے" — **buried in Srinagar**; Hamza's best-loved murid; left the country when the **Chak sultans**, "جن کا مذہب شیعہ تھا", martyred **Qazi Musa Kashmiri**, declaring "جب تک کشمیر سے سلاطین چک کی سلطنت دور نہ ہو گی۔ ہم کشمیر میں نہ آئیں گے", and returned with **Qasim Khan Bahri and Akbar's army** — editor: Akbar attacked in **994 AH / 1585 CE** and "حضرت بابا داؤد خاکی اکبر بادشاہ کے لشکر کے ہمراہ کشمیر پہنچے اور اسی سال انتقال کیا"; works *Wird al-Muridin* (printed Lahore 1897), *Dastur al-Salikin*, *Qasida-e Jalaliya*, *Risala-e Ghusliya*. (pp. 178–179, folios 176–177 as printed; p. 297)
- **Baba Nasib al-Din Suhrawardi Kashmiri / بابا نصیب الدین** — entry no. 122; died **13 Muharram 1047 AH**, chronogram "وہو خیر الصالحین" = 1047; "مزار سرزمین کشمیر میں ہے"; "خلیفہ اعظم شیخ داؤد کشمیری کے"; "سوائے نان خشک کے کوئی چیز آپ کی غذا نہ تھی" and "نہ کبھی دیدہ حق بین خواب سے آشنا ہوئے"; his *Nur Nama* on the mashaikh of Kashmir survives in the Sherani collection. **He is also listed, impossibly, among Nur al-Din Wali's six khalifas — the editor catches it.** (pp. 183, 205, folio 181 as printed; p. 296)
- **Khwaja Muin al-Din Naqshbandi, son of Khwaja Khawand Mahmud Lahori / خواجہ معین الدین** — entry no. 87; died "بقول صاحب تواریخ" **2 Muharram 1085 AH** — **no grave stated**; settled in Kashmir "اس خانقاہ میں جو ان کے باپ کی تعمیر تھی", the khanqah his father built, appointed there by his father for the guidance of the people; "علمائے ظاہری بھی حضرت کے دل سے فرمانبردار تھے۔ ہر ایک **مقدمہ** میں جو حضرت فتویٰ دیتے۔ کسی کو انکار نہ ہوتا"; a student of Shaikh Abd al-Haq Muhaddith Dehlvi; ten works listed with shelfmarks, including *al-Murfiqat al-Qulub* and *Kanz al-Sa'adat* at the Punjab Public Library; **the editor corrects the nisba of the "تواریخ ڈوہری" the author cites** to Khwaja Muhammad Azam **Dida Mari**'s *Tarikh-e Kashmir Azami*. (pp. 136–138, folios 124–126; pp. 295, 305, 348–349)
- **Jiti Shah Majzub Kashmiri / جیتی شاہ مجذوب** — entry no. 178; died **981 AH** per *Tawarikh-e Azami*; "مزار کشمیر میں **شیخ ہروی رئیسی [OCR?]** کے مقبرہ کے اندر ہے"; read hearts at sight; frequented Hamza Kashmiri and Baba Daud, who "خلوت کر کے اس سے اسرار الٰہی کی تقریریں کرتے"; **the editor disputes the name** — "یہاں **سہو کتابت** معلوم ہوتا ہے۔ خواجہ محمد اعظم نے یہ نام **غیبی** تحریر کیا ہے"; the editor's Persian has him at rest "**بیرون قلعہ در ملکوٹہ [OCR?]**" and adds that after his death "ہر کہ قسم دروغ می خورد۔ در ساعت بسزای رسید". (p. 239, folio 227)
- **Shah Badi al-Din Majzub Kashmiri, famous as Badi Shah / بدیع الدین بدی شاہ** — entry no. 179; died "سال نو سو بانوے ہجری", **992 AH**; "مزار پُر انوار کشمیر میں ہے"; "سردی کے موسم میں جب برف برستی تو یہ جنگل میں تمام رات گذرانتا … سوائے ایک تہ بند کے کوئی اور کپڑا اس کے جسم پر نہ ہوتا"; said "کلمہ **ہمہ اوست** بر ملا" even in jazb, and a fatwa followed. (pp. 239–240, folios 227–228)
- **Khwaja Daud Majzub Kashmiri / خواجہ داؤد مجذوب** — entry no. 180; died to end the **plague of 1026**; editor's Persian has him send word to **Khwaja Habib Allah Naushehri and Mir Yusuf Qadiri b. Mir Nazuk** that he would make himself a sacrifice, and die shortly after, the epidemic lifting, buried "در **محلہ لودہ گیر [OCR?]**"; "کبھی اس نے اپنے ارادے سے لقمہ منہ میں نہ رکھا", eating only what was put in his mouth, his station "جنگل ویرانے میں". (p. 240, folio 228)
- **Shaikh Ahmad Shah Kashmiri "تازہ بلی [OCR?]"** — entry no. 167; died **1277 AH**; **mazar in Kashmir**; "اس زمانہ کے اولیا میں سے قطب وقت"; resident in the **city of Srinagar**; **bai'at in four orders — Suhrawardi, Qadiri, Chishti and Naqshbandi — at the hand of Adam Shah, famous as اک شاہ [OCR?]**; a cow-slaughter case recorded; Maulvi Amanat Ali came from Hindustan to take faiz from him. (pp. 124, 233–234, folios 221–222)
- **Bibi Lalla Kashmiri, "عارفہ کاملہ", called لل دیوی / Lal Ded** — entry no. 198; **no grave** — per *Tawarikh-e Azami* "جسم عنصری اس کا مانند روح آسمان کو پرواز کر گیا اور طرفۃ العین میں لوگوں کی نظر سے غائب ہوگیا اور یہ واقعہ **سات سو چھپن** میں وقوع میں آیا", 756; of the city of Srinagar, her parents attached to Raja **نرنجن دیو [OCR?]**; became Muslim with the raja at Bulbul Shah's hand, "شیخ اس کو **اپنی لڑکی** کہتے تھے"; married, neglected the household for worship, and her husband freed her; the frozen water, the new spring and the oven; "ایسی مجذوب ہوئی کہ عورت تک بھی اس کو پروا نہ تھی"; the persons index carries "للّہ کشمیری بی بی | ۲۴۰". (pp. 252–253, folios 240–241; p. 327)
- **Baba Haji Adham / بابا حاجی ادھم** — "بعض نے **ادہمی** بھی لکھا ہے", with a lineal connection to Sultan Ibrahim Adham; **buried outside the Shahi Qila of Kashmir**. (p. 204, folio 192)
- **Sayyid Ahmad Samani b. Kamal al-Din b. Mahmud** — buried at **نندو فتکدل [OCR?]**; a murid of Mir Sayyid Muhammad Hamdani and author of *Tanwir al-Siraj*, a commentary on the *Siraji*. (p. 204)
- **Mir Sayyid Muhammad b. Amir Kabir Sayyid Ali Hamdani** — came to Kashmir in **Sultan Sikandar's** time and "کشمیر میں **خانقاہ و مدرسہ قائم کر کے** تبلیغ و تدریس کا بہت کام کیا". (p. 204)
- Also named: **Shaikh Baha al-Din Kashmiri**, brother in the path of Nur al-Din (p. 204) · **Mir Sayyid Husain Samani** (p. 204) · **Qazi Musa Kashmiri**, martyred by the Chak sultans — the event that empties Kashmir of Baba Daud (pp. 178–179) · three more **Rishis at folio 171** in the persons index — "شکر الدین رشی", "لطف الدین رشی", "لطیف الدین رشی", with "رجب الدین رشتی [OCR?]" and "نوروز [OCR?] رشی بابا" (pp. 318, 327, 331–332) · **Shaikh Muhammad Murad Kashmiri (d. 1718)**, the subject of an unpublished book by the editor (p. 307).

### Delhi

- **Khwaja Qutb al-Din Bakhtiar Kaki Aushi Dehlvi / خواجہ قطب الدین بختیار کاکی** — entry no. 42 (contents: printed p. 73); died **14 Rabi al-Awwal ۶۳۴ [OCR?] AH**, **in the very midst of a sama assembly** — **editor: 14 Rabi al-Awwal 633 AH** (*Akhbar al-Akhyar* 25); of the sadat of **Aush**, a qasba in Transoxiana which the editor places in **Farghana, south-east of Andijan**; genealogy to Imam Jafar Sadiq; his father died in his childhood and "والدہ ماجدہ نے ان کو پڑھایا اور تربیت کی"; his pir "ان کو دہلی کو مامور کیا"; editor: he met Khwaja Muin al-Din and received the khirqa in the mosque of Imam Abu al-Laith Samarqandi **in Baghdad** (*Sair al-Auliya* 48); seven sources listed. (pp. 16–19, 85–86, folios 73–74)
- **Sultan al-Mashaikh Nizam al-Din Badauni Dehlvi / سلطان المشائخ نظام الدین اولیاء** — entry no. 47 (contents: printed p. 82); born at Badayun **634 AH**; died **Wednesday 18 Rabi al-Akhir 725 AH**, ۷۲۵ھ in the chronogram margin; "روضہ مبارک شہر دہلی میں زیارت گاہ خلق ہے"; real name Muhammad b. Ahmad Daniyal b. Ali Bukhari; both grandfathers came from Bukhara and "چند سال **لاہور** میں سکونت رکھی" before Badayun; orphaned at five and raised by his mother **Bibi Zulaikha**; through Shaikh Najib al-Din Mutawakkil he came to Baba Farid; "لاکھوں آدمی طالبان حق خدمت میں حاضر ہوئے". (pp. 16–19, 94–96, folios 82–84)
- **Khwaja Amir Khusrau, poet, Chishti of Delhi / خواجہ امیر خسرو چشتی** — entry no. 48 (contents: printed p. 84); died **Wednesday 18 Shawwal 725**, six months after his pir; "مزار گوہر بار سلطان المشائخ کے روضہ کے پاس ہے"; real name **Abu al-Hasan**, takhallus Khusrau; father **Amir Saif al-Din**, "ستر سال کی عمر میں کفار کے جہاد میں شہید ہوا"; maternal grandfather **Amir Imad al-Mulk**; brother **Aʿazz al-Din Ali**; "تصانیف ان کی ایک کم سو مشہور ہیں" — ninety-nine famous works, and Persian verses "پانچ لاکھ سے کم اور چار لاکھ سے زیادہ" with countless Hindi verses; absent on the Bengal campaign at his pir's death, he came back, put on black and sat by the grave "بے خور و خواب … با سینہ بریاں و چشم گریاں" for six months until he died. (pp. 16–19, 96–97, folios 84–85)
- **Shaikh Nasir al-Din Mahmud Audhi, called Chiragh-e Dehli / چراغ دہلی** — entry no. 49; died **18 Ramadan, Friday, 757 AH**; **mazar in the city of Delhi**; great friend and true khalifa of Nizam al-Din; lineage to Imam Hasan, grandfather Sayyid Abd al-Latif, father Sayyid Yahya; "بعد وفات سلطان المشائخ کے **ولایت خطہ دہلی** کی ان کو ملی". (pp. 97–98, folios 85–86)
- **Shaikh Sayyid Haji Abd al-Wahhab Bukhari Dehlvi / سید حاجی عبدالوہاب بخاری** — entry no. 114; died **932 AH**, "اور شیخ حاجی مادہ تاریخ ہے" — the chronogram of the year; of the progeny of Sayyid Jalal al-Din Mir Surkh Bukhari Uchi; first lived in **Multan**, then Hajj and the Haramain, then Delhi; **Sultan Sikandar Lodhi was his murid**; wrote a tafsir of the whole Quran read as praise of the Prophet, "ہر ایک آیت کے ایسے معنی بیان کیے ہیں کہ ان سے پیغمبر خدا … کی مدح ظاہر ہوتی ہے", begun **Rabi al-Thani 915 AH** and finished **17 Shawwal 915 AH**; editor, from a Persian *Tazkirat al-Abrar* seen at Hyderabad, Deccan: **born 869 AH**, mother Fatima bint Qutb al-Din, drew faiz at Delhi from Shaikh Abdullah b. Yusuf Qurashi Multani, **died at Delhi 932 AH aged 63 and was buried near the mazar of Shaikh Abdullah Qureshi** — the year Babur entered Delhi after Panipat (932 AH/1526 CE). **See Doubts on the two pirs.** (p. 176, folio 174 as printed; pp. 349–350)
- **Sayyid Jamal al-Din Suhrawardi Dehlvi / سید جمال الدین سہروردی** — entry no. 115; died **948 AH**; full brother, murid and khalifa of Abd al-Wahhab Bukhari; by an inward sign he went from Hind to **Kashmir**, where thousands took his bai'at, then returned to Hindustan; a book *Jamal-e Irfan* was written on his marvels; editor: his bai'at chain reaches **Makhdoom Jahaniyan through six intermediaries**, he worked in Kashmir "آخری سلاطین کشمیر کے عہد میں", and "حضرت مخدوم حمزہ کشمیری کی تربیت آپ کی توجہ کا نتیجہ ہے". (p. 177, folio 175 as printed)
- **Khwaja Muhammad Baqi, famous as Baqi Billah Naqshbandi Dehlvi / خواجہ باقی باللہ** — entry no. 82 (contents: printed p. 115); died **Monday the 25th [OCR?] of Jumada al-Thani 1012 AH, aged forty**; mazar in the city of Delhi, a place of pilgrimage; outward bai'at from **Khwaja Muhammad Khwajgi امکنگی [OCR?]** but his inward nisbat with Baha al-Din Naqshband, drawing much faiz from the ruhaniyat of Khwaja Ubaid Allah Ahrar; studied fiqh, hadith and tafsir at **Kabul**; "نماز عشا کے بعد نماز تہجد تک **دو ختم قرآن شریف** کے کرتے۔ بعد تہجد صبح تک **اکیس مرتبہ سورۃ یٰسین** پڑھنا آپ کا روزمرہ کا ورد تھا"; correspondent of Shaikh Abd al-Haq Muhaddith Dehlvi. (pp. 16–19, 127–128, folios 115–116)
- **Maulana Fakhr al-Din Fakhr-e Jahan Shahjahanabadi Chishti / فخر الدین فخر جہاں** — entry no. 70; born **1126**; died 7 Jumada al-Thani "**ایک ہزار ایک صد نونوے [OCR?]**"; "مزار گوہر بار دہلی میں **اندرون احاطہ مزار خواجہ قطب الدین بختیار کاکی** کے ہے" — inside the enclosure of Bakhtiar Kaki's mazar; chief among the khalifas of **Shaikh Nizam al-Din Aurangabadi**, his own father; paternal genealogy to Shihab al-Din Suhrawardi, maternal to Gesu Daraz; "خاندان چشت اہل بہشت میں … زمانہ اخیر کا قطب"; **Nawab Nizam al-Mulk Ghazi al-Din Khan, Nazim of Hyderabad**, recorded "بے شمار کرامتیں" of him in *Manaqib-e Fakhriya*, printed at the Matba Mujtabai Delhi, with an Urdu translation by Mir Dard Kakorvi, Karachi 1961; the editor corrects the day. (pp. 116–118, folios 104–106)
- **Shah Shams al-Din Habib Allah, addressed as Mirza Jan-e Janan / مرزا مظہر جان جاناں** — entry no. 93 (contents: printed p. 131); born **11 Ramadan, Friday, 1111 AH**; died **9 Muharram 1195 AH, aged eighty-four**, **assassinated**; of the **Alavi sayyids**, twenty-eight intermediaries to Imam Muhammad b. Hanif; "ان کے شاہان دہلی کے دربار میں **امیر کبیر** تھے۔ قرابت بھی سلاطین **تیموریہ** کے ساتھ رکھتے تھے" — and "امیر عبدالسبحان **نواسہ اکبر بادشاہ** کا ان کا دادا تھا", **which the editor denies**; father **Mirza Jan Turk** renounced wealth for faqiri and became a murid of Shah Abd al-Rahman Qadiri; orphaned at sixteen, murid of **Sayyid Nur Muhammad Badayuni**, then faiz from **Haji Muhammad Afzal (d. 1146 AH)**, **Hafiz Sa'd Allah (d. 11 Shawwal 1152 AH)** and **Khwaja Muhammad Abid**; editor: "ایک بلند پایہ ادیب اور شاعر", diwan and bayaz printed as ***Kharita-e Jawahir***, with an eleven-item bibliography including a British Museum manuscript and Khaliq Anjum's thesis. (pp. 16–19, 143–145, folios 131–133)
- **Shah Abdullah, addressed as Sayyid Ghulam Ali Shah Naqshbandi Mujaddidi Dehlvi / شاہ غلام علی دہلوی** — entry no. 94; died **22 Safar 1240 AH**, chronogram ۱۲۴۰ھ; true khalifa and janishin of Mirza Jan-e Janan Shahid; father **Sayyid Abd al-Latif**, who lived at qasba **روتالہ [OCR?]** and was a murid of Shah Nasir al-Din Qadiri; "تمام عمر دہلی میں رہ کر بہدایت خلق مصروف رہے" and "گویا اخیر زمانہ میں **ہند کی ولایت** ان کی ذات پر ختم ہوئی"; eight works named; his khalifa **Maulana Khalid Kurdi** "عرب [اور ترکستان] میں … سارے عرب کو متاثر کیا". (pp. 145–147, folios 133–135)
- **Shah Abu Said Mujaddidi Dehlvi / شاہ ابوسعید مجددی** — entry no. 95; died at **Tonk** on the day of Eid, a Tuesday, "سنہ ایک ہزار دو سو پچاس", **1250, aged fifty-five**, on his way home from the Hajj (the editor dates the journey **1249 AH**), and "شاہ عبد الغنی حضرت کے صاحبزادے ان کی نعش کو **دہلی** میں لائے اور شاہ غلام علیؒ و مرزا جان جاناں کے مزار کی **قربت میں دفن** کیا"; a direct Mujaddidi descendant — "صفی القدر بن عزیز القدر بن محمد عیسیٰ بن محمد سیف الدین بن خواجہ محمد معصوم بن شیخ احمد مجدد الف ثانیؒ" — with those ancestors dated **Safi al-Qadr d. 1236 AH, buried at Lucknow; Muhammad Aziz al-Qadr d. 1178 AH [OCR?]; Muhammad Isa d. 1153 AH**; learning from **Mufti Sharaf al-Din Dehlvi** and Maulana Rafi al-Din, son of Shah Wali Allah; editor: through him the Mujaddidiya was propagated in Hindustan, **Arabia and Turkey**, and "مجددی سلسلہ کی **آخری فعال خانقاہوں** میں سے آپ کی خانقاہ نے بہت سے نمایاں کام سرانجام دیے"; his son **Shah Abd al-Ghani**, dated by the editor **1235–1296 AH**, "عالم اور معروف مدرس". (pp. 147–148, folios 135–136)
- **Shaikh Abd al-Haq Muhaddith Dehlvi / شیخ عبدالحق محدث دہلوی** — entry no. 141; died **1051 AH** in the text, mazar in **Delhi** — **the editor corrects the year and locates and dates the tomb at the Hauz-e Shamsi**, built by Nawab Mahabat Khan; murid of **Musa Pak Shahid Gilani Multani** from 6 Shawwal 985 AH/1577 CE, with an **Uwaisi** nisbat to Ghaus al-Azam, and after Musa Pak's death a khirqa from **Shaikh Abd al-Wahhab Muttaqi**, khalifa of Shaikh Ali Muttaqi; the Hijaz journey of **996–999 AH** and the message that sent him home — "دہلی واپس جانا چاہیے کیونکہ دہلی تمہاری جدائی میں نالاں ہے"; works the *Sharh Mishkat* (Arabic and Persian), *Sirat al-Mustaqim*, ***Akhbar al-Akhyar*** (Meerut 1278 AH), *Sharh Futuh al-Ghaib*, *Jazb al-Qulub*, *Kitab al-Makatib wal-Rasail*, *Risala Tahqiq al-Ishara*; the book's **most-cited authority, twenty-three folios in the index**. (pp. 128, 137, 205–207, folios 193–195; pp. 298, 321)
- **Shah Wali Allah Muhaddith Dehlvi / شاہ ولی اللہ محدث دہلوی** — entry no. 149 (contents: printed p. 205); died **1180 AH** in the text; mazar in **Delhi** — **the editor corrects both the genre and the year**; editor: born **4 Shawwal 1114 AH**, son of **Shah Abd al-Rahim**, chronogrammatic name "عظیم الدین", Hajj **1143–1145 AH**, meeting Haji Muhammad Saeed at Lahore in 1143 AH, "تقریباً ۶۰ تصانیف دریافت ہو چکی ہیں", and "اہل حدیث علماء نے بھی چند کتابیں آپ کی طرف منسوب کر دی ہیں"; **an ijazat-nama in his own hand is reproduced in facsimile** from the Khuda Bakhsh Library, Patna, and a sixteen-link **Sahih Bukhari isnad** from Bukhari down to him is printed with a seal. (pp. 16–19, 217–218, 225, folios 205–206, 213)
- **Maulana Shah Abd al-Aziz Dehlvi / شاہ عبد العزیز دہلوی** — entry no. 156; died **1239 AH**; mazar in **Delhi**; "دہلی میں امام المحدثین مقتدائے مفسرین" and "اگر خاتم العلما کہا جائے تو درست و بجا ہے"; works *Sair al-Shahadatain*, *Bustan al-Muhaddithin*, *Tafsir Fath al-Aziz*, *Tuhfa Ithna Ashariya*; editor: born **the night of Friday, 25 Ramazan 1159 AH**, chronogrammatic name "غلام حلیم", with thirteen sources including an article on **a rare composition of his on music**; a **Bukhari sanad in his own hand and seal** is reproduced, its grantee named "مولوی غلام محی الدین" — see Doubts. (pp. 223–226, folios 211–214)
- **Maulana Abd al-Qadir b. Wali Allah Muhaddith Dehlvi / مولانا عبدالقادر** — entry no. 158; died **1242 AH**; "کمال فصاحت و بلاغت ترجمہ تفسیر فتح الرحمان **ہندی زبان** میں کیا کہ مقبول و مطبوع خاص و عام ہے۔ **کسی کو اس پر جائے اعتراض نہیں**"; editor: born **1167 AH / 1753 CE**, his memorial the Quran translation with brief tafsir named ***Mauzih-e Quran***, and "موضح القرآن کے نام سے جو تفسیر آپ کی طرف منسوب کی گئی ہے، وہ **علمائے اہل حدیث** کا کارنامہ ہے". (p. 227, folio 215)
- **Maulana Muhammad Ishaq Dehlvi / مولانا محمد اسحاق دہلوی** — entry no. 162; died **1262 AH**; **grandson (نواسے) of Shah Abd al-Aziz**; editor: son of Shaikh Muhammad Afzal Faruqi, **janishin** of Shah Abd al-Aziz, who after his death "مسلمانان ہند و پاکستان کی **مذہبی قیادت** سنبھالی", and in "**۱۲۵۷ھ / ۱۸۴۱ء [OCR?]** میں … **حجاز** کو ہجرت کر گئے"; several of his students took part as ulama in the **war of independence of 1857** — Mufti Inayat Ahmad Kakorvi, Sadr Amin of Bareilly; Maulana Abd al-Jalil **کوٹلی علی گڑھی [OCR?]**; Mufti Sadr al-Din Azurda; Shah Abu Saeed Mujaddidi; he translated the *Mishkat* into **Urdu**. (p. 230, folio 218)
- **Hakim Sarmad Dehlvi Maqtul / حکیم سرمد دہلوی مقتول** — entry no. 143; **killed 1070 AH**; grave in **Delhi**; "پہلے **یہودی مشرب** تھا۔ کتاب توریت کمال شوق سے پڑھا کرتا۔ من بعد مشرف با اسلام ہوا" — first of the Jewish persuasion, then Muslim, famous in **دہلی [OCR?]** for learning; then "یہ ایک **ہندو بچہ** پر عاشق ہوا" and, "بحکم **المجاز قنطرۃ الحقیقت**", passed from that to absorption in the real Beloved; "سر و پا برہنہ مکشوف العورت کبھی بازاروں میں پھرا کرتا"; **executed under Aurangzeb Alamgir — the book's only Jewish convert and its only saint executed by a court.** (pp. 209, 340, folio 197)
- **Sayyid Makhdum Mir Jahan Sadr Jahan / سید مخدوم میر جہاں صدر جہاں** — entry no. ۲۲۳; died "سنہ ایک ہزار ایک سو بیاسی", **1182** (era not stated); mazar "شہر دہلی کے اندر **محلہ روشن پورہ** میں"; janishin of Makhdum Shah Alam; held "**اجازت ارشاد**" in the Qadiriya, Chishtiya and Naqshbandiya; "مدت مدید تک حضرت نے خاص شہر دہلی میں ہنگامہ مشیخت گرم رکھا"; the **sajjada nashin** is **Shah Baha al-Din alias Abdullah Shah, takhallus "Bashir"**, "جن کی زیارت سے مؤلف کتاب بھی بہرہ یاب ہوا ہے" — visited by the author himself. (pp. 268–269, folios 256–257)
- **Bibi Sara / بی بی سارہ** — entry no. 192; died "سنہ چھ سو اڑتیس ہجری", **638 AH** — **editor: "اخبار الاخیار (۲۷۶) میں بی بی سارہ کا سال وفات مرقوم نہیں ہے"**, the source the author is using gives no year; "مزار پُر انوار دہلی میں **مُتصل مقبرہ خواجہ قطب الدین بختیار کاکی** کے ہے"; mother of **Shaikh Nizam al-Din Abu al-Muayyid Dehlvi**; "حافظہ و عابدہ و زاہدہ و صائمہ"; a rain-prayer recorded. (pp. 249–250, folios 237–238)
- **Bibi Fatima Sam Dehlavi / بی بی فاطمہ سام** — entry no. 193; died "سنہ چھ سو تینتالیس", **643**; **mazar in Delhi**; "عورات صالحات و قانتات و عارفات شہر دہلی سے"; "**سلطان المشائخ** اکثر اوقات اس بی بی کے مزار پر جا کر مشغول بمراقبہ رہتے تھے"; "یہ بی بی صاحب علم و فضل و **شاعرہ** بھی تھی", with one verse quoted; **editor, quoting Abd al-Haq: "ہر دو لفظ غلط است نام ایشاں بی بی فاطمہ سام است"** — both "Bibi Sham" and "Bibi Saima" are wrong. (p. 250, folio 238)
- **Bibi Zulaikha / بی بی زلیخا** — entry no. 195 (contents: printed p. 239); died "سال چھ سو اڑتالیس", **648**; "مزار متصل **مقبرہ شیخ نجیب الدین متوکل** کے ہے"; mother of **Nizam al-Din Auliya**, who brought him up after his father's death and whose grave he visited; her dream-knowledge recorded. (pp. 96, 251–252, folios 239–240)
- **Bibi Awliya / بی بی اولیا** — entry no. 196; died "چھ سو پچپن ہجری", **655 AH**; **grave (not stated)**; "یہ **بی بی پاک دامن** دہلی میں رہتی تھی۔ کمال بزرگ و صاحب عبادت تھی"; a forty-day chilla recorded; "**سلطان محمد تغلق** بادشاہ دہلی اس کا کمال معتقد تھا"; editor: her descendants were still resident in Delhi when *Akhbar al-Akhyar* was compiled in 999 AH, one of them **Shaikh Ahmad Marvi**. **The epithet "بی بی پاک دامن" applied to a Delhi woman — see Doubts.** (p. 252, folio 240)
- **Mian Maruf Majzub Dehlvi / میاں معروف مجذوب دہلوی** — entry no. 174; **vanished, no grave** — "جب **شیر شاہ** بادشاہ نے قلعہ دہلی کو سال **نو سو سینتالیس** میں ویران کیا تو یہ مجذوب گم ہو گیا۔ نہ معلوم کہاں گیا", and Abd al-Haq's Persian: "ہیچ نشانی از و پیدا نشد"; lived "خواجہ قطب الدین بختیار کے مزار کے پاس قدیمی گنبد میں جو **مقبرہ شیخ برہان الدین بلخی** کے پاس ہے"; deeply learned; the author's **947** and the editor's **948 AH/1541 CE** refer to different acts — destruction and founding. (p. 237, folio 225)
- **Shaikh Hasan Lodla Majzub / شیخ حسن لودلہ مجذوب** — entry no. 175; died "سال نو سو چونسٹھ", **964**, "اور قبر دہلی میں ہے" — editor, in Persian: "قبر او **در بازار دہلی نزدیک روضہ خواص خان** است", near the rauza of **Khawas Khan**, a client of Sher Shah put to death by Salim Shah b. Sher Shah in **958**; son of a Delhi amir, "اکثر سراپا برہنہ پھرا کرتا"; bilocation recorded; he reappears in Lahore in entry no. 177. (p. 238, folio 226)
- **Shaikh Muhammad Abid Naqshbandi Mujaddidi, named by the editor Shaikh Muhammad Abid Hanafi Naqshbandi Sunami / شیخ محمد عابد سنامی** — entry no. 92; died **18 Ramadan 1160 AH "القدس [OCR?]"** — the editor reports a rival year in the footnote, and in the appendix settles it: Shah Ghulam Ali gives **1160 AH**, "جو مفتی صاحب کے عین مطابق ہے اس لئے **فقیر محمد جہلمی** کا مندرجہ سنہ **۱۱۶۶ھ** درست نہیں ہے"; "مزار مبارک باغ کے سامنے **آزاد پور دہلی** کے قریب ہے"; khalifa, janishin and **qaim-maqam** of Abd al-Ahad; paternal nisbat to Abu Bakr Siddiq; "بکمال اشتیاق **پا پیادہ** ہند سے روانہ ہو کر حرمین الشریفین میں پہنچے" — reached the Haramain **on foot**; Mirza Mazhar Jan-e Janan took inward benefit from him; the nisba is from **Sunam**, "قصبہ ایست از نواح سہرند". (pp. 142–143, folios 130–131; p. 310)
- **Mufti Sharaf al-Din Rampuri Hanafi / مفتی شرف الدین رام پوری** — died **1260 AH**; among the famous teachers of Hindustan and "**پنجاب سے رام پور گئے تھے**"; great ulama were his students; *Siraj al-Mizan*, *Sharh al-Sullam*, *Fatawa-e Fiqh* and many risalas. (p. 147, folio 135)
- Also named: **Shaikh Najib al-Din Mutawakkil**, by whose maqbara Bibi Zulaikha lies and through whom Nizam al-Din reached Baba Farid (pp. 94, 251) · **Maulana Rafi al-Din**, son of Shah Wali Allah (p. 148) · **Shaikh Nizam al-Din Abu al-Muayyid Dehlvi**, Bibi Sara's son (p. 249) · **Gangaram Pandit Dehlvi**, appointed to "دیوانی کا کام" at the darbar (p. 255).

### Sirhind, Panipat, Thanesar, Kaithal and Gangoh

- **Shaikh Ahmad Mujaddid Alf-e Thani Faruqi Kabuli Sirhindi / شیخ احمد مجدد الف ثانی** — entry no. 83 (contents: printed p. 117); born "باقوال صحیح" **971 AH**; died **Tuesday at dawn on the last day (سلخ) of Safar, in the year 1035**, aged sixty-three, chronogram carrying ۱۰۳۵; **mazar at Sirhind** — **the editor overturns the death year**; titled Ghaus al-Alamin, Qutb al-Aqtab, "دافع بدع و ضلالت"; paternal genealogy to Umar Faruq through **twenty-eight** intermediaries — **the appendix raises this to thirty-two on Maulana Abul Hasan Faruqi's authority**, holding that *Zubdat al-Maqamat* and *Hazarat al-Quds* wrote only twenty-eight, "جو کتب انساب کے مطابق درست نہیں ہیں"; **bai'at in four silsilas**; "اگرچہ علمائے ظاہر ہی نے حضرت کی عداوت پر کمر بستہ رکھی، مگر خدا کی امداد اور پیران عظام کی حمایت سے فتح یاب نہ ہوئے"; a letter of his is reproduced in facsimile. (pp. 16–19, 129–130, folios 117–118; pp. 284, 309)
- **Shaikh Muhammad Saeed Naqshbandi Mujaddidi / شیخ محمد سعید** — entry no. 85; died **1070 AH**, age eighty; **mazar at Sirhind** — editor: **born 1005 AH, died 27 Jumada al-Thani 1070 AH** (*Rauzat al-Qayyumiya* 1/291–192 [OCR?]); **second son** of the Mujaddid; his maktubat published from Lahore by the late Hakim Abd al-Majid Saifi; *Taliqat-e Mishkat al-Masabih* noticed by Kishmi; **eight sons and five daughters**, the sons named Shah Abdullah, Lutf Allah, Maulvi Farrukh Shah, Sa'd al-Din, Shaikh Abd al-Ahad Wahdat, Khalil al-Rahman, Muhammad Yaqub, Muhammad Taqi. **See Doubts on the age.** (pp. 134–135, folios 122–123)
- **Shaikh Muhammad Masum Naqshbandi Mujaddidi Sirhindi / خواجہ محمد معصوم** — entry no. 86; born **1009**, died **1080** — **editor: 1079 AH correct**; "فرزند میانگی", the **middle son** of the Mujaddid, Qutb al-Mashaikh and his father's janishin; at sixteen he "دستار فضیلت کی باندھی"; refused Aurangzeb's patronage; three lakh murids; travelled to the **Haramain**; *Maktubat-e Masumiya* in **three volumes** and *Azkar-e Masumiya*. (pp. 135–136, folios 123–124)
- **Shaikh Saif al-Din b. Shaikh Muhammad Masum b. the Mujaddid / خواجہ سیف الدین** — entry no. 88; died **1098 AH**; **mazar at Sirhind** — **the editor moves the date and puts him inside the imperial fort**; janishin of his father, titled **Muhi al-Sunnat** for his scrupulousness; "جو کوئی شخص کفار یا فساق و فجار میں سے ان کے روبرو آتا تائب ہو جاتا" and "دنیا داروں کی صحبت سے ان کو کمال نفرت تھی"; his khanqah's kitchen recorded; *Maktubat-e Saifiya* cited for letters to Asad Allah Wazirabadi. (pp. 138–139, folios 126–127; p. 213)
- **Shaikh Abd al-Ahad b. Shaikh Muhammad Saeed b. the Mujaddid / شیخ عبدالاحد وحدت** — entry no. 91; died **1142 AH [OCR?]** — **the editor renames him and moves the year sixteen years**; janishin and khalifa of his own father, seated on the masnad of hidayat and irshad after him; "سلسلہ مجددیہ ان کی ذات بابرکات سے تمام زمانہ میں پھیل گیا۔ **خلفا ان کے تمام دور دراز ملکوں سے مکہ مدینہ میں جا کر** مصروف بہدایت خلق ہوئے"; "خوارق و کرامت کے اظہار میں حضرت کو کمال انکار تھا"; a manuscript Persian diwan of his survives at the **Bengal Asiatic Society, Calcutta**, no. ۱۴۷۶ [OCR?]. Burial (not stated). (pp. 141–142, folios 129–130; p. 310)
- **Shaikh Adam Banuri / شیخ آدم بنوری** — **d. 1054 AH** in one of the editor's footnotes and **1052 AH** in a second footnote of the same entry; "خلیفہ اعظم حضرت مجدد الف ثانیؒ"; "آپ کی مذہبی سرگرمیوں کی وجہ سے **شاہجہان نے آپ کو ملک بدر کر دیا** تھا۔ چنانچہ آپ عرب شریف میں رہے اور یہیں آپ کا مزار **جنت البقیع** میں ہے"; walked from Banur to Lahore for Shaikh Tahir; works *Khulasat al-Maarif* (a two-volume ms of 1037 AH), *Nazm al-Nikat*, *Nikat al-Asrar*, *Wuzuh al-Mazahib*; his khalifa **Maulana Muhammad Amin Badakhshi** wrote a bulky *Nataij al-Haramain* on him, with manuscripts at Peshawar and Karachi. (pp. 59, 139–140, folios 127–128; pp. 292, 295)
- **Khwaja Abd al-Khaliq Sirhindi** — a murid of Shaikh Adam Banuri, named by the editor as the author of the *Tazkira-e Adamiya* the text cites anonymously. (p. 134, folio 122)
- **Mulla Badr al-Din Mujaddidi** — narrator of the betel-leaf miracle, identified by the editor as "مصنف **حضرات القدس** اور **وصال احمدی**". (p. 134, folio 122)
- **Sayyid Nur Muhammad Badayuni / سید نور محمد بدایونی** — entry no. 90; died **11 Zi al-Qada 1135**; the text says **mazar at Sirhind, which the editor corrects**; greatest khalifa of Saif al-Din; istighraq and intoxication dominated him; a Shia bai'at episode recorded; editor: "اجل عالم اور پابند شرع صوفی" who "ہر وقت کتاب و سنن اپنے پاس رکھتے" — "ایک مرتبہ بیت الخلا میں داخل ہوتے ہوئے **راست پاؤں پہلے** رکھ دیا تو **تین روز** تک سیر باطنی بند رہا"; foremost of those who took faiz from him was **Mirza Mazhar Jan-e Janan**, "جن سے سارے عالم اسلام میں مجددی سلوک کا عام رواج ہوا". (pp. 140–141, folios 128–129)
- **Shah Sikandar Kaithali / شاہ سکندر کیتھلی** — **appendix: died 1023 AH**, "شاہ کمال کیتھلی کے **پوتے** اور شاہ عماد الدین کے فرزند تھے۔ مزار **کیتھل شریف** میں مرجع خلائق ہے", his mazar at Kaithal Sharif a resort of the people, on three manuscripts held at the Khanqah Sharif, Kaithal; in the text he is Shaikh Tahir's first pir, named "شاہ اسکندر بن **پیرہ شاہ کمال کیتھلی**" in the footnote's Persian, and the Mujaddid's Qadiri chain runs through him. (pp. 59, 129, folios 47, 117; p. 309)
- **Shah Kamal Kaithali / شاہ کمال کیتھلی** — dates (not stated) beyond being Shah Sikandar's grandfather; **this is a name the archive also carries, for Lahore — see Doubts; the appendix is explicit that the Kaithal figure is a different man.** (pp. 129, 309)
- **Shaikh Shams al-Din Turk Panipati / شیخ شمس الدین ترک پانی پتی** — entry no. 45; died **715 AH**; **mazar at Panipat**; khalifa of Sabir Kaliyari, sent by him to Panipat; originally of **Turkestan**, his grandfather **Shaikh Ahmad Yasawi** among the famous awliya; genealogy joining Imam Muhammad Hanif, the family called **Alvi Qureshi**. (p. 93, folio 81 as printed)
- **Shaikh Sharaf al-Din Bu Ali Qalandar Panipati / بو علی قلندر پانی پتی** — entry no. 46; died **13 Ramadan 724 AH**; genealogy to Imam Azam Kufi, spiritual chain through Shaikh Ashiq-e Khuda, Imam al-Din Abdal and Shaikh Badr al-Din Ghaznavi to Qutb al-Din Bakhtiar Kaki; "حضرت کا طریق **مجذوبانہ قلندرانہ** اور کلام آپ کا **توحیدانہ** تھا"; **the editor holds his affiliation unknown and one attributed work spurious.** (pp. 93–94, folios 81–82 as printed)
- **Shaikh Jalal al-Din Mahmud Panipati Gazruni, called Kabir al-Auliya by the editor / جلال الدین محمود پانی پتی** — entry no. 50; died **765 AH**; real name Muhammad b. Mahmud; genealogy to Uthman; murid and khirqa-holder of Shams al-Din Turk; jazb so overcame him that "at prayer his attendants called حق حق حق loudly in his ear to bring him round"; **forty perfect awliya were his khalifas, "جن سے علیحدہ علیحدہ سلسلے جاری ہوئے"**; *Zad al-Abrar* among his works; his kitchen and offerings recorded. (pp. 98–99, folios 86–87)
- **Khwaja Ala al-Din Ali Ahmad Sabir Kaliyari / صابر کلیری** — entry no. 44; died "in the year six hundred and ninety", **690**, in the state of sama; **Khwaja Shams al-Din, then his only attendant, buried him "اور روضہ مکلف بنوایا جو اب تک زیارت گاہ خلق ہے"** at **Kaliyar**; khalifa and, the text says, sister's son and son-in-law of Baba Farid; twelve years on tree leaves in his pir's kitchen; the quarrel with Khwaja Jamal al-Din at Hansi, the lit finger, the torn farman and "تو نے میرے فرمان کو پھاڑ ڈالا ہے۔ میں نے تیری ولایت کو پھاڑ ڈالا"; at Kaliyar, removed by the arm from the qazi's prayer-place, he said "قاضی سے بڑھ کر رتبہ قطب کا ہے اور ہم اس سرزمین کے قطب ہیں". (pp. 90–93, folios 78–81 as printed)
- **Khwaja Jamal al-Din Hansvi / خواجہ جمال الدین ہانسوی** — dates (not stated); holder of the counter-seal on Baba Farid's farmans, who came two miles out of Hansi to meet Sabir, walked on foot beside his palanquin and made him imam at the evening prayer. (pp. 90–91)
- **Shaikh Abd al-Quddus Gangohi / شیخ عبدالقدُّوس گنگوہی** — entry no. 52 (contents: printed p. 88); died **945 AH**; **mazar at Gangoh (5)[OCR?]**; great khalifa of Shaikh Muhammad b. Shaikh Arif b. Shaikh Ahmad Abd al-Haq Rudaulvi and so joined to Jalal al-Din Panipati "تین واسطوں سے"; of the "خاندان چشت اہل بہشت **سلسلہ صابریہ**"; *Anwar al-Uyun* (4)[OCR?]; editor: his era was "بڑا ہوش ربا" and "آپ کو مجبوراً **سیاست** میں حصہ لینا پڑا", with a long maktub (33[OCR?]) to **Sikandar Lodhi** and another to **Babur**, both giving the kings counsel; thirteen works listed with editions. (pp. 16–19, 100–102, folios 88–90)
- **Shaikh Ahmad Abd al-Haq Rudaulvi / شیخ احمد عبدالحق رودولوی** — **d. 836 AH [OCR?] / 1433/34 [OCR?] or 1434 CE**; "آپ کے نفسِ گرم سے سلسلہ صابریہ کو بڑا عروج حاصل ہوا"; **he founded the Chishtiya khanqah at Rudauli.** (p. 101, folio 89)
- **Shaikh Jalal al-Din Thanesari / شیخ جلال الدین تھانیسری** — entry no. 53; died **989 AH**; khalifa and janishin of Abd al-Quddus Gangohi; genealogy to Umar b. al-Khattab, father Mahmud also a learned man; "ولی مادر زاد", who memorised the Quran at seven and finished the sciences at seventeen; editor: *Tahqiq-e Arazi-e Hindi* (Karachi) and *Irshad al-Talibin* (Amritsar) current, and Mulla Abd al-Qadir Badayuni met him twice and recorded his impressions. (p. 102, folio 90)
- **Shaikh Nizam al-Din Balkhi / شیخ نظام الدین بلخی** — **Nizam al-Din Balkhi b. Abd al-Shukur Faruqi Thanesari, d. 1036 AH**; a well-known scholar and shaikh, great janishin of Jalal al-Din Thanesari; expelled from India to the Haramain by the king; ten or more works listed, manuscripts in the Jawahir collection of the Etawah Museum (now Aligarh) and with Dr Maulvi Muhammad Shafi at Lahore. (pp. 103–104, folio 91)
- **Shaikh Abu Said Chishti Sabri Gangohi / شیخ ابوسعید گنگوہی** — entry no. 56; died "in the year one thousand and forty-nine[OCR?]", **1049**; **mazar at Gangoh** — **the editor corrects the descent and the date, and the appendix lays out three incompatible datings**: the 1049 AH the author took from *Khazinat al-Asfiya* 1/472 on *Mir'at al-Asrar*'s authority, "لیکن مراۃ الاسرار میں سرے سے ان کا سال وصال درج ہی نہیں ہے", nor does *Iqtibas al-Anwar* 282, while "مشتاق احمد ایٹھوی [OCR?] نے **بغیر کسی حوالے** کے ان کا سال وصال **ربیع الاول ۱۰۴۰ھ** دیا ہے"; son of Shaikh Nur al-Din(3) b. Abd al-Quddus Gangohi, trained and given the khirqa by Nizam al-Din Balkhi. (pp. 104–105, folios 92–93; pp. 345–346)
- **Shaikh Muhammad Sadiq b. Fath Allah Gangohi Hanafi Chishti Sabri / شیخ محمد صادق گنگوہی** — entry no. 57; died "اٹھارہویں محرم ۱۰۵۸ھ", **18 Muharram 1058 AH** — **disputed at length by the editor**; khalifa of Shaikh Abu Said, "صاحبِ مقامات بلند ومدارج ارجمند", unique of his age in zauq, shauq, wajd, sama and love; the **Jagannath** speaking-idol episode recorded. (pp. 105–106, folios 93–94)
- **Shaikh Muhammad Daud b. Shaikh Muhammad Sadiq Gangohi / شیخ محمد داؤد** — entry no. 61; died **5 Ramadan 1095** — **the editor moves his death year twenty-two years**; murid and khalifa of his own father; **of his many khalifas four were the great ones — Shaikh Sondha, Shaikh Bilaqi, Sayyid Gharib Allah and Shah Abu al-Maali — "جن سے علیحدہ علیحدہ سلسلے جاری ہوئے"**. (p. 108, folio 96)
- **Shah Abu al-Maali Chishti Sabri (Anbethvi) / شاہ ابوالمعالی چشتی انبیٹھوی [OCR?]** — entry no. 62; died "in the year one thousand one hundred and sixteen", **۱۱۱۶ھ**; khirqa from Shaikh Daud and faiz also from Muhammad Sadiq Gangohi; his father **Sayyid Muhammad Ashraf** lived at **qasba Amethah, district Saharanpur**, and of him the editor says only "شیخ محمد اشرف کے حالات معلوم نہیں ہو سکے"; orphaned young, "والدہ نے ان کو شیخ محمد صادق کے سپرد کیا" and Muhammad Sadiq at his own death handed him to Shaikh Daud — a guardianship passed twice by hand; a detailed article by the editor gathers "تمام مباحث" on him. **Not to be mapped to the archive's Abul Muali of Lahore, nor confused with the Shah Abul Muali who fathers entry no. 23.** (pp. 109, 346, folio 97)
- **Shaikh Sondha walad Shaikh Momin Siddiqi Chishti Sabri, named by the editor Shaikh Sondha Safidoni / شیخ سوندھا سفیدونی** — entry no. 64; died **24 Jumada al-Awwal 1129 AH** ("ایک ہزار ایک سو انتیس ۱۱۲۹ھ"); khalifa of Shaikh Daud; genealogy to Abu Bakr Siddiq; "باپ ان کا شیخ مومن **بادشاہی جاگیردار امیر کبیر** تھا" and Sondha, orphaned young, "سامان حشمت وجاہ ترک کیا"; a village urs and his own death during qawwali recorded. The editor's name resolves the "سوندھا سفیدوفی [OCR?]" of the earlier chunk. (pp. 110–111, folios 98–99)
- **Sayyid Muhammad Saeed, called Miran Shah Bhikha Chishti Sabri / میراں سید بھیکہ چشتی** — entry no. 65; born **7 Rajab, a Monday, 1046**; died **5 Ramadan al-Mubarak 1131 AH, aged eighty-four** — the appendix's *Nuzhat al-Salikin* gives **4 Ramazan 1131 AH / 1719 CE, aged 85**; "مزار گوہر بار **قصبہ گھرام** میں زیارت گاہ خلق ہے", and the appendix: "انہیں **قصبہ کہرام** جو موضع **کم تحلہ [OCR?]** سے **بارہ کوس** پر واقع ہے دفن کیا گیا"; great khalifa and janishin of Shah Abu al-Maali; "مشائخ متاخرین میں سے کسی بزرگ کو ایسے کشائش ظاہری وباطنی نصیب نہیں ہوئی" and his murids mostly reached the ranks of *aqtab* and *abdal*; **a vernacular poet sung at Sufi gatherings** — "اشعار ہندی و دوہڑے حضرت کے بمضامین توحید بہت مشہور ہیں۔ جو صوفیوں کی محفل میں گائے جاتے ہیں", the dohras copied by Lutf Allah Ambalvi into *Thamarat al-Fawad*; twenty-four-link genealogy to Imam Husain; orphaned at nine, taught by a scholar named **Farid al-Din**; mother **Bibi Malko**; son **Mir Muhammad Baqir**; chronogram carrying "۹۹۹ [OCR?]" and "۱۱۳۱ھ ارتحال". (pp. 111–113, folios 99–101; pp. 330, 347–348)
- **Sayyid Shah Zaid Salar Lashkar / سید شاہ زید سالار لشکر** — in Miran Bhikha's ancestry; came from the city of **Tirmiz** with a large army "بارادۂ جہاد" and halted in the city of **Sawana**, where the raja, named **Siyana**, "بکمال حسد حضرت کو **نماز میں شہید** کر دیا"; his sons then fought the raja, won and settled there, and **Sultan Shams al-Din, Shah of Delhi**, gave his daughter to his son **Sayyid Shihab al-Din** — a marriage **the editor flatly denies**; editor: Sayyid Shah Sulaiman Kuffar Shikan renamed Siyana "Sewana" after the conquest. (p. 112, folio 100)
- **Shaikh Abd al-Rashid Jalandhari Chishti / شیخ عبدالرشید جالندھری** — entry no. 63; died **the first (غرہ) of Rabi al-Awwal, Friday, in the year one thousand one hundred and twenty-one**; of the family of the great sayyids of the city of **Jalandhar**, father Sayyid Ashraf; bai'at with Shah Abu al-Maali, who handed him to **Miran Sayyid Bhikha** for training; his son **Ghulam Muhi al-Din** was also an accepted man, whose bai'at was likewise at Miran Sayyid Bhikha's hand — a father and son both bound to the same second shaykh. (p. 110, folio 98)
- **Shaikh Atiq Allah Chishti Jalandhari / شیخ عتیق اللہ جالندھری** — entry no. 66; died **1131 AH**, the same year as Miran Bhikha; **no grave given**; of the sayyids of Jalandhar of sound lineage; bai'at with Shah Abu al-Maali; "زہد وتقویٰ ابدرجہ کمال". (pp. 113–114, folios 101–102)
- **Shah Bahlol Barki Chishti Sabri Jalandhari, named by the editor Shaikh Bahlol Gul b. Mirza Khan Barki thumma Jalandhari / شاہ بہلول برکی جالندھری** — entry no. 68; died **1170 AH** ("۱۱۷۰ھ ایک ہزار ایک سو ستر ہجری"); **mazar in Jalandhar**; khalifa of Shah Bhikha, "قوم کے **افغان**", resident of Jalandhar; a most eminent scholar who learned from Sayyid Abd al-Rashid, Sayyid Kabir and Sayyid Atiq Allah Jalandhari; "وضع ان کی **قلندرانہ** تھی"; after Shah Bhikha's death "یہ لاہور میں آئے اور **شاہ بلاق لاہوری** سے فیض پایا"; *Fawaid al-Asrar*, a commentary on Hafiz's Diwan, and his own diwan on tawhid; "**مولوی جان محمد ترک** کے ساتھ ہمیشہ ان کا بحث رہتا تھا"; students Sayyid Alim Allah Jalandhari, "بسھاجند [OCR?]" takhallus **Nadir**, and Azmat Khan Barki; eight dated Persian manuscripts in the Sherani collection; appendix: hadith sanads at **Mecca in 1086 AH** from Shaikh Muhammad Zain al-Abidin and Shaikh Ali al-Tabari and from Maulvi Farrukh Shah b. Khwaja Muhammad Saeed Sirhindi, and in **1088 AH** from Shaikh Muhammad Yahya b. the Mujaddid; he explained his own pen-name "گول کہ بمعنی **نادان و احمق** آمدہ". **The index calls him "بہلول گول برمکی | ۱۰۲" — a different nisba and a different page from the appendix's "ص ۱۱۲": see Doubts.** (pp. 114–115, folios 102–103; pp. 309–310, 314)
- **Shah Lutf Allah Chishti, Lutf Allah Ambalvi / شاہ لطف اللہ انبالوی** — entry no. 69; died **20 Zi al-Qada 1180 AH**; **mazar in Jalandhar**; of Miran Sayyid Bhikha's murids of pure conviction, "**اس کی رہائش شہر انبالہ** میں تھی"; raised from boyhood in his pir's presence, and "کتاب **ثمراۃ الفواد** اپنے پیر کے حال میں لکھی جو اب تک یادگار زمانہ ہے"; the editor summarises his own autobiography. (pp. 115–116, folios 103–104)
- **Shaikh Sayyid Alim Allah b. Sayyid Atiq Allah Chishti Jalandhari / شیخ سید علیم اللہ جالندھری** — entry no. 71; born **22 Jumada al-Thani 1109**; died **16 Safar 1202**; "**روضہ عالیہ جالندھر میں زیارت گاہ خلق ہے**", chronogram "(۱۲۰۲)"; sayyids of sound descent on both sides from qasba Jalandhar, genealogy to Zaid b. Imam Hasan; first bai'at with Shah Abu al-Maali, then completion and khirqa from Miran Sayyid Bhikha; "علوم ظاہری میں بھی یہ صاحب تدریس تھے"; works *Anhar al-Asrar*, a *Sharh Bostan*, *Risala Nuzhat al-Salikin*, *Sharh Akhlaq-e Nasiri*, *Zubdat al-Riwayat*, and *Nathr al-Jawahir*, a Persian translation of a work "مؤلفہ **مرزا خان برکی**" — the father of Bahlol Barki; **his khalifa Shaikh Abdullah wrote a book *Asrar al-Alim* on him**; the *Nuzhat al-Salikin*'s text "تاحال طبع نہیں ہوا", its Urdu translation *Tuhfat al-Salihin*, Lahore c. 1931, composed 1182 AH/1768 CE, carries his life: home qasba Jalandhar; at fifteen the *Mutawwal* with Sayyid Kabir; an early Suhrawardi leaning through the *Awarif al-Maarif*, turned to the Chishtis by a dream; met Miran Bhikha's khalifa Shah Bahlol Barki at Jalandhar and took bai'at at **Panipat**, the shaykh calling him "فاضل جالندھری". (pp. 118–119, folios 106–107; pp. 346–348)
- **Sayyid Ali Shah Chishti Sabri Jalandhari / سید علی شاہ جالندھری** — entry no. 73; died **1213**; **mazar at qasba Jalandhar, "زیارت گاہ خلق"**; khalifa of Sayyid Alim Allah, "مسند ارشاد پر متمکن ہوئے" after his pir's death. (p. 121, folio 109)
- **Shaikh Mahmud Saeed Chishti Jalandhari / شیخ محمود سعید جالندھری** — entry no. 75; died **1220 AH**; **no grave given**; of Sayyid Alim Allah's khalifas; "ہزاروں آدمیوں نے ان کی ذات سے بہرۂ کامل تلقین وتعلیم سے پایا" and "تمام عمر انھوں نے طلبہ کی تکمیل وتربیت میں صرف کی". (p. 122, folio 110)
- **Hafiz Musa Chishti Manikpuri / حافظ موسیٰ مانک پوری** — entry no. 77; died **16 Ramadan, a Sunday, 1245**; **mazar at qasba Manikpur**; of Shah Bhikha's silsila, murid and khalifa of Shaikh Sayyid Azam Chishti Rupri; "ابتدائے حال میں **مقفلی گری** کے کام میں مشغول رہتے اور **دو زوجہ** ان کی تھیں۔ جب جاذب حقیقی نے ان کو اپنی طرف کھینچا۔ دونوں عورتوں کو **طلاق** دے دی" — a **lock-maker** who divorced both wives; whoever met his gaze in wajd became majzub, "کئی شخص مثل **کریم شاہ** و**محمد شاہ** وغیرہ مجذوب ہو گئے"; six khalifas — Amanat Ali Amrohi, Ghulam Muin al-Din Shah Khamosh, Khwaja Abdullah Amrohi, Mir Amanat Ali Thani, Muhammad Bakhsh, Pir Shah. (pp. 122–123, folios 110–111)
- **Maulvi Amanat Ali Chishti Sabri / مولوی امانت علی** — entry no. 79; died **1280**; of Hafiz Musa's khalifas, "تارک الدنیا"; after his pir's death **he went to Kashmir** and took faiz also from Shaikh Ahmad Shah Kashmiri, then returned and **settled at Amroha**; three of his murids — Shaikh Abd al-Rahim, Abd al-Rahman, Shaikh Sami Allah — "موجود ہیں", living. (pp. 124–125, folios 112–113)
- **Shaikh Muhammad Fazil Qadri Batalvi / شیخ محمد فاضل قادری بٹالوی** — entry no. 35; died **14 Zi al-Hijja ۱۱۵۱ھ [OCR?]**; "مزار **قصبہ بٹالہ [OCR?]** میں ہے"; among the greatest ulama and fuqara, his whole life in teaching "with thousands completed through him"; his khanqah's expenditure recorded; a modern publication on the shrine is cited — Muhi al-Din, *Darbar-e Qadiriya Faziliya ka Qartas al-Taaruf*, Lahore 1971; the place index carries "بٹالہ (**وجہ تسمیہ و آبادی**) | ۱۶۹". (p. 76, folio 74; pp. 292, 333)
- **Sayyid Imam Ali Shah Samari al-Hasani al-Husaini Naqshbandi Mujaddidi / سید امام علی شاہ** — entry no. 99; born **1212**; died **13 Shawwal 1282 AH**; "روضہ منورہ موضع **رٹڑ چھتر ضلع گورداسپور** میں ہے"; "آخری زمانہ میں یہ بزرگ گویا **قطب وقت** و چشمہ فیض تھے"; a chain of six intermediaries to the Mujaddid which **the editor repairs**; "خلیفے ان کے اقالیم دور دراز میں پہنچ کر ہدایت خلق مصروف ہوئے"; a langar and a reputation as a healer; his son **Sayyid Sadiq Ali Shah** took the masnad after him. (pp. 155–157, folios 143–145)
- **Shah Qamees b. Sayyid Abi al-Hayat Gilani Sadhauri / شاہ قمیص سادھوری** — entry no. 14; died **3 Zi Qada, "the year nine hundred and ninety-two"**; **maqbara at Sadhaura**; his father came from Baghdad to Hind, stayed a while in **Bengal**, then settled at **qasba Sadhaura Khizrabad in the Ambala area**, where a scholar named Nasr Allah gave him his daughter; "ولی مادر زاد"; "گویا سلسلہ قادریہ **ان کی ذات بابرکات سے ہند میں شائع ہوا**"; "ان کی اولاد اب بھی سادھورہ میں رہتی ہے"; the footnote carries his line down to Sayyid Rahm Ali Shah (d. 1214 AH) and thence, khalifa by khalifa, to Haji Shah Abdul Rahim Shaheed, Mian Nur Muhammad Jhanjhanwi and Haji Imdad Allah Muhajir Makki; a *Khawariqat-e Shah Qamis Qadri* by Pir Muhammad Lahori survives in the Azar collection. (p. 52, folio 40; pp. 292, 297)
- **Miran Sayyid Ghulam, famous as Shah Sahib / میراں سید غلام المشہور شاہ صاحب** — entry no. 168; died **1278 AH** and "**اپنے مقام سکونت میں دفن** ہوا" — buried in his own place of residence; the **middle son** of Sayyid Ghulam Muhi al-Din Qadiri; "پچاس برس تک حضرت موضع **ہر دو تھلہ [OCR?] ضلع ہوشیار پور** میں خلوت نشین رہے" — fifty years in seclusion at Har Do Thala, district Hoshiarpur, often going out into the jungle at night. (p. 234, folio 222)
- **Hafiz Muhammad Shah / حافظ محمد شاہ** — described in the present tense: world-renouncing, a hafiz of the Quran, who "شہر **امرت سر** میں بمقام **باغ رامانند** تمام زمانہ سے الگ ایک حجرہ میں **تنہا رہتے ہیں**" — living alone in a hujra at Bagh Ramanand, Amritsar, "**اور مؤلف کتاب بھی ان کی زیارت سے بہرہ مند ہوا ہے**", visited by the author; brother of Shah Sahib. (p. 234, folio 222)
- **Sayyid Ghulam Ghaus / سید غلام غوث** — entry no. 169; died **1279 AH**; **eldest son** of Sayyid Ghulam Muhi al-Din Gilani; "اعتقاد مند لوگوں کا ہجوم ہمیشہ حضرت کے دروازے پر رہتا … **بڑے بڑے رئیس اور حکام وقت** حضرت کے ارشاد کی تعمیل بدل و جان کرتے تھے"; descendants and a named sajjada nashin recorded. (pp. 234–235, folios 222–223)
- **Fath Allah Shah, famous as Fath Shah Bukhari / فتح شاہ بخاری** — dates (not stated); **a fourth son of Mauj Darya**, mentioned by Nami on the authority of an anonymous manuscript, buried "بیرون دروازہ **لوہ گڈھ دلاہوری [OCR?] امرتسر**" — outside the Loh Garh gate, Amritsar; "**تقسیم پاک و ہند تک امرتسر میں یہ مزار سب سے زیادہ مرجع خلائق تھا۔ اب بھی شکستہ صورت میں موجود ہے**" — until Partition the most frequented mazar in Amritsar, and it still exists in a broken state. **The only statement in the book about a shrine's condition after 1947 and about a shrine now across the border.** (p. 182, folio 180 as printed)
- **Shaikh Fath Shah Amritsari / شیخ فتح شاہ امرت سری** — entry no. ۲۱۵; grave and date **(not stated)**; "یہ بزرگ بھی **امرت سر** میں صاحب کمال **مست و مجذوب** گذرا ہے۔ کشف و کرامات اکثر خلقت کے زبان زد ہیں". (p. 266, folio 254)
- **Mian Saranga Majzub Hansvi / میاں سرانگا مجذوب ہانسوی** — entry no. 171; died "سال چھ سو چھیالیس", **646**; **grave (not stated)**; "یہ مجذوب **شہر ہانسی** میں سکونت رکھتا تھا" and followed Baba Farid to Delhi and back; editor: "**قوم کوردہ [OCR?]** سے تعلق رکھتے تھے" (*Akhbar al-Akhyar* 275). (p. 236, folio 224)
- **Shaikh Allah Din Majzub Narnauli / شیخ اللہ دین مجذوب نارنولی** — entry no. 173; died "بقول صاحب اخبار الاخیار **پندرھویں شعبان شب برات** کے روز سال نو سو چھیالیس ۹۴۶ ہجری", 15 Shaban, Shab-e Barat, **946 AH**, chronogram "**مجذوب صادق**"; **grave (not stated)**; "یہ مجذوب **نارنول** میں رہتا تھا", wandered the streets, talked to himself, "پرانی پھٹی گودڑی کے بغیر کچھ نہ پہنتا۔ **ہاتھ پاؤں میں لوہے کے حلقے** رکھا کرتا", and read hearts. (p. 237, folio 225)
- **Sayyid Shah Abdullah Majzub Naushahi / سید شاہ عبداللہ مجذوب نوشاہی** — entry no. 182; died "**۱۱۳۱ھ** ایک ہزار ایک سو اکتیس ہجری" — **editor: 1092 AH**; **grave (not stated)**; son of **Nawab Mir Murtaza Khan** and himself "منصبدار **ہفت [OCR?] ہزاری** دربار عالمگیری میں", a mansabdar of seven thousand at the Alamgiri court, whom "خانگی و بادشاہی تعلق یک قلم … چھوٹنے محال تھے"; "دن رات ویران جنگل میں پھرا کرتا". (p. 241, folio 229)
- **The four majzubs labelled نوشاہی at entry nos. 181–184**, printed pp. 228–230 — names not carried over in this material; and **Shaikh Abdul Rahman, known as Pak Rahman Naushahi**, entry no. 38, printed p. 68: a second Naushahi figure besides Nausha Ganj Bakhsh, whose *samadiyat* so overcame him "کہ کھانا، پینا، سونا ان کا بالکل موقوف ہو گیا تھا", who hung head-down in a well through the night and in seclusion "زمین میں قبر کھود کر بیٹھ جاتے اور اوپر سے بند کرا دیتے", being taken out after forty days; his servant **Shaikh Shadi** is named; **burial (not stated)**. (pp. 16, 20, 68, 80–81)
- **Shah Maruf Chishti wa Qadri / شاہ معروف چشتی و قادری** — entry no. 12; died "in the year nine hundred eighty-seven Hijri", **987**; **burial (not stated)**; first a murid of his own father in the Chishti way, then went to Sayyid Mubarak Haqqani at **لکھی جنگل (Lakhi Jangal)** and took the khirqa of khilafat in the Qadiri silsila — the only man to receive khilafat from Mubarak Haqqani. (pp. 44, 50–51, folios 38–39)
- **Shaikh Abu al-Ghaith Bukhari Majzub / شاہ ابوالغیث بخاری مجذوب** — entry no. 176; died "سال نو سو سرسٹھ ہجری", **967 AH**; **place and grave (not stated)**; "فرزند دلبند **سید حاجی عبد الوہاب بخاری** تھا", whose entry is in the book's Suhrawardi section; as a student he said "خدا جانے مجھ پر کون سی حالت آئے گی۔ یہ وقت غنیمت ہے", and on finishing his studies became majzub; a griddle miracle and a household urs recorded. (p. 238, folio 226)
- Also named without a place: **Shaikh Bilaqi** and **Sayyid Gharib Allah**, two of Shaikh Daud's four great khalifas (p. 108) · **Shaikh Sayyid Azam Chishti Rupri**, Hafiz Musa's pir (p. 122) · **Hafiz Muhammad Ali** and his khanqah at **Khairabad** (p. 124) · **Shah Rauf Ahmad Naqshbandi Mujaddidi**, entry no. 96, **died at sea** on the Hajj voyage in **1253** — "جب جہاز دریائے **محیط** میں پہنچا۔ پیک اجل آیا" — murid, khalifa and maternal cousin of Shah Ghulam Ali, posted by him **to the city of Bhopal**, takhallus **Rafat**, born at **Rampur in 1201 AH / 1786 CE**, first taught suluk by Shah Dargahi, autobiography at the end of *Jawahir-e Alawiya* pp. 294–309, eleven works listed (p. 149) · **Shah Ahmad Saeed Mujaddidi**, entry no. 98, who "جب شورش فوج انگریزی **۱۸۵۷ء** میں ہوا اور تمام شہر تہ و بالا ہو گیا" left Delhi with wife and children for the House of God and **died there in 1277 AH**, editor: born at **Rampur in 1217 AH**, **buried in Jannat al-Baqi**, eight works including ***Haq al-Mubin fi Radd al-Wahhabin***, and whose two surviving sons **Hafiz Abd al-Ghani and Abd al-Mughni** "اب تک وطن واپس تشریف نہیں لائے" (pp. 154–155) · **Muhammad Mazhar b. Shah Ahmad Saeed**, **d. 1301 AH**, who "مدینہ منورہ میں ایک خانقاہ '**رباط مظہری**' بنائی تھی، جو اب تک قائم ہے" (p. 155).

### The Deccan

- **Shaikh Nizam al-Din Aurangabadi / شیخ نظام الدین اورنگ آبادی** — **1060–1142 AH / 1649–1729 CE**; a famous elder of the Chishtiya silsila and father and pir of Fakhr-e Jahan; after **Khwaja Gesu Daraz (d. 825 AH)** no elder of the Chishtiya Nizamiya of such rank "سرزمین دکن پر … قدم نہیں رکھا"; "امرا کو آپ سے بڑی عقیدت تھی", and **Nizam al-Mulk Asaf Jah I** (dates printed "۱۷۴۸ - ۱۶۷۱ء") was his devotee and wrote a book, "اشک گلستان ارم [OCR?]", on him; his teachings affected both the people and the rulers of the Deccan. Burial (not stated here). (pp. 116, 304, folio 104)
- **Nawab Nizam al-Mulk Ghazi al-Din Khan, Nazim of Hyderabad** — author of *Manaqib-e Fakhriya*, "بے شمار کرامتیں حضرت کی اپنی کتاب میں درج کی ہیں"; editor: "نبیرہ آصف جاہ اول", grandson of Asaf Jah I. (p. 117, folio 105)
- **Khwaja Gesu Daraz / خواجہ گیسو دراز** — **d. 825 AH**; named as the last Chishti Nizami of the first rank in the Deccan before Nizam al-Din Aurangabadi, and as Fakhr-e Jahan's maternal ancestor. (pp. 116–117)

### The Hijaz, Arabia and the west

- **Shaikh Abdul Qadir Jilani, Ghaus al-Azam / حضرت غوث الاعظم** — the Qadiri chapter's founder; spiritual chain given from Imam Ali b. Musa Raza through Maruf Karkhi, Sari Saqti, Junaid Baghdadi, Abu Bakr Shibli, Abdul Wahid Tamimi, Abul Faraj Tartusi, Abul Hasan Qureshi Hakari and Abu Said Mubarak Makhzumi to him; both genealogies set out in full, "صحیح الطرفین حسنی و حسینی"; **madhhab Hanbali**; karamat "as many as the stars in the sky"; "called Jili and Jilani from **Jilan**, where he lived"; a *Bahjat al-Asrar* on him printed Egypt 1304 AH. (pp. 39–40, folios 27–28; p. 296)
- **Shaikh Abd al-Wahhab Muttaqi / شیخ عبدالوہاب متقی** — dates (not stated); "پاک و ہند کے ان عدیم المثال علما بر حدیث [OCR?] میں سے تھے۔ جنہوں نے **مکہ معظمہ کے ایک گوشہ میں بیٹھ کر ساری علمی دنیا کو اپنی طرف متوجہ کر لیا** تھا اور اپنے علمی تبحر کا سکہ **حجاز، یمن، مصر اور شام** سے منوایا تھا"; Abd al-Haq's second shaykh, who sent him home with picked students "**پاک و ہند کی کایا پلٹنے کے لیے**". (p. 206, folio 194)
- **Shaikh Ali Muttaqi / شیخ علی متقی** — "بن حسام الدین بن عبد الملک بن قاضی خان قادری شاذلی مدنی چشتی"; **born at Burhanpur in 885 AH, died aged ninety in 975 AH**; "شیخ علی متقی کو تصنیف و تالیف سے عشق تھا", and **Abd al-Haq put the number of his works at a hundred**; the subject of the editor's own article in *Risala Sarhad*, March 1974 — the latest imprint in the book. (p. 206, folio 194)
- **Sayyid Mahmud b. Sayyid Ali Husaini Kurdawi / سید محمود کردوی** — dates (not stated); the shaykh from whom Haji Muhammad Saeed Lahori received the **Qadiri khilafat**, taking bai'at **at Medina**; his chain to Abd al-Qadir Jilani is printed in full and **twice corrected by the editor**. (pp. 212–215)
- **Maulana Khalid Kurdi / مولانا خالد کردی** — dates (not stated); Shah Ghulam Ali's khalifa, who "**عرب [اور ترکستان] میں** … سلسلہ ارشاد و تلقین شروع کر کے سارے عرب کو متاثر کیا". (p. 145, folio 133)
- **Sayyid Abul Abbas Ahmad b. Sayyid Sufi** and **Sayyid Abu Sulaiman** — the two brothers who left Baghdad at the sack under Hulagu — "۶۵۶ھ / ۱۲۵۸ء میں ہلاکو کے ہاتھوں بغداد کا سقوط ہوا" — went to Rum and settled at **Aleppo**; the Uch Gilanis' forebears. (p. 40, folio 28)
- Chain-links named from outside India, useful for `silsila` fields rather than as rows: **Shaikh Khizr Sistani**, Mian Mir's pir (p. 60) · **Shaikh Daud Kirmani** and his teacher **Maulana Ismail Lahori** (pp. 48–49) · **Shaikh Wajih al-Din Gujrati**, **Shah Muhammad Ghaus Gwaliari**, **Shaikh Ilah Dad Qadri Akbarabadi**, **Shaikh Muhammad Jalal**, **Sayyid Nur**, **Sayyid Zain al-Abidin Husaini**, **Shaikh Abdul Ghafur** — the Shattari chain behind Shah Raza Lahori (p. 73) · **Shah Latif Burhanpuri**, **Shaikh Burhan ترلامی [OCR?]**, **Shaikh عیسیٰ زندول [OCR?]** — Fath Shah Shattari's chain (p. 212) · **Shaikh Auhad al-Din Isfahani**, Miran Badshah's pir (p. 203) · **Khwaja Uwais Qarni**, from whose spirit the Uwaisiya took its faiz, one of whose teeth is among the Lahore Fort relics (pp. 219, 259) · the twenty-two-name **Naqshbandi chain** printed as the chapter's frame, opening "اول امام اس خاندان عالی شان کے حضرت **ابوبکر صدیق** رضی اللہ عنہ ہیں", against the Chishti and Qadiri chains' descent from Ali (p. 127) · the **Chishti chain** from Hasan Basri, "اور یہ سلسلہ ہند اور پنجاب میں حضرت **خواجہ معین الدین حسن سجزی** سے شائع ہوا", whose nisba the editor insists is **Sajzi**, "نہ کہ سنجری" (pp. 84–85) · **Khwaja Muin al-Din Hasan Sijzi Chishti Ajmeri** himself, who "نے بھی یہاں **چلہ** پاؤں کی طرف کیا اور سرداری حاصل کی۔ **شاہنشاہ ہند** خطاب پایا" at Data Ganj Bakhsh's feet — his own shrine is at **Ajmer** and is not an archive row (p. 195).

### Place not stated

Figures the book names and describes but does not bury anywhere the transcription records. Each still needs a page-level check before being dropped, because in several cases the burial sentence falls at a chunk edge.

**Shah Maruf Chishti wa Qadri** (no. 12, d. 987) · **Shaikh Abdul Rahman "Pak Rahman" Naushahi** (no. 38) · **Shaikh Abd al-Ahad b. Shaikh Muhammad Saeed** (no. 91, d. 1142 AH [OCR?]) · **Khwaja Muin al-Din Naqshbandi Kashmiri** (no. 87, d. 2 Muharram 1085 AH) · **Shaikh Atiq Allah Chishti Jalandhari** (no. 66, d. 1131 AH) · **Shaikh Mahmud Saeed Chishti Jalandhari** (no. 75, d. 1220 AH) · **Shaikh Abd al-Hakim Sialkoti** (no. 142, d. 1068 AH) · **Sayyid Abu Turab "Shah Gada"** (no. 144, d. 14 Shawwal 1071 AH) · **Maulvi Ghulam Rasul Fazil Lahori** (no. 160, neither year nor grave) · **Sayyid Shah Abdullah Majzub Naushahi** (no. 182, d. 1131 AH / editor 1092 AH) · **Mian Mongar Majzub Lahori** (no. 177, d. ۹۷۵ھ [OCR?]) · **Shaikh Abu al-Ghaith Bukhari Majzub** (no. 176, d. 967 AH) · **Bahle [OCR?] Shah Majzub Lahori** (no. 190, d. 1265) · **Jali Shah Majzub Lahori** (concordance row ۷۲, both columns blank) · **Mai Bhagi Lahori** (no. 201, d. 1264; concordance: "اب نشان مٹ چکا ہے") · **Mian Saranga Majzub Hansvi** (no. 171, d. 646) · **Sohan Majzub Ajodhani Pakpattani** (no. 172, d. 728 AH) · **Shaikh Allah Din Majzub Narnauli** (no. 173, d. 946 AH) · **Mian Maruf Majzub Dehlvi** (no. 174, vanished 947) · **Bibi Awliya** (no. 196, d. 655 AH) · **Bibi Lalla Kashmiri** (no. 198, "flew to heaven" 756, therefore no grave) · **Shaikh Fath Shah Amritsari** (no. ۲۱۵) · **Pir Ghazi "az Ghaib"** (no. ۲۲۷, name and date both unknown) · **Mazar Marg Naini** (no. ۲۳۹, location and date unknown) · **Shaikh Nizam al-Din Aurangabadi** (1060–1142 AH) · **Shaikh Adam Banuri**'s year, which the same entry gives twice (1054 and 1052 AH) · **the unnamed tail of entry no. 106**, whose Multan mazar and 737 AH date cannot yet be attached to a name.

---

**Count.** The section lists roughly **300 distinct figures and sites**, of which about **175 are marked [PK]** — a burial place, or a named shrine, in present-day Pakistan. Around **125 of those [PK] entries are in Lahore and its immediate environs** (Mozang, Garhi Shahu, Miani, Dholanwal, Faizpur, Khoi Miran), with the rest spread over Multan, Uch, Satghara, Hujra Shah Muqim, Jarwa, Chunian, Sharaqpur, Kasur, Gujranwala, Wazirabad, Kotla, Sialkot, Peshawar, Maharan and Taj Sarwar, Mubarakpur, Kot Lajnat, Layyah and Pakpattan. A further **35 or so are undated or unplaced** and are gathered under "Place not stated"; another **30 have a burial place now in India** (Delhi, Sirhind, Panipat, Kaithal, Gangoh, Sadhaura, Kaliyar, Jalandhar, Gharam/Kahram, Batala, Ratar Chhatar in Gurdaspur, Amritsar, Hoshiarpur, Manikpur, Amroha, Rudauli, Srinagar and the Kashmir valley) and about **10 outside the subcontinent** (Jannat al-Baqi, Medina, Mecca, Aleppo, Baghdad, and one at sea). These counts are of lines in this section, not of the book's numbered entries: the book runs to 247 entries, and a single line here sometimes covers a family enclosure holding a dozen graves.

## Citable passages

Twenty passages, chosen from the whole book rather than from one stretch of it, for passages that do
work inside an entry: a shrine's building, rebuilding or loss in the author's own words; a dated
burial; an urs, a langar or a weekly practice; a statement about custodianship, endowment or revenue;
the author on himself and his family; and the sharpest corrections in the apparatus. Urdu is
reproduced exactly as the chunk notes give it; the gloss is a plain rendering, not a free
translation.

**1. A rauza paid for, and its stone carried off**

> "ان کا روضہ دارا شکوہ نے کئی لاکھ روپے خرچ کر کے بنوایا تھا جس کے پتھر تمام و کمال رنجیت سنگھ اتروا کر امرت سر لے گیا۔"

Gloss: Dara Shikoh had his rauza built at a cost of several lakh rupees, whose stones Ranjit Singh
had entirely taken down and carried away to Amritsar. — p. 69, folio 57. Good for a building's
patron, its cost and its later stripping in one sentence.

**2. Who paid for the dome, and when**

> "حضرت کے مرقد مقدس پر پہلے گنبد نہ تھا۔ بارہ برس گذرے ہیں کہ حاجی نور محمد سادھو نے تعمیر کیا۔"

Gloss: There was no dome over the Hazrat's holy tomb before; twelve years ago Haji Nur Muhammad
Sadhu built it. — p. 195, folio 193 as printed. Good for dating a dome to a named donor, relative to
the book's own present.

**3. A mazar built in one night, with the reason given**

> "رات کو انہوں نے جمع ہو کر ایک رات میں مزار کو تعمیر کر دیا اور مشہور ہوا کہ رات کو فرشتے بنا گئے ہیں"

Gloss: At night they gathered and built the mazar in a single night, and it became famous that angels
had built it by night. — p. 271, folio 259. Good for a foundation legend quoted alongside the labour
that produced it, since the same sentence supplies both.

**4. A saint who paid for his own tomb**

> "آپ نے اپنا رہائشی مکان فروخت کر کے اپنا مقبرہ بنوایا تھا۔"

Gloss: He sold his dwelling house and had his own tomb built. — of Mahmud Shah Lahori, p. 157,
folio 145. Good for a self-funded tomb, and for how a shrine's first structure got paid for.

**5. A demolition halted, and the building put back**

> "اس کی والدہ چند کور نہایت ڈری اور حکم دیا کہ مزار حضرت کا نہ گرایا جائے۔ چنانچہ گرا ہوا مکان اسی وقت تعمیر ہو گیا۔"

Gloss: His mother Chand Kaur was greatly frightened and ordered that the Hazrat's mazar not be pulled
down. Accordingly the fallen building was rebuilt at that very time. — p. 78, folio 66
[marker: 76]. Good for a rebuilding attributed to a named Sikh-court patron.

**6. A mazar inside the Fort, and the land before the Fort**

> "مزار پُر انوار بادشاہی قلعہ لاہور کے اندر تہ خانہ میں ہے کہ تعمیر قلعہ اکبری سے اول وہ زمین متعلق شہر کے تھی۔"

Gloss: His mazar is in a basement inside the Badshahi Fort, Lahore; before the building of Akbar's
fort that land belonged to the city. — p. 174, folio 162. Good for a site enclosed by later
construction, with the sequence stated.

**7. A mazar that is gone**

> "لنڈا بازار ، اب یہ مزار نہیں ہے۔"

Gloss: Landa Bazar; this mazar no longer exists. — of Shah Kaku Chishti, p. 288, folio 276. Good for
a recorded loss with the address kept.

**8. A dated burial, in the author's own family**

> "مدفن گورستان بی بی پاکدامنان … ہے۔"

Gloss: His burial place is the graveyard of Bibi Pakdamanan. (The ellipsis is in the notes.) — of the
author's father, d. 1274 AH, p. 233, folio 221. Good for a dated burial at a named graveyard, and for
the author's own family's presence there.

**9. The author's address**

> "اب تک وہ کوچہ کوٹلی مفتیاں کہلاتا ہے۔ جس میں اب بھی مؤلف کتاب قیام پذیر ہے۔"

Gloss: To this day that lane is called Kotli Muftiyan, in which the author of the book still resides.
— p. 232, folio 220. Good for placing the author in Lahore in his own voice, and for the standing of
his Lahore reporting.

**10. A custodian named, with his offices**

> "سجادہ نشین و مہتم لنگر و تدریس قرآنی حافظ احمد الدین ہے۔"

Gloss: The sajjada nashin and manager of the langar and of the Quranic teaching is Hafiz Ahmad al-Din.
— p. 188, folio 186 as printed. Good for custodianship as an office with named duties attached.

**11. Offices and offerings divided by a family record**

> "مسجد وزیر خاں کی امامت ، خطابت اور مزار حضرت سید اسحق گازرونی کی فتوحات وغیرہ میرے صاحبزادے اللہ بخش کو ملیں"

Gloss: the imamat and khitabat of the Wazir Khan Mosque and the offerings of the mazar of Sayyid
Ishaq Gazruni are to pass to my son Allah Bakhsh. — p. 337, folio 325. Good for shrine income and
mosque office descending together by a family instrument.

**12. A khanqah endowed with villages**

> "رنچن نے … بابا بلبل شاہؒ کے لیے ایک خانقاہ بنوائی اور اس پر کئی گاؤں وقف کیے۔ جن کی آمدنی سے ملازمین کا خرچ چلتا تھا۔"

Gloss: Rinchan had a khanqah built for Baba Bulbul Shah and endowed several villages upon it, from
whose income the employees' expenses were met. (The ellipsis is in the notes.) — p. 203, folio 191 as
printed. Good for a founding endowment with the use of the income stated.

**13. Relics in government custody, with the custodian named**

> "یہ کل انتیس زیارتیں قلعہ لاہور میں بقبضہ سرکار انگریزی علیحدہ مکان میں بحفاظت تمام بہ تحویل منشی غلام محمد تحویلدار رکھے ہیں۔"

Gloss: All these twenty-nine relics are kept in Lahore Fort, in the possession of the English
government, in a separate building under full protection, in the custody of Munshi Ghulam Muhammad,
tahvildar. — p. 259, folio 247. Good for a count, a location and a named custodian under colonial
administration.

**14. Seventeen urs a year**

> "ہر سال میں سترہ عرس … کیا کرتے اور ہر ایک سالینہ میں طعام وافر پکا غربا و مساکین کو تقسیم کرتے۔"

Gloss: Every year he performed seventeen urs, and at each annual he cooked abundant food and
distributed it to the poor and destitute. (The ellipsis is in the notes.) — p. 125, folio 113. Good
for an urs calendar kept by a living establishment rather than a single anniversary.

**15. An urs kept by one trade**

> "دھوبی لوگ حضرت کا عرس کرتے ہیں۔"

Gloss: The dhobi people perform the Hazrat's urs. — p. 174, folio 162. Good for an urs whose
constituency is an occupational community.

**16. A guild's saint, and an annual urs**

> "تمام لاہور کے رنگ ریز اس کو اپنا مقتدیٰ و پیشوا تصور کرتے ہیں۔ ہر سال عرس بھی ہوتا ہے۔"

Gloss: All the dyers of Lahore regard him as their leader and guide; an urs is also held every year.
— p. 278, folio 266. Good for a trade's patron saint, with the urs recorded as current.

**17. A weekly vigil, and where the visitors come from**

> "اب بھی ہر ایک جمعرات کو معتقدان شہر لاہور و شہر امرتسر جمع ہو کر حضرت کے مزار پر تمام رات بیدار رہتے ہیں۔"

Gloss: Even now, every Thursday, the devotees of the city of Lahore and the city of Amritsar gather
and stay awake all night at the Hazrat's mazar. — p. 195, folio 193 as printed. Good for a weekly
practice and for a pre-Partition catchment naming two cities.

**18. A death year refused, on internal evidence**

> "آپ کا سال وفات ۴۶۵ھ غلط ہے۔ بلکہ کشف المحجوب کے اندرونی شواہد آپ کے ۴۹۰ھ تک بقید حیات ہونے کا ثبوت مہیا کرتے ہیں۔"

Gloss: His death year of 465 AH is wrong; rather the internal evidence of *Kashf al-Mahjub* provides
proof that he was alive up to 490 AH. — p. 196, folio 194 as printed. Good for a dated correction
that names its method; the notes do not say in whose voice it stands.

**19. A shrine's location corrected against the author**

> "مفتی صاحب نے لکھا ہے کہ آپ کا مزار سرہند میں ہے۔ یہ درست نہیں۔ آپ کا مزار دہلی میں … باغ مکرم خاں میں ہے۔"

Gloss: The Mufti wrote that his mazar is at Sirhind. This is not correct. His mazar is in Delhi, in
Bagh Mukarram Khan. (The ellipsis is in the notes.) — p. 141, folio 129. Good for a correction that
moves a shrine, with the author named in the third person as the party corrected.

**20. The editor on the relics' provenance**

> "تاریخی طور پر ان زیارات کے پنجاب میں پہنچنے کا کوئی معاصر ثبوت نہیں ہے۔ یہ محض منسوب ہیں۔"

Gloss: Historically there is no contemporary evidence of these relics reaching Punjab. They are
merely attributed. — the editor, p. 261, folio 249, حاشیہ ۱. Good as the sharpest editorial refusal in
the book, and as the counterweight to the relic list quoted at no. 13.

## Legends and miracles

Every item is reported as the book reports it: "the author relates", "the author relates on the
authority of X", "narrated as نقل ہے", "the editor notes", "reported as locally famous". Where the
author reports a legend and the editor treats it differently, both are kept. Nothing here is stated
as fact.

**Abdul Qadir Jilani** — the author writes that his karamat are "as many as the stars in the sky"
(p. 40, folio 28).

**Sayyid Abdul Qadir Sani** — the author relates that his famous karamat was that "مشہور کرامت ان کی
یہ تھی کہ جو کوئی کفار یا فساق و فجار میں سے ان کی خدمت میں حاضر ہوتا مسلمان اور تائب ہو جاتا", any
unbeliever or reprobate who came into his presence became a Muslim and repented (p. 42, folio 30).

**Sayyid Mahmud Huzuri** — the author relates that the day anyone took bai'at at his hand, that same
night he was honoured with a vision of the Prophet, which is why he was given the title "Huzuri"
(pp. 43–44, folios 31–32).

**Mubarak Haqqani** — the author relates that whoever came before him became *mast* and *majzub*, and
that he himself remained twelve years intoxicated (p. 44, folio 32).

**Shah Latif Bari** — the author writes that thousands of khawariq and karamat are famous, without
specifying any; and that his pir Hayat al-Mir Zinda Pir is said to be "زندہ جاوید", eternally living
(p. 45, folio 33).

**Bahawal Sher Hujrawi**
- the author relates that he lived 250 years, an age no Qadiri shaykh has reached; that he sat three
  separate twelve-year seclusions, worshipping without food or sleep; that sitting forty years in one
  spot in a cave his back stuck to the stone he used as a pillow and the skin stayed on the stone when
  he rose; and that his beard only began to grow at the age of one hundred (p. 47, folio 35).
- the founding of Hujra: narrated by the author as "نقل ہے" (it is related) — driven twice off land
  belonging to the zamindars of the "قوم دھول [OCR?]" where he had built a "صفہ [OCR?]" on the
  riverbank, he came into *jalal* and ordered the river to move and vacate the place; the water went
  several miles off at once, a high ridge emerged, he settled on it, the zamindars on seeing the
  karamat all became his murids, and habitation grew there — the present qasba of Hujra (p. 47,
  folio 35). Two readings in this line are uncertain: the tribe's name and the built structure both
  carry `[OCR?]`.

**Shaikh Daud (Kirmani)** — the author relates that he was born four months after his father's death
(p. 49, folio 37); and that his special *nisbat* with Ghaus al-Azam arose from sheer austerity, with
Ghaus al-Azam's inner permission directing him to his pir (p. 48, folio 36).

**Sayyid Muhammad Nur and the blinded mason** — narrated by the author as "نقل ہے": absent at his
father's burial, he pitched a tent over the grave, cleared everyone away and dug it open with his own
hand to see his father's face; a mason among his murids came in without permission and was struck
blind; years later, when a dome was to be built, the mason asked to be given his sight so that he
might build the tomb himself, and was told he would see as long as he was at work and go blind
whenever he rose from it — "چنانچہ جب تک مقبرہ تیار ہوتا رہا۔ ایسا ہی ہوتا رہا", and so it continued
as long as the tomb was being built (p. 51, folio 39).

**Shah Qamees** — the author writes that he was a *wali* from his mother's womb (p. 52, folio 40).

**Sayyid Musa Pak** — the author relates a constant presence with the spirituality of Ghaus al-Azam,
and hundreds of visions of the Prophet, waking and sleeping (p. 53, folio 41).

**Shah Husain (Lal Husain)** — the author relates that he was made a perfect wali at seven by a single
glance from Shaikh Bahlul (p. 54, folio 42); his karamat beyond counting are said to be recorded in
*Haqiqat al-Fuqara* and some in *Khazinat al-Asfiya*, and the author explicitly declines to reproduce
them — "اس مختصر میں ان کی گنجائش نہیں۔ اس لیے ترک کی گئیں" (pp. 54–55, folios 42–43).

**Shah Abul Muali** — the author gives as his "least" karamat a vision of Ghaus al-Azam granted to
every murid on the day of his bai'at (p. 58, folio 46).

**Mian Mir** — the author reports his austerities as marvels: a night passed in one breath, a weekly
fast-breaking, a month at a time without food (p. 61, folio 49). Separately, the author relates that
Mian Mir bestowed a *shughl* in a dream on a man sleeping at his tomb (p. 78, folio 66
[marker: 76]); and that he rebuked another saint for displaying a karamat at all (p. 255, folio 243,
under Mai Bhagi below).

**Shah Bilawal** — the author, attributing the story to the book *Mahbub al-Wasilin*, relates that
aged seven he raised a neighbour's dead boy of eight: standing by the cot he said "اے دوست بے وقت
سونا کیا ضروری ہے۔ اٹھ بیٹھ کہ آپس میں کھیلیں", and "لڑکا فی الفور زندہ ہو گیا اور اٹھ کر ان کے ساتھ
روانہ ہوا" (p. 63, folio 51).

**Shah Muhammad Muqim** — the author relates that, sleeping nightly at his ancestor Bahawal Sher's
rauza after zikr, he saw him in a dream saying "اے نور العین۔ تیرا حصہ ہمارے پاس نہیں بلکہ حیات
المیر زندہ پیر کے پاس ہے۔ تم لاہور جاؤ", which sent him to the Miani graveyard (pp. 63–64, folios
51–52).

**Khwaja Bihari** — the author, attributing the story to Dara Shikoh's *Safinat al-Auliya*, relates
that he sat in a lit fire for the space of an hour during a dispute about *tauhid* at Nawab Ghazi
Khan's house and "ایک بال ان کا نہ جلا", not one hair of his was burnt; he then said that tauhid
requires "حال درکار ہے نہ قال", a state and not talk (p. 66, folio 54).
