### 2. Identification unresolved (27 items)

*Each item says what evidence would settle it. Where a chunk worker wrote "leave unmapped", that
refusal is preserved.*

**I1. Four Jamal/Kamal identifications, and the book supports all four as distinct.** (1)
"**شاہ کمال کیتھلی**" — Shah Kamal **of Kaithal**, in the Mujaddid's Qadiri chain, with no grave, no
date and no Lahore connection (p. 129, folio 117; chunk 012; and the index at p. 309, folio 297,
chunk 029, as grandfather of Shah Sikandar Kaithali). (2) **Shah Kamal, Shah Jamal's second brother**,
devout, a man of jazb, a **Sayyid Husaini** of a **Suhrawardi** family from Baha al-Din Zakariya —
against the row's **Qadiri** — with no grave, no date, no location and no silsila given (p. 185, folio
183 as printed; chunk 017). (3) A **Shattari** "شاہ جمال", Shah Gada's third khalifa, whose pir died
1071 AH (p. 210, folio 198; chunk 020). (4) The archive's `shah-jamal`, Suhrawardi, d. 1050 AH per the
book. Add appendix row 4, "سید **کامل** شاہ لاہوری | موضع بابو صابو" (p. 287, folio 275; chunk 027),
and the index's "شاہ جمال لاہوری | ۱۶۸، ۱۶۹، ۱۷۳" (p. 315, folio 303; chunk 029).
**`shrine-of-baba-shah-kamal` must stay unmapped**, although the case is tempting — the row is at
Muslim Town / Samanabad near Ichhra, the same quarter as Shah Jamal's damdama. Evidence that would
settle it: a grave, a date or a silsila for the Ichhra brother; folios 168–173 read whole.
(Raised independently by chunks 012, 017, 020, 027, 029.)

**I2. `shah-noorani-shrine-syed-bilawal-shah-noorani` — six chunks refused the same name.** The book
carries a **Shah Bilawal Lahori** as Shah Shams al-Din's khalifa and four separate Bilawals among Shah
Husain's khalifas, three in Lahore and one in the Deccan (pp. 57, 55; folios 45, 43; chunk 004); a Shah
Bilawal with a mazar **outside Delhi Gate, Lahore**, a family jagir at Sheikhupura and a death in 1044
AH (pp. 62–63, folios 50–51; chunk 005); the man at whose indication Abd al-Hakim Sialkoti translated
*Ghunyat al-Talibin* (p. 208, folio 196; chunk 020); "لاہور کے بزرگوں میاں میر و **شاہ بلاول**" and
"شاہ **رنگ [OCR?]** بلاول حضرت لال حسین لاہوری کے خلیفہ" — where it is not even clear the two mentions
are one man (pp. 270, 271; folios 258, 259; chunk 026); index lines "بلاول لاہوری شاہ | ۵۰، ۵۱" and
"بلاول لاہوری | ۱۹۶" (p. 314, folio 302; chunk 029); and an explicitly "لاہوری" figure with no shrine,
no date and no location, plus "محمد حیات (نبیرہ شاہ بلاول) | ۵۰" (p. 344, folio 332; chunk 032). **The
row is at Lahoot Lamakan, Khuzdar, Balochistan. No chunk places any Bilawal in Balochistan.** Evidence
that would settle it: any Balochistan or Khuzdar placement, which this book does not supply.

**I3. `shrine-of-baba-shah-chiragh` (and `darbar-sakhi-shah-chan-charagh`) — at least four
Chiragh-named men, cross-referenced nowhere.** (1) "**سید عبدالرزاق قادری**", contents no. 28, printed
p. 55, against a separate "**مزار چراغ شاہ**", contents no. 238, printed p. 263 — the two cannot be
told apart from the contents (pp. 15, 21; folios 3, 9; chunk 001). (2) "**حضرت شاہ چراغ گیلانی
لاہوری**", pir of Shah Dargahi, entry ۲۲۰ (p. 267, folio 255). (3) "**شاہ عبدالرزاق مکی**", murid of
Mauj Darya, tomb in Anarkali bazar, d. "ایک ہزار چوراسی", entry ۲۲۵ — **the row's personal name, in
the row's quarter of Lahore, but a different pir and a different year** (p. 269, folio 257). (4)
"**مزار چراغ شاہ**" outside Roshnai Gate, successor of Sayyid Ali Gilani d. "ایک ہزار دو سو ستائیس",
entry ۲۳۸ (p. 275, folio 263) — plus a fourth, unrelated "چراغ شاہ" as a seal-engraver's father
(p. 274, folio 262) (chunk 026). The index files "شاہ چراغ لاہوری (عبدالرزاق) | ۵۵" and
"چراغ شاہ لاہوری | ۲۶۲" under different first words with no cross-reference (p. 316, folio 304; chunk
029), and holds at least four Chiragh-named men in all (p. 322, p. 326; chunk 030). Only a son is
indexed for the archive's figure: "مصطفیٰ ابن شاہ چراغ لاہوری | ۵۶" (p. 330, folio 318; chunk 031).
**Nothing separates the Lahore Shah Chiragh from the Rawalpindi Shah Chan Charagh.** Evidence:
folios 55, 56 and 262 read against the row (est. pp. 67, 68, 274). (Chunks 001, 026, 029, 030, 031.)

**I4. `darbar-abul-muali-qadri` — three Abul Mualis, and the index makes the row's figure the
father.** Entry no. 19 is Shah **Khair al-Din** Abul Muali Lahori b. Sayyid Rahmat Allah b. Sayyid Fath
Allah Kirmani, nephew and son-in-law of Daud Kirmani, d. 1024 (pp. 57–58, folios 45–46); entry no.
23's subject is son of a Shah Abul Muali of the **Hujra/Jarwa Gilani** line, not the Kirmani line
(p. 63, folio 51); the contents carry a third, "**شاہ ابوالمعالی چشتی**", no. 62 (p. 16, folio 4). The
row matches entry no. 19 on the name Khair al-Din, the Kirmani descent, the Daud Bandagi affiliation
and the year 1024, **but the name alone can no longer place a citation anywhere in this book** (chunk
005). The index then prints "**خیر الدین (والد شاہ ابوالمعالی) | ۴۵**" — making Khair al-Din the
**father** where the row makes him the saint (p. 317, folio 305; chunk 029) — and the same gloss
"(والد شاہ ابوالمعالی)" is reused for a different father, "محمد اشرف امبیٹھوی | ۹۷" (p. 327, folio
315; chunk 030). A fourth form, "شاہ ابوالمعالی **چشتی انبیٹھوی [OCR?]**", carries a flagged nisba and
the wrong order for the row (p. 346, folio 334; chunk 032). Separately, chunk 020's only dated act for
the row — a commission in **1023 AH** — is matched on the year falling immediately before the row's 16
Rabi ul Awal 1024 AH and on the commissioned work being a commentary on Abd al-Qadir Jilani; the chunk
names no city, no silsila, no dates and no shrine, and **the worker records the identification as his
own, "strong but circumstantial"**. Evidence: the entry at folios 33–46 (est. pp. 45–58), and PDF
p. 109. (Chunks 001, 005, 010, 020, 029, 030, 032.)

**I5. `shergarh` / Daud Bandagi Kirmani — the name is placed on a shared name only, and "Bandagi"
never appears.** A footnote's "حضرت داؤد کرمانی" is placed against the row on the shared name alone,
with no place, no date and no silsila, while the book's own contents call the Shergarh figure
"شیخ داؤد **جہنی وال** شیر گڈھی" (p. 47, folio 35, حاشیہ 4; chunk 003). The entry heading then reads
"شیخ داؤد **چونی وال** شیر گڈھی [OCR?]" (p. 48, folio 36); the entry's own account of the father moving
to **qasba Chunian** favours چونی وال, but the heading carries the flag and the contents form does
not, **so neither reading can be preferred on the transcription alone** (chunk 004). The index has
"داؤد کرمانی" without "Bandagi" (p. 317, folio 305; chunk 029) and the place index has
"**شیر گڈھ |**" with **no page number at all** (p. 335, folio 323; chunk 031). Note also that
`darbar-abul-muali-qadri` carries a "Syed Dawood Bandgi Kirmani" affiliation, and that the book's
"شیخ **طاہر** بندگی" is one letter from "داؤد بندگی" in transcription (p. 274, folio 262; chunk 026).
Evidence: the entry at printed p. 36 (est. p. 48) and folios 33–46.

**I6. `shrine-of-makhdoom-jahaniyan-jahangasht` — a "Pir Jahaniyan" who is a Kamal, in Chunian, with
living descendants.** Shah Kamal Bukhari, Muhammad Nur's maternal grandfather, is "famous as Pir
Jahanian" with a mazar at **qasba Chunian** — a different place and person from Uch Sharif's Makhdoom
Jahaniyan Jahangasht (p. 51, folio 39; chunk 004). Entry ۲۴۷-region has
"**پیر سید کمال المشہور پیر جہانیاں**" of Chunian, "سادات بخاری", with living descendants there
(p. 266, folio 254; chunk 026). The index carries "جہانیاں پیر سید کمال | ۲۵۴" (p. 316, folio 304;
chunk 029) and "کمال بخاری شاہ (پیر جہانیاں) | ۳۹" (p. 326, folio 314; chunk 030). **A human must
decide whether this is a distinct saint with a shared sobriquet or a second site claimed for the Uch
figure; no chunk supports either reading.** Evidence: folios 39 and 254 (est. pp. 51, 266). Note also
that the row takes only two sentences from the Suhrawardi chapter, both in footnotes and neither about
his shrine, and that the row's 1384 CE against Rukn-e Alam's 1335 makes him a khalifa surviving his pir
by forty-nine years, which no chunk remarks on (pp. 161, 165; folios 149, 153; chunk 015).

**I7. `shrine-of-jalaluddin-surkh-posh-bukhari` — Multan, not Uch, and no death year.** The chunk has
him migrating from Bukhara to **Multan** in 635 AH / 1227 CE; the row locates him at **Uch Sharif** and
dates him 1291. **The chunk gives him no death year and no "پوش"**; the identification rests on the
name and on his position as grandfather of Makhdum Jahaniyan Jahangasht (p. 349, folio 337; chunk 032).
The place index puts Uch on folios 157, 160, 161, 169 — not on 156 or the Multan folios — so the two
accounts are not joined (chunk 031). A bare index line, "جلال بخاری سید | ۱۴۶" at the first Multan
folio, "could be either, or a third man" and was **not assigned**, since the index has a separate
explicit line for "سرخ بخاری اوچی" (pp. 315, 318; folios 303, 306; chunk 029).

**I8. `shrine-of-abdullah-shah-ghazi` is NOT entry no. 40, and the two must be kept apart
deliberately.** Entry no. 40's Shaikh Abdullah Shah Baloch Lahori Qadri was a **Baloch camel-driver of
Mozang, Lahore**, khalifa of Sharaf al-Din Panipati, d. 8 Jumada al-Awwal **1212 AH**; the row is at
**Clifton, Karachi**, d. **773**. "The names are close enough that the two must be kept apart
deliberately; nothing in this book connects entry 40 to Karachi" (pp. 82–83, folios 70–71 as printed;
chunk 007).

**I9. `shrine-of-pir-sher-muhammad` — same qasba, different man, 114 years apart.** Entry no. 74's
**Shaikh Muhammad Saeed Chishti Sabri Sharaqpuri**, d. 1214 AH, mazar in Sharaqpur, is not the row's
Mian Pir Sher Muhammad Sharaqpuri (d. 1928, Qadiri): different silsila, 114 years apart. **Do not map**
(pp. 121–122, folios 109–110; chunk 011). Sharaqpur recurs without the row's saint ever appearing — as
the home of Maulana Nusrat Naushahi, owner of a *Thamarat al-Quds* manuscript (chunk 008), and as the
imprint of *Khazina-e Marifat*, from which **no shrine press may be inferred** (p. 156, folio 144;
chunk 014).

**I10. `mithankot-kot-mithan` — the town, not the shrine; and a different Ghulam Farid.**
"قصبہ کوٹ مٹھن" appears once, as where Qazi Muhammad Aqil taught Sulaiman Taunsvi before 1184–1205 AH;
**the book says nothing about a shrine there, nothing about Khwaja Ghulam Farid, and does not spell the
name in a way that excludes another Kot Mithan** (p. 123, folio 111; chunk 011). Separately,
"مولوی غلام فرید لاہوری" died **1216 AH** and is buried in the **Miani graveyard, Lahore** — the row's
Khwaja Ghulam Farid is of Mithankot, d. 1901, Chishti: a different man by a century, a province and an
order (p. 222, folio 210; chunk 021).

**I11. `bibi-pak-daman` — where the book's own entry on the Bibis is has never been established.** The
editor cross-refers to it ("ملاحظہ ہو کتاب ہذا تحت بی بیاں پاکدامن", p. 199, folio 187; chunk 019) and
warns that "ان کی نسبت خاصی غلط بیانی کی گئی ہے" — considerable misstatement has been made about them.
Chunk 022 could not tell whether it falls before p. 168 or after p. 235; chunk 028 records that the six
names reach it only through the editor's table. **Chunk 029 locates it: "بی بیاں پاک دامن" is indexed
at folios 187 and 235 (p. 314, folio 302), the second of which lies past the end of the numbered
entries.** Evidence: read est. pp. 199 and 247. Two near-misses to keep off the row: "بی بی سیدہ پاک
دامن" is Miran Bhikha's mother, whose name the editor's own footnote replaces with **Bibi
Malikwah[OCR?]** — "do not map this to bibi-pak-daman" (p. 112, folio 100; chunk 010); and
"یہ بی بی پاک دامن دہلی میں رہتی تھی" is an epithet for **Bibi Awliya of Delhi** (p. 252, folio 240;
chunk 024). Also "محلہ چہل بی بیاں", the quarter of the **forty** bibis, where the father's grave is
placed, is not shown to be "بی بیاں پاکدامن", and forty does not agree with six (p. 200, folio 188;
chunk 019).

**I12. `shrine-of-mian-umar-baba-chamkani` — the row's saint may be one of this edition's sources.**
"محمد عمر چمکنی" is cited as the author of a manuscript *Zawahir* on figures who died around 1100–1102
AH; the row is Mian Muhammad Umar Chamkani, Chamkani, Peshawar, **d. 1776 CE (≈ 1190 AH)**, Naqshbandi
— chronologically possible for a writer on that period, **but the chunk gives him no dates, no
location and no order, and the folio reference is left blank in the transcription ("ظواہر قلمی ورق")**
(p. 213, folio 201, حاشیہ 2; chunk 020). **Leave unmapped and flag as a candidate**: if confirmed it
would make an archive shrine's saint one of this edition's sources. Evidence: the missing ورق number,
recoverable only from the page image.

**I13. `sakhi-sarwar` — the index names Sayyid Ahmad as the saint and, elsewhere, as his father.**
"سرور سلطان سخی [OCR?] (**سید احمد**) | ۱۸۵" (p. 319, folio 307; chunk 030) against
"**احمد والد سخی سرور** | ۱۸۵" (chunk 029). **Either the book's Sakhi Sarwar and his father are both
Ahmad, or one of the two glosses is wrong**; the epithet is flagged, the personal name is not.
Evidence: folio 185 (est. p. 197). His birthplace is unrecoverable — "**کہر سی کوٹ [OCR?]**" flagged
both times it appears (p. 197, folio 185; chunk 019) and again as "کہرسی کوٹ [OCR?] | ۱۸۵" in the place
index (chunk 031). Note two absences the workers thought worth recording: 577 AH dates the **murder**,
not the shrine — the roza at Nigaha has no building date, no patron, no urs and no custodian — and
**the editor adds no footnote whatsoever to this entry**, which is unusual for a figure of this
standing (chunk 019).

**I14. `shrine-of-mauj-darya-bukhari` — a father and a son both called Safi al-Din on one folio.**
"صفی الدین بن موج دریا بخاری | ۱۶۹" and "صفی الدین (**والد** موج دریا بخاری) | ۱۶۹" print as adjacent
index lines, neither flagged; his wife is likewise indexed twice, at folios 20 and 242, under two
different wordings. **"A duplicated line with one gloss mistyped is as likely as a real papponymy; do
not put either name in the row unaided"** (pp. 320, 325; folios 308, 313; chunk 030). The editor's
table identifies the shrine by name and neighbourhood only — "بیرون مزار میران محمد شاہ", 1974
"لیک روڈ پرانی انارکلی", with **no "موج دریا" and no "بخاری"** (p. 291, folio 279; chunk 028). The
sons are incompletely recorded: the text names three, the editor adds a fourth from an **anonymous
manuscript** via Nami, the third son's name carries two flags
("شاہ شہاب الدین المعنی [OCR?] طلب بہ مہر بی بی فورنگ [OCR?]"), and the second wife's name is not
recoverable (p. 182, folio 180 as printed; chunk 017). Evidence: folio 169 (est. p. 181) read whole.

**I15. Three men named Jan Muhammad of Lahore — the book half-distinguishes them and no chunk
merges them.** (1) A **Chishti** "جان محمد چشتی لاہوری" in a chain the editor **rejects** for Ghore Shah
(p. 179, folio 177 as printed; chunk 017). (2) Entry no. 125, "**شیخ جان محمد سہروردی لاہوری**", d.
1082 AH, buried by the **Masjid Qassab Khana** outside Lahore (p. 186, folio 184 as printed; chunk
017 — where the entry breaks off at the word "وفات"). (3) Entry no. 127, d. 1100 AH, buried in
**Mohalla Parvezabad** then reburied beside Shaikh Ismail. The editor's footnote names
"**میاں جان محمد قصاب پوری**" and "**میاں جان محمد پرویز آبادی**" as two distinct teachers in the same
madrasa; the quarter-names line up with the two graves, **but the chunk never states the
identification** (p. 189, folio 187 as printed; chunk 018). **Do not merge them.**

**I16. Four men named Ghulam Muhyi al-Din, one of them on a plate.** (1) **Miran Sayyid Ghulam Muhyi
al-Din**, Qadiri, d. 1240 AH, entry no. 159. (2) **Maulana Ghulam Muhyi al-Din Qasuri**, whose
manuscript collection at the **Ganj Bakhsh Library, Rawalpindi** is cited in chunks 018, 019 and 021.
(3) The author's own cousin, son of Mufti Ghulam Rasul, alive when the book was written (p. 233, folio
221; chunk 022). (4) The **Bukhari sanad plate's grantee**, "مولوي غلام محي الدين صاحب [OCR?]"
(p. 224, folio 212; chunk 021). **"Chronologically, (1) could be the plate's grantee — Shah Abd al-Aziz
died 1239 AH and he died 1240 AH — but the chunk says nothing of Delhi or of a hadith sanad. Leave all
four separate and flag the possibility."** If (2) and (4) are the same man it would explain the
collection's contents, "but that is a hypothesis, not a finding" (chunk 021).

**I17. Shah Kaku's pir and Shaikh Haji Abd al-Karim Chishti Lahori — same name and nisba, 165 years
apart.** The footnote's "**شیخ عبد الکریم لاہوری**", from whom Shah Kaku is said to have taken faiz
(p. 99, folio 87 [marker 97]), shares a name with the Shaikh Haji Abd al-Karim Chishti Lahori of entry
no. 55, who died in **1045 AH** (p. 104, folio 92) — some 165 years after Shah Kaku's 880. **"Either
two men of the same name and nisba, or an error; the chunk does not distinguish them"** (chunk 009).

**I18. "شیخ محسن شاہ" stands in two chains of different orders.** Here a **Qadiri** link between Shah
Abd al-Rashid Lahori and Mulla Shah (p. 222, folio 210; chunk 021); in chunk 017 a "محسن شاہ" in the
**Chishti Sabiri** chain the editor rejected for Ghore Shah (p. 179, folio 177 as printed). **"The
chunk gives no basis for identifying or separating them; leave both as printed."**

**I19. Sabir of Kaliyar may be two men, or none.** The editor's footnote leaves open whether the Sabir
of *Sair al-Auliya* and the Sabir buried at Kaliyar are the same person, and records that the later
literature rests on *kashf*; the mosque-collapse and city-destruction narrative has no contemporary
source behind anything the chunk cites (p. 92, folio 80 [marker 90]; chunk 008). **Kaliyar has no
archive row, but the Sabiri nisba attached to contents nos. 54, 56, 59 and 74 descends from this
contested figure.**

**I20. `bari-imam` — nothing in the book places Shah Latif's shrine geographically.** The
identification "rests on the editor's citation of a book titled *Hayat-e Bari Imam* (Rawalpindi) and on
the Pothohar meeting, not on any statement in the text" (pp. 45–46, folios 33–34; chunk 003). The
bibliography confirms the shape of that evidence and no more: item ۲۰۶ "حیات بری امام" is certain but
undated ("س۔ن"), and item ۲۲۱ "شاہ لطیف بری" is **a probable, not a stated, match** (p. 303, folio 291;
chunk 028). The whole re-dating rests on one Persian risala cited three ways — see C6.

**I21. `shrine-of-hafiz-muhammad-jamal-multani` — the index has the name without the title.**
"محمد جمال ملتانی" without "حافظ"; "the ج section of the persons index (outside this chunk) may carry a
'جمال ملتانی' or 'حافظ جمال' entry that is the real match, and Multan has more than one Muhammad
Jamal" (p. 328, folio 316; chunk 031). Evidence: PDF p. 120, and the ج section.

**I22. Khoi Miran and Chah Miran — a philological guess three chunks refused to make into a finding.**
Sain Qutb Shah is buried at "**موضع کھوئی میراں**" (p. 235, folio 223; chunk 022); the editor put the
600 AH Husain Zanjani "مدفون **چاہ میراں**" (p. 196, folio 184; chunk 019), which is the row's location
for `shrine-of-miran-hussain-zanjani-zanjani-sahib`. *Khoi* and *chah* both mean "well", "**so the two
names could denote one Lahore locality in two languages. This is a philological guess, not a finding;
flag it for a human, and map nothing on it.**" The appendix then gives them **different present
locations** — row 46 "میراں حسین پارک چاہ میراں" and row 65 "کوٹھ خواجہ سعید نزد محراب قدیم", while
keeping "موضع کھوئی میراں" as the book's own address for Qutb Shah — which "neither confirms nor
refutes the identity of the two localities; it shows the compiler treated them as two addresses"
(pp. 289–290, folios 277–278; chunk 027). The place index's "چاہ میراں | ۷۳" is a **locality, not the
saint**, and **folio 184 is not in that entry**, so "the index is incomplete or the two spellings
differ"; the same index has "میراں بادشاہ لاہوری" = Ishaq Gazruni, a different Miran of Lahore
(pp. 331, 334; folios 319, 322; chunk 031). Evidence: PDF p. 85.

**I23. `shrine-of-shah-yusaf-gardez` — the book prints no given name.** Entry ۲۴۲ calls him only
"**حضرت شاہ گردیز ملتانی**"; the placement "rests on the nisba and the city, not on a name the chunk
prints", and the row's death year (1150) is (not stated) here (p. 276, folio 264; chunk 026). The index
has "**گردیز شاہی ملتانی | ۲۶۴**" — epithet and city, **no personal name** — and folio 264 falls in
the post-entry Lahore run rather than the Multan block at 146–172 (p. 326, folio 314; chunk 030).
**Not assigned.** Evidence: folio 264 (est. p. 276).

**I24. `mazar-of-bulleh-shah` — two flagged look-alikes a name search will catch.** Entry no. 190 is
headed "**بہلے [OCR?] شاہ مجذوب لاہوری**" — close to بلھے شاہ, but placed at Lahore with no fixed
abode, dated 1265, and saying nothing of Kasur, poetry or a shrine; **not mapped** (p. 246, folio 234;
chunk 024). The index has "**بھلے [OCR?] شاہ لاہوری | ۲۰ ، ۲۳۴**" filed under ج — "which no reading
beginning بھ‍ would be, so the first letter is probably a ج or چ that the transcriber could not settle"
— at different folios from "بلھے شاہ قصوری | ۶۹، ۷۰، ۲۰۷". **"Do not let a search for Bulleh Shah pick
up folio 234"** (pp. 314, 316; folios 302, 304; chunk 029).

**I25. The Uch and Miani Sahib rows that take nothing, and must not be given anything.** Uch:
`tomb-of-baha-al-halim-uch-sharif`, `tomb-of-javindi-bibi`, `tomb-of-ustad-nuriya` — "the chunk names
Uch only as Mir Surkh Bukhari's burial place. **Do not attach the Bukhari genealogy or the five sons to
those rows**" (chunk 015). Three further Uch mazars in the book have no row at all — Rajan Qattal
(827 AH), Nasir al-Din (847 AH), and by origin Shah Jhula's family (chunk 016). Miani Sahib: the four
rows that stand in that graveyard, `wasif-ali-wasif` among them, **take nothing** from entry no. 153 —
"the citable fact is about the **site**: a scholar of Lahore was buried there in 1216 AH. An entry may
use it as context for the burial ground; no row may claim it as its own" (p. 222, folio 210; chunk
021).

**I26. Explicit do-not-map rulings on a shared name, one line each.** Every one of these was written as
a refusal by the chunk worker; each is a trap a name search would fall into.
- `golra-sharif` — "مہر شاہ (**شہاب الدین**) | ۱۷۰" is not Pir Meher Ali Shah; the parenthesis gives
  the personal name (p. 330, folio 318; chunk 031).
- `darbar-ghamkol-sharif-zinda-pir` — "حیات المیر زندہ پیر | ۳۳ ، ۳۸ ، ۵۱ ، ۵۲" with the
  cross-reference "جمال اللہ دیکھیں حیات المیر زندہ پیر", indexed among the Lahore folios; not Khwaja
  Muhammad Qasim, Kohat, d. 1999 (pp. 315, 317; chunk 029).
- `shrine-of-imam-ali-ul-haq` — "امام علی شاہ ترٹ شٹری[OCR?] | ۱۴۳", flagged nisba, "not shown to be"
  the Sialkot row (p. 313, folio 301; chunk 029). And Imam Ali Shah's own shrine, **Makan Sharif
  (Ratar Chhatar), district Gurdaspur**, is in **East Punjab** by the editor's own note and so falls
  outside this archive's geography (chunk 014).
- `shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam` — "ابوالفتح بن عبدالعزیز | ۱۶۰" has a patronymic that is not Rukn
  al-Din's; **not assigned** (p. 312, folio 300; chunk 029).
- `gurdwara-shaheed-ganj-singh-singhnian` — "مزار شہید گنج", contents no. 211, printed p. 252, listed
  among Punjab elders of unproven death date; "may or may not be the same place; nothing in the chunk
  settles it" (p. 20, folio 8; chunk 001).
- `shrine-of-shah-shams-ud-din-sabzwari` — Pir Hadi Rahnuma is "شمسی سید اولاد **شاہ شمس الدین
  تبریزی**"; the nisba printed is **Tabrizi, not Sabzwari**, and the chunk says nothing about Multan
  (p. 267, folio 255; chunk 026).
- `langer-makhdoom` — "مقبرہ شیخ برہان الدین **بلخی**" is "خواجہ قطب الدین بختیار کے مزار کے پاس" in
  **Delhi**; shared name-element, different city (p. 237, folio 225; chunk 023).
- `shrine-of-makhdoom-abdul-rahim-girhori` — three Abd al-Latifs, "all glossed as someone's father or
  grandfather and none a Kazmi or a Bhittai" (p. 322, folio 310; chunk 030).
- `mazar-e-iqbal` — "محمد اقبال مجددی | ۲۳" is the **edition's editor**, not Allama Iqbal (p. 327,
  folio 315; chunk 030).
- `valmik-mandir-naqi-road` — appendix rows 23–26 at "عقب برکت علی محمڈن ہال **نزد مندر بالمیکاں**";
  the row is on Naqi Road and the appendix names no road. "A temple of the same dedication in the same
  city; whether it is this one is (not stated). Worth a human's look given the shared-ground work, but
  not a match" (p. 288, folio 276; chunk 027).
- `jagannath-temple-sialkot` — "جاگ ناتھ | ۹۳" in the place index is unexplained, "a place, a temple,
  or a person misfiled"; **not to be mapped** (p. 333, folio 321; chunk 031).
- `kalat-kali-temple` — Qalat appears only as the residence of "حاجی احمد ساکن قلات"; the row is a
  Hindu temple there and "wholly unrelated" (p. 156, folio 144; chunk 014).
- `loh-temple-lava-temple` — the Fort holds 29 relics "علیحدہ مکان میں" and the chunk "says nothing of
  a temple or of any other structure in the Fort; no mapping is proposed" (p. 259, folio 247; chunk
  025).
- `samadhi-of-maharaja-ranjit-singh` — the index "names the man, not the monument"
  ("رنجیت سنگھ | ۱۵ ، ۶۵ ، ۲۴۳", p. 318; chunk 029), and the relic material is about his acquisition
  and loss of a collection, not the samadhi: "a human should decide whether the row wants context of
  this kind" (pp. 257–258; chunk 025). The "nine years" prophecy is the author's report of a saying,
  **not a dated event** (p. 245, folio 233; chunk 023).
- `darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz` — appendix row 44, "**شیخ ایاز لاہوری** قدس سرہ | اندر شہر لاہور |
  **چوک رنگ محل**", gives a saint's honorific, no "ملک", no date, and a different landmark from the
  row's Shah Alam Market: "could be the same site; the chunk gives no ground to say so. Left
  unmatched" (p. 289, folio 277; chunk 027). And **the persons index does not name Malik Ayaz at
  all** as transcribed — the م section runs "ملا شاہ بدخشی" straight into "ملکو بی بی", where he would
  fall (p. 330, folio 318; chunk 031).
- `shrine-of-shah-inayat-qadiri` — Shah Gada's fourteen-step Shattari line supplies the doctrinal
  content of the row's "Qadri Shattari" label, but "**Shah Inayat is nowhere in this chunk**" and Shah
  Gada's own grave is not given; nothing may enter the row from there except the chain, cited as this
  book's account of the Shattari silsila — and **four names in that chain are flagged or unresolved, so
  it cannot be published as transcribed** (pp. 210, 212; folios 198, 200; chunk 020).
- `ganj-e-inayat-sarkar` and `mohra-sharif-khanqah` — do not attach Khawand Mahmud (Lahore, 1052 AH)
  "on the strength of the shared Naqshbandi label — those rows' figures died in 2011 and are unnamed
  here" (chunk 012).
- `shrine-of-baba-shah-kamal`, `shrine-of-mian-mir` — do not attach the unplaced Lahore graves of
  chunks 009 and 010 (Shah Kaku outside Delhi Gate, Jan Allah at Bagh Maharan Singh[OCR?], Abd
  al-Karim by Chauburji[OCR?], Abd al-Khaliq unlocated, Muhammad Arif 1071 AH, Muhammad Siddiq 1084 AH,
  Muhammad Salim 1151 AH) "on the strength of a shared city and a Chishti-Sabiri nisba". They belong
  on a candidate list.

**I27. Unplaced probables the contents alone cannot settle (chunk 001, pp. 15–21, folios 3–9).**
- "**شاہ سلیمان قادری**" (no. 26, printed p. 54) could be Shah Sulaiman Nuri of `purana-bhalwal`
  (Qadiri) or, less likely given the chapter, the Sulaiman of
  `darbar-hazrat-khawaja-shah-muhammad-sulaiman-taunsvi-r-a`; and "**خواجہ محمد سلیمان چشتی**" (no. 78,
  printed p. 111) sits in the Chishti chapter where Taunsvi would belong **but is not named Taunsvi**
  (pp. 15, 17; folios 3, 5).
- Contents entry no. 191 groups **six women in a single line** —
  "بی بی حاج۔ بی بی تاج۔ بی بی نور۔ بی بی حُور۔ بی بی گوہر۔ بی بی شہباز", printed p. 235.
  "Six bibis in one entry invites comparison with `bibi-pak-daman` ('the six Bibis'), but the chunk
  never names Pak Daman and **the identification must not be made from the contents alone**" (p. 20,
  folio 8).
- Entry no. 11's name differs between entry and contents: "شیخ **ابواسحاق** قادری لاہوری" (p. 50, folio
  38) against "شیخ **ابوالحق** قادری" at contents no. 11. Entry no. 18 confirms the entry-heading form,
  "so the contents reading is the likelier error — **but the archive should not silently correct
  either**" (chunk 004).
- Eight name-forms in the p. 32 list differ from the contents — مائی بھاگ / مائی بھاگی; شیخ لدھی
  شاہ [OCR?] / شیخ لدھے شاہ; پیر بھولا / پیر بھجو; مستقیم شاہ فیض پوری / مستقیم شاہ لاہوری; جلی شاہ /
  جھلے شاہ مجذوب; شیخ موسیٰ کھوکھر / شیخ موسیٰ اکھو کھر; شیخ گھلن شاہ [OCR?] / شیخ کھلن شاہ سرمست
  قادری; and **سید صوف [OCR?], which has no counterpart in the contents at all**. "Any of these may be
  the transcription rather than the book; check against the entries themselves" (p. 32, folio 20;
  chunk 002).

---

### 3. Numerals and dates needing a look at the page image (102 items)

*Every date, amount, count and age carrying `[OCR?]`, in PDF page order so the reader opens each image
once. The project's own research note records that numbers stay the persistent risk even where
aggregate accuracy is good, and this book's own workers said the same in eleven separate chunks.
Flagged **citation page-numbers, folio references and shelfmarks** are a different failure — a
reference that cannot be followed rather than a fact that may be wrong — and are listed in Apparatus
item A27, also grouped by page, so a single pass over an image can clear both. Items N101 and N102 are
class-level and govern dozens of dates each; read them first.*

### 4. Illegible and lost (24 items)

*`[illegible]` spans, the plates and tables that did not survive transcription, and the pages the
workers reported as garbled, dislocated or duplicated. Items L1–L10 are whole documents: each needs a
human at the page image before anything is written from it.*

**L1. The Mujaddid's autograph letter — twenty-five lines lost.** The plate at p. 131 (folio 119)
carries "[illegible: 1 line]" and "[illegible: 24 lines]" of what the caption calls a letter of the
Mujaddid in his own hand, addressed to an "[illegible]" recipient. **Only the caption survives:**
"ماخوذ از عرفانیات باقی [OCR?] مرتبہ نظام الدین احمد کاظمی حیرت۔ مطبوعہ دہلی ۱۹۷۰ء". "**A human must
re-read this page from the PDF before anything is said about the letter**, including who it was
addressed to" (chunk 012).

**L2. The Qasuri plate — the caption and the plate do not describe the same document.** The footnote
announces "حضرت قصوریؒ کی سند بخاری کا عکس", a facsimile of the **Bukhari sanad** (p. 151, folio 139),
but the plate's Arabic text is a **fiqh passage on apostates and inheritance**, closing
"هو المختصر وعليه المفتى", with a colophon naming Qasuri as the copyist and a marginal "۱۲۳۷ [OCR?]" —
which reads as a page of his *Risala Ilm-e Mirath*, not a hadith sanad. "Either the edition carries two
plates, or the caption and the plate are mismatched, or the transcription has merged them. **A human
must re-read pp. 151–153 from the PDF before anything is said about either document.**" Twenty-two
lines of the plate's margins are "[illegible]", and nine further phrases in its Arabic are flagged
(p. 153, folio 141; chunk 014).

**L3. The Shah Wali Allah ijazat-nama — six lines lost, grantee unknown.** p. 218 (folio 206) carries
"[illegible: 6 lines]" under a caption identifying an ijazat-nama **in Shah Wali Allah's own hand**
from the **Khuda Bakhsh Library, Patna** — with two OCR flags in the caption itself. "A human must
re-read this page from the PDF, **in particular the grantee's name**" (chunk 021).

**L4. The Bukhari sanad plate of p. 224 (folio 212) — four gaps in a hadith chain.** Partly legible
Arabic with **four "[illegible]" gaps** and three OCR flags, including on its own heading. Its grantee
is named "مولوي غلام محي الدين صاحب [OCR?]" and may or may not be the collector cited throughout these
chunks — see I16 (chunk 021).

**L5. The Bukhari sanad plate of p. 225 (folio 213) — the seal's text is lost.** "[image: seal
impression] [illegible]", the caption's final figure "۳۹۳ [OCR?]" flagged in both chunks where it
appears, one "[illegible]" near the opening and one flagged transmitter's name ("احمد الشناوي
[OCR?]"). "This is the only plate in these chunks whose document can largely be read. **A human should
still verify it against the PDF, since it is a hadith chain and a single dropped name would corrupt
it**" (chunk 022).

**L6. The Shah Muhammad Ghaus facsimile — three lines wholly gone.** "مزار بزرگ و ولی **[illegible]**
شاہ غوث [OCR?]" and the date "ذی قعدہ [OCR?] **[illegible]**" are broken, **three lines are wholly
illegible**, and the owner's residence in the attestation is lost. Every substantive word of the
facsimile's Arabic prayer is flagged (p. 79, folio 67 as printed; chunk 007).

**L7. The manuscript leaf of p. 74 (folio 62) — twenty lines and the whole caption.** "The caption
line of the facsimile and the twenty illegible lines of the manuscript leaf itself"; and **every
element of the caption is flagged** — "عکس تحریر حضرت شاہ رضا قادری [OCR?]",
"کتاب مطالع الطالبین [OCR?]", "تالیف شیخ محمد رضا لاہوری [OCR?]", "اسلام آباد [OCR?]". The caption
attributes *Matali al-Talibin* to Shaikh Muhammad Raza Lahori while the entry's own footnote names his
work *Irshad al-Ashiqin*: "both may be his, but **nothing in the caption can be relied on**" (chunk
006).

**L8. The Muhammad Baqir plate — eight lines, nothing recoverable.** "[illegible: 8 lines]" — "**nothing
of the document's text can be given**". Its owner's name is flagged twice, "مولانا **عبدالرشد [OCR?]**۔
مالک **نہر کرنو [OCR?]**" (p. 284) and "مولانا **عبدالرشید سیالکوٹی [OCR?]**" (p. 285), as is
"**حررہ [OCR?]** محمد اقبال مجددی" (pp. 284–285, folios 272–273; chunk 027).

**L9. The Mian Kalu plate — the ownership note lost.** "[illegible: 3 lines]" and
"مالک ایں کتاب **[illegible]**"; the caption's pedigree and the seal legend with its date ۱۱۸۸ھ are
clear (p. 286, folio 274; chunk 027).

**L10. The three Wazir Khan imamate deeds — almost everything.** p. 338: "[illegible: 3 lines]" and
"[illegible: 1 line, signature and date]"; p. 339: "[illegible: 9 lines]"; p. 340: "[illegible: 3
lines]" twice, "[illegible: 8 lines]", "[illegible: 2 lines]". "**Everything said about the documents
rests on the editor's typeset summary (p. 337), not on the plates.** A human reading of the plates
against the PDF is the only way to recover the witnesses' names, the year of the third deed and the
'تقسیم' clause." Note also that **p. 338 carries no folio marker at all** — it is a full-page plate,
folio 326 on the relation, and "the page cannot be cited by folio" (pp. 337–340, folios 325–328;
chunk 031).

**L11. The one word that would locate a grave in Lahore.** "مدفن شریف شہر لاہور میں **[illegible]**
خانقاہ کے اندر واقع ہے" — Bibi Jamal Khatun's khanqah. "The one word that would locate a grave in
Lahore is the one that could not be read", and the editor's own quotation says she never left Sehwan
(p. 255, folio 243; chunk 024). Bears on **`shrine-of-mian-mir`**; see B17.

**L12. The pir who rebuilt Shaikh Tahir's mazar.** "جناب پیر **[illegible]** گیلانی مدظلہ" — "the
rebuilding is attributable only to a Gilani pir living at the time of the 1974 introduction" (p. 59,
folio 47, حاشیہ 2; chunk 005). Bears on **`tahir-bandagi-qadri`**.

**L13. Sayyid Hasan Peshawari's replacement genealogy.** The editor rejects the author's chain outright
and his replacement is partly illegible: "سید **[illegible]** ین احمد… سید شرف الد**[illegible]**
یحییٰ… سید عبد اللہ **[illegible]**". "**Neither the author's chain nor the corrected one can be used
whole**" (p. 72, folio 60, حاشیہ 3; chunk 006).

**L14. The Mulla Shah apparatus.** Illegible: the second entry in the list of seven manuscript tafsirs;
the page numbers in the *Sakinat al-Auliya* and *Safinat al-Auliya* citations; the journal reference
"کالج می**[illegible]** اگست ۱۹۳۷ء"; and "احوال و کر**[illegible]**" in the *Sahibiya* citation
(pp. 69–70, folios 57–58; chunk 006).

**L15. Two shorter losses in running prose.** "عظماء فقرائے **[illegible]**" in entry no. 35 (p. 76,
folio 74 as printed; chunk 006); and "انھوں نے **[illegible]** جا کر قیام کیا" in the Bhopal posting —
"the destination is given in the preceding clause, so the loss is probably harmless, **but the sentence
cannot be quoted whole**" (p. 149, folio 137; chunk 014).

**L16. `shrine-of-mian-mir` — the appendix's landmark is illegible.** "میاں میر نزد **[illegible]**
ریلوے سٹیشن" — "the name of the railway station cannot be given" (p. 287, folio 275; chunk 027).

**L17. Two empty footnote markers, one of them load-bearing.** "حاشیہ: **د۔ک**" stands alone before the
nine-item source list at p. 169 (folio 157): "its content, if any, is lost to the transcription"
(chunk 016). And "حاشیہ: **۲۔ دارا شکوہ:**" at p. 196 (folio 184) "consists of the name and a colon.
**Whatever reference or comment stood there is lost, and it is the note that would have sourced
[`data-darbar`]'s best-known votive practice** — the forty Thursdays. A human must re-read this page"
(chunk 019).

**L18. Citations with empty folio slots — missing, not merely illegible.**
"محمد عمر چمکنی: ظواہر قلمی **ورق** " and "**ظواہر ورق**" — "the manuscript leaf numbers are missing
from the transcription" (p. 213, folio 201; chunk 020). These are the references that would settle
I12, **`shrine-of-mian-umar-baba-chamkani`**.

**L19. Entry no. 124 is dislocated across two pages — every Shah Daula reference is affected.** As
transcribed, p. 186 carries the heading and "پنجاب کے مشہور بزرگوں میں سے یہ بزرگ صاحب مقامات بلند و
مدارج" and then, immediately, "ان کو اولاد حاصل ہو۔ ارشاد ہوتا کہ…"; p. 184 begins
"ارجمند و خوارق و کرامت…" and ends "…استدعائے دعا کرتے کہ خدا کی جناب سے". **The joins are exact at
both ends**, so the reading order is **p. 186 (first line) → p. 184 → p. 186 (remainder)**, and entry
no. 124's body stands *before* entry no. 123 in the page sequence. "**Every page reference to Shah
Daula in these notes is therefore split between pp. 184 and 186 as transcribed, and a human must
establish the true pagination before any of it is cited**" (chunk 017). See B19.

**L20. A clause lost at a page break, possibly in the printing.** p. 272 ends "چونکہ یہ" and p. 273
opens "ربیع الاول سنہ ۱۲۱۹ھ (۱) میں یہ فوت ہوا". "**The 'چونکہ' clause has no completion**, so whatever
the author said about this saint between the karamat sentence and the death date is missing from the
chunk — possibly a printed-page loss rather than a transcription gap. A reviewer should check the
facsimile" (folios 260–261; chunk 026). Entry ۲۱۷ is likewise **split by its own footnote block**: its
text runs "فیض آپ کا" at the foot of p. 266 and resumes "بہت جاری تھا" at the head of p. 267, with the
two حاشیہ lines printed between — **the heading is on the first page and the death year on the second**
(chunk 026).

**L21. A colophon with text omitted between the chain and the death formula.** The
**`garh-maharaja-shorkot`** colophon carries two runs of dots, "۔۔۔۔۔۔۔۔" and "۔۔۔۔", "so text is
omitted between the chain and the death formula; **whether by the editor or the transcriber is not
determinable**". Its opening link is unreadable too: "حضرت سلطان باہو **نہ [OCR?]** تلقین از سید عبد
الرحمان" — "the word between the saint and his first shaikh is unreadable; the rest of the ten-link
chain is unflagged" (p. 265, folio 253; chunk 025).

**L22. Entries and footnotes that break off, so the row cannot be written from the chunk alone.**
Entry no. 106's tail opens mid-entry at p. 168 with **its heading in the previous chunk** (chunk 016);
entry no. 125 breaks off at the word "**وفات**", leaving Shaikh Jan Muhammad Suhrawardi Lahori with no
death year and no grave (p. 186; chunk 017); entry no. 140 (Nur al-Din Wali) and the
"بی بیاں پاکدامن" cross-reference both run past their chunk (chunk 019); entry no. 147 leaves Haji
Muhammad Saeed with no death year and no grave (chunk 020); entry no. 156 leaves Shah Abd al-Aziz
Dehlvi with no dates, no works and no grave (chunk 021); entry no. 160 ends with a teaching timetable
and **his death is mentioned only in entry no. 165** — "do not supply a year from elsewhere" (p. 229;
chunk 022); entry no. 188 is cut at p. 245 with no death year, no burial and the outcome of the
summons before "**میجر میگریگر [OCR?] صاحب**" in the next chunk — "the officer's name is flagged;
**do not normalise it to any known officer**" (chunk 023); entry ۲۴۳ breaks off after
"صاحب شریعت و طریقت و علم و فضل" (p. 276; chunk 026). Three footnotes run across page breaks
("باقی بر صفحہ آئندہ", "بقیہ حاشیہ"), and **the `data-darbar` death-year argument is one of them**:
"it must be read whole in chunk 019 before anything is written from it" (p. 195; chunk 018). The
Sultan Bahu source list likewise runs from p. 265 into p. 266 (chunks 025–026).

**L23. Passages the workers would not gloss, and chronograms they would not quote.** The Persian
*Khazinat al-Asfiya* extract at p. 179 (folio 177 as printed) is not glossed by the book: "the
translation offered in these notes is a working rendering of the parts that carry facts; **the full
passage should be translated by a human before it is quoted at length**" (chunk 017). The three
chronograms for **`data-darbar`** are partly unreadable — "ماہ ہجویرے [OCR?]", "لامکانے [OCR?]" and
"**چو لبد و اد [OCR?]** سرور و سرخیل ابرار", "and its line is the one whose numeral is printed as 465
without a legible chronogrammatic phrase. **Do not quote the verses; quote only the numeral and the
entry's prose year**" (p. 195; chunk 018). Also not to be quoted: Mian Mir's chronogram
("دشگیر [OCR?]", p. 62), the Mujaddid's ("بابر دیگر [OCR?]", p. 130), Alim Allah's
("قطب الحق(۹)[OCR?]", p. 119), Baha al-Din Zakariya's ("ستحریر [OCR?]", p. 161), Qasuri's
("ہم سجان [OCR?]", p. 154 — where three phrases yield ۱۲۰۲ھ and six yield ۱۲۷۰ھ, "so the chronogram
adds no independent evidence"), Miran Bhikha's ("تو کید او[OCR?]", p. 113), Sayyid Ghulam Muhyi
al-Din's ("چُگل [OCR?]", p. 229), entry ۱۶۸'s ("بجد [OCR?] ای شع [OCR?]", p. 234 — "the verse should
not be quoted"), and the *Masnavi* couplet at p. 211, "مثنوی ای ضیا را [OCR?] الحق حسام الدین بیا",
where "the flagged words fall exactly where the *Masnavi*'s own opening invocation would stand".
The chronogram at p. 38 (folio 26) encodes the year of composition but **the chunk prints no resulting
numeral, so no date can be read off it** (chunk 003).

**L24. Six relic objects whose nature cannot be stated, and four pages that cannot be cited at all.**
Flagged on the descriptive word: "تعویذ **صدور صد خاص [OCR?]**", "دستار کہنہ **بر لیسی [OCR?]** نما",
"ابر **ایشمی [OCR?]** نما" (p. 259), "**مہم آلفی [OCR?]**", "**فرہ [OCR?]** علم مبارک حضرت عباس"
(p. 260), "ایک **منجر [OCR?]** کپڑا" (p. 261). "The counts and the attributions are unaffected; **the
nature of these six objects cannot be stated**" (chunk 025). And at the end of the volume, pp. 357–360
are transcribed as `[blank page]` and pp. 353–356 and 361 as `[back matter]`: "**none of these six
pages can be cited by folio, and the four blanks carry nothing to verify**" (chunk 032).

---

### 5. Internal contradictions in the book itself (45 items)

*Where neither author nor editor is obviously right, or where the editor corrects one figure and
leaves an arithmetic he created. None of these is resolved here. The blocking cases that also touch an
archive row are in Group 1 and are cross-referenced, not repeated.*

**C1. The base edition is stated two ways inside the same apparatus.** The preface gives the source
text as "مطبوعہ لکھنؤ (۱۹۰۶ء)", printed at **Lucknow** 1906 (p. 14, folio 2), while the list of
editions gives no. 4 as Munshi Nawal Kishore **Lucknow 1899** and no. 5 as Munshi Nawal Kishore
**Kanpur 1906**, and says the text is based on that last one (p. 35, folio 23). **"The place of the
1906 printing is therefore stated two ways inside the same book's apparatus"** (chunk 002).

**C2. The number of entries.** The editor states the book holds **۲۴۴ [OCR?]** sufis of Punjab (p. 31,
folio 19) while the table of contents numbers its biographical entries to **247** (p. 21, folio 9).
The figure carries an `[OCR?]` flag, "so it may be a transcription artefact rather than a real
discrepancy — **but it must be resolved by counting before either number is cited**" (chunks 001,
002). Chunk 027 shows entries **244 to 247** falling on pp. 277–279, and chunk 032's final annotation
is headed **شمارہ ۳۰۸** (p. 351) — see A22.

**C3. Where *Khazinat al-Asfiya* was printed.** The author says twice, "لاہور و دہلی" — **Lahore and
Delhi** (p. 37, folio 25) — while the editor's own print history names Lahore 1284 AH, Lucknow
۱۳۰۴ء[OCR?], Kanpur 1312 AH and 1902 CE, and **no Delhi printing at all** (p. 30, folio 18) (chunks
002, 003).

**C4. The date of *Haqiqat al-Fuqara*, and its printing.** Given as **1041 AH**, in Persian verse
(p. 54, folio 42, حاشیہ 5, and p. 57, folio 45) and as "**۱۰۷۱ھ/۱۶۶۰ء**" (p. 65, folio 53, حاشیہ 2).
"**The editor's own argument about the silence of the sources up to 1096 AH depends on which date is
right.**" Its printing is likewise doubled: published by the Majlis-e Shah Husain Lahore in **1966** in
one footnote, and "پیر محمد: حقیقت الفقرا ۱۰۴۱ھ لاہور **۱۹۶۵ء**" in the source list (chunks 004, 005).
This is the book on which everything about Madho rests, and which the editor elsewhere calls careless
and uncritical — see B14.

**C5. The date of *Tazkira-e Muqimi*.** **1172 AH** at p. 45 (folio 33) and p. 47 (folio 35); **1177
AH** here, twice (pp. 63–64, folios 51–52). "**Both are the same Rawalpindi Ganj Bakhsh manuscript by
Ahmad Ali Astarabadi**" (chunks 003, 005).

**C6. The Persian risala behind Bari Imam's re-dating is cited three ways, by three workers.**
"مطبوعہ پشاور **۱۲۸۴ھ**" (p. 46, folio 34; chunk 003); "مطبوعہ **لیث ور [OCR?] ۱۲۸۳ھ**" (p. 73, folio
61; chunk 006); "مطبوعہ **پشاور ۱۲۸۳ھ [OCR?]**" (p. 78, folio 66 as printed; chunk 007). "The year and
the place both differ… **This matters because the same risala is the evidence for Shah Latif Bari's
re-dating**" — and "all three refer to the same Persian risala".

**C7. The editor gives Shaikh Adam Banuri two death years, in two footnotes of one entry.** Footnote 3
says "ف **۱۰۵۴ھ**" (p. 139, folio 127); footnote 1 on the next page, attached to his death at Medina,
says "بسال **۱۰۵۲ھ** (بدرالدین سرہندی : حضرات القدس دفتر دوم ص ۳۵۶)" (p. 140, folio 128). "**Two years
apart, unremarked, both from the editor's own hand**" (chunk 013).

**C8. The editor gives Muin al-Din's arrival at Lahore two dates, three pages apart, in two arguments
that both depend on it.** "قریباً **۵۸۵ھ**" (p. 198, حاشیہ 1, citing *Tarikh al-Salaf* 7, 9) and
"ورود لاہور حدود **۵۸۸**" (p. 200, حاشیہ 2). "Neither is flagged; the difference is three years and
does not affect either conclusion, **but it should not be quoted as a single figure**" (chunk 019).

**C9. Bahawal Sher Hujrawi lived 250 years and nobody draws the inference.** Said to have lived **250
years**, to have been born at Baghdad, and to have died 18 Shawwal 973 AH. "On that arithmetic he
would have been born around 723 AH, and the genealogy makes him the twelfth generation from Abdul
Qadir Jilani (d. 6th century AH). **The author states the age as a mark of distinction and draws no
inference from it; neither does the editor correct it**" (pp. 46–47, folios 34–35; chunk 003). His
genealogy is separately contested: *Tazkira-e Muqimi* has فتح اللہ where the text has مسیح الدین
(p. 46, folio 34, حاشیہ 1, citing ms p. ۴ [OCR?]).

**C10. Entry no. 106's subject lived 160 years, and the book says no Suhrawardi elder did.** Birth
"۵۷۷ھ [OCR?]", death 22 Rabi al-Awwal 737 AH, age **160**, mazar at "موضع **موعلاۃ [OCR?]** ملتان".
"The stated arithmetic is internally consistent (577 + 160 = 737) but 160 lunar years is
extraordinary, and **the book itself says no Suhrawardi elder attained such an age — which is an
assertion of the claim, not a defence of it**." The heading naming this saint falls in the previous
chunk, and the place name is unresolved (p. 168, folio 156; chunk 016).

**C11. Shaikh Musa Ahangar — the editor corrects the death year from the same book that supplies the
birth year, and the result is a 144-year life.** Text: **925 AH**. Editor: Thursday 18 Safar **962
AH** from *Manaqib-e Musawi* p. 63, with the text's year called *ghalat*. Editor's birth, in the very
next footnote: 27 Rajab **818 AH [OCR?]** from *Manaqib-e Musawi* p. 15. "818 → 962 is **about 144
lunar years**; 818 → 925 is about 107. **The editor corrects the death year from the same book that
supplies the birth year and does not remark that the result is a 144-year life.** Record all three
figures, the OCR flag on the birth year, and this arithmetic; **do not present 962 AH as settled**"
(p. 175, folio 163; chunk 016). Compounding it, *Manaqib-e Musawi*'s own date is given as ۱۱۵۲ھ
(p. 175) and "۱۱۵۷ ھ [OCR?]" (p. 176) **in the very footnotes carrying the correction**.

**C12. The Mujaddid — the age the text states supports the editor's year, not the text's own.** Text:
"سلخ ماہ صفر سال ایک ہزار پینتیس", the last day of Safar **1035**, with age sixty-three. Chronogram:
**۱۰۳۵**. Editor: **1034 AH**. "Against a birth of 971 AH 'باقوال صحیح', 1034 gives sixty-three lunar
years and 1035 gives sixty-four — so **the age the text states supports the editor's year, not the
text's own. Record all three and the arithmetic**" (p. 130, folio 118; chunk 012).

**C13. Muhammad Saeed's stated age contradicts the editor's dates by fifteen years.** Text: died 1070
AH "اور عمر **اسّی** برس کی", aged eighty (p. 135, folio 123); editor: birth 1005 AH, death 27 Jumada
al-Thani 1070 AH (p. 134, folio 122, حاشیہ 1), which is **sixty-five**. "**The editor does not remark
on the age. Do not carry 'eighty' without this note**" (chunk 012).

**C14. Mian Mir's birth year is given three ways in three lines.** Text: "سال نوسو ساٹھ" (**960**) on
the authority of *Safinat al-Auliya*, with death 1045 AH and age 87 — "which requires a birth in
**958**" — while the editor's footnote gives **957 AH / 1550 CE** from *Sakinat al-Auliya*. "**Sixty
years at Lahore from the age of twenty-five also implies 85, not 87**" (pp. 61–62, folios 49–50;
chunk 005). His siblings are named differently by text and footnote: the brother "**میاں قاضی**"
against "**قاضی بولن**", the sister "**بی بی بادی**" against "**بی بی باجی**".

**C15. Miran Bhikha's stated age does not match his stated dates, and the editor corrects the wrong
thing.** Born 7 Rajab **1046**, died 5 Ramadan **1131**, "عمر شریف **چوراسی** برس کی" — eighty-four.
"1131 − 1046 is eighty-five Hijri years, and the death month falls after the birth month, so the
completed age is eighty-five. Either the age or one of the years is out by one; **the editor corrects
only the birth *date*, not the arithmetic**" — and that correction is itself a dispute:
"مولانا لطف اللہ نے تاریخ ولادت **نہم** رجب لکھی ہے", the ninth of Rajab (*Thamarat* p. 23), "**and
Lutf Allah is the pupil who wrote the biography the editor uses throughout**" (pp. 112–113, folios
100–101; chunk 010).

**C16. Mirza Jan-e Janan — his age and the editor's birth year cannot both stand.** Text: born 11
Ramadan **1111**, died 9 Muharram **1195**, "چوراسی برس کی عمر پائی", aged eighty-four — "which the
text's own figures support". The editor prefers **1110 AH** for the birth, "which gives eighty-five.
**One or the other**"; the thesis reference behind it is itself flagged ("ص ۱۲ [OCR?]") (p. 144, folio
132, حاشیہ 5; chunk 013). Note also that the killers and the means of his assassination rest on three
flagged words — "عقیدہ نجدیہ [OCR?]" (their creed), "بضرب [OCR?]" (the blow), "بگلولہ [OCR?] تفنگ"
(the musket ball): "the date and the fact of martyrdom are unflagged; **the identity of the killers
and the means are not. Do not attribute the killing to a named movement from this page**".

**C17. Shah Abu Said's age implies a birth year the book never states, and the interval to his
successor does not close.** "پچون سال حضرت کی عمر تھی", fifty-five, against a death in 1250 AH,
"which implies c. 1195 AH — a year the chunk never states" (p. 148, folio 136). Shah Rauf Ahmad is
said to have outlived him by "صرف اڑھائی برس", only two and a half years, with his own death in 1253
and Abu Said's on Eid 1250: "workable only if the Eid is Eid al-Fitr and the death falls early in
1253; **do not compute a month from these two statements**" (pp. 148–149, folios 136–137; chunk 014).

**C18. A correction that is itself internally impossible.** Nizam al-Din's birth: text **634 AH**; the
footnote says Amir Khurd recorded "**۵۳۶ھ [OCR?] / ۱۲۳۸ء**" and that the Mufti's 634 is therefore not
correct. "**But 536 AH is not 1238 CE — 1238 CE falls in 636 AH** — so the footnote's Hijri numeral is
almost certainly a transcription of ۶۳۶, in which case the correction differs from the text by two
years, not a century. **Do not carry either figure without this note**" (p. 94, folio 82 as printed,
حاشیہ 3; chunk 008).

**C19. Hamid Qari dies before he is born, as printed.** Born "سنہ **ایک ہزار اکتر [OCR?]**" (1071),
died "چودہ جمادی الثانی سنہ **ایک ہزار چھیاسٹھ**" (1066); the entry opens "عہد محمد شاہ بادشاہ میں";
the editor says "**بارہویں صدی ہجری**". "**At least one numeral is wrong; the birth is flagged, the
death is not, and neither is consistent with the editor's century. Unusable until read from the PDF**"
(p. 278, folio 266, and حاشیہ 2; chunk 027).

**C20. A tomb built about 250 years after its occupant died, uncorrected.** Entry ۲۱۸: the tomb
"**بابر شاہ کے عہد میں تعمیر ہوا**" but the saint died "**سنہ چھ سو اکیاسی**" (681). "Either the year
is wrong, or the era is not the one the reader assumes, or the tomb is much later than the burial.
**The chunk offers no reconciliation and no footnote corrects it. Nothing here may be used as a death
date**" (p. 267, folio 255; chunk 026).

**C21. Karam Shah's death year is given twice in one sentence, differently.**
"سال ایک ہزار دو سو میں … شہید ہوا" and "**۱۲۰۱ھ** اس کا سال وفات ہے" — **1200 and 1201 AH**, and
"the editor does not remark on it". His nisba is flagged ("ہنکاری [OCR?]"), as is the phrase describing
the Sikhs ("غارت گر ملا [OCR?] قوم سکھ"): "the flagged word sits inside the only sentence in these
chunks about Sikh-era plundering, so **the characterisation itself should be re-read from the PDF
before it is quoted**" (p. 190, folio 188 as printed; chunk 018).

**C22. Sayyid Muhammad Ghaus Uchi's sons are four in the text and three in his source.** The text
names four — Abdul Qadir Sani, Abdullah Rabbani, Mubarak Haqqani, Muhammad Nurani — while the footnote
quotes Abdul Haq Dehlvi for three, with descendants known as Mir Miran resident at Lahore (p. 41,
folio 29, and حاشیہ 1; chunk 003).

**C23. One footnote gives Sayyid Abdul Qadir Sani's family two irreconcilable versions.** Two sons
(Shaikh Abdul Razzaq, d. 5 Jumada al-Akhir ۹۴۲ھ [OCR?], and Sayyid Zain al-Abidin) per
*Akhbar al-Akhyar* p. 193, **against seven sons** per Sharafat Naushahi's *Hadaiq al-Anwar*, ms
p. ۳۵ [OCR?]. His age at death is given as ۸۷ [OCR?] years (p. 43, folio 31, حاشیہ 1; chunk 003).

**C24. The date of his arrival at Uch is disputed in the apparatus, and the chunk refuses to close
it.** The author narrates the migration without a year; the footnote gives **887 AH** on Ali Asghar
Gilani's authority, via *Khazinat al-Asfiya* 1/116 citing *Shajarat al-Anwar*. "Against a death in 923
AH that leaves 36 years at Uch, during which Sikandar Lodhi (named as his murid) did reign — **but the
book does not make that argument and the reader should not either**" (p. 40, folio 28, حاشیہ 2; chunk
003).

**C25. The author's own family pedigree is contested inside the family's literature and repeats its
names.** Footnote 1 records that Mufti Mahmud Alam made Khwaja Muhammad Ayub Lahori a son of Mufti
Muhammad Taqi and put him in the family tree, "**جو درست نہیں**" (which is not correct), since Ghulam
Sarwar himself called him Muhammad Taqi's pupil and son-in-law (خزینہ ۲/۳۷۰); and that in Ghulam
Sarwar's own account **the name of Mufti Muhammad Taqi is missing after Mufti Rahim Allah** (p. 24,
folio 12, حاشیہ 1). Footnote 5 rejects Mahmud Alam's naming of Mufti Abdul Salam's father as Qazi
Mufti Muhammad Tahir in favour of Shaikh Mufti Inayat Allah (p. 26, folio 14). "**The chain to
Bahauddin Zakariya is thus contested in detail even where the editor accepts it as a whole**" (chunk
002). The genealogy also prints "مفتی حافظ محمد تقی بن ۱ **مفتی محمد تقی**" (p. 24) while the separate
paragraph on حافظ محمد تقی makes him the son of "**حافظ مفتی محمد تقی [OCR?]**" (p. 26) — "i.e. as
transcribed, of a man of the same name. **One of the two is likely an OCR duplication; the death years
(1146 AH [OCR?] and 1131 AH) are all that separate them**." The line then gains an extra generation in
chunk 021: the editor's version has Hafiz Rahmat Allah ← **Mufti Muhammad Naqi [OCR?]** ← Mufti
Muhammad Taqi[OCR?] against chunk 020's author-voice version without it — "**an extra generation, and
both names flagged. Do not reconstruct the line from these two passages**" (p. 222, folio 210).
Chunk 027 adds a third instance of the same collision at p. 277, and chunk 031 a fourth in the index
("محمد تقی بن محمد **لفتی [OCR?]** | ۲۲۰"). Also unexplained: the index calls **Imam Bakhsh** the
author's "جد" at folios 70, 98, 99, which does not fit the pedigree chunk 022 records — "**maternal
grandfather, a looser use of 'جد', or another Ghulam Sarwar Lahori** — the chunk does not say" (p. 313;
chunk 029).

**C26. An impossible-looking generation span the chain itself cannot check.** Mufti Abdul Salam
(d. 1031 AH) is made a great-great-grandson of Mian Kalan, who died 891 AH, "across four generations
in 140 years — arithmetically possible, but the chain as printed **also places Mufti Muhammad Mahmud
and Maulana Burhan al-Din between Abdul Salam and Mufti Abdul Sami without dates, so no generation
length can be checked. Flagged only so that no one derives a date from the chain**" (pp. 26–27, folios
14–15; chunk 002).

**C27. A khalifa predeceases his pir by the book's own dates.** Nur Muhammad Thani Naruwala died
**1204 AH** while his pir Nur Muhammad Maharvi died **1205 AH** — "possible, but the text calls him
the first of the four khalifas 'صاحب قرب' without noting it" (p. 120, folio 108, and حاشیہ 3; chunk
011). The same shape recurs at book scale: **`shrine-of-makhdoom-jahaniyan-jahangasht`** died 1384 CE
while Rukn-e Alam died 1335, "a khalifa surviving his pir by forty-nine years, which the chunk does
not remark on" (chunk 015); and Hassu Teli (d. 1012 AH) is the murid of a Shah Jamal who died **1050
AH**, thirty-eight years later — "not impossible, but the book neither notices nor explains it, and
**it is possible that entry no. 119's 'شاہ جمال لاہوری' is not entry no. 123's Shah Jamal**" (pp.
180–181, 185; chunk 017).

**C28. Two men of one name a generation apart, not identified with each other by the book.** Shah Abu
al-Maali's father is "**سید** محمد اشرف" in the text (p. 109, folio 97) and
"**شیخ** محمد اشرف کے حالات معلوم نہیں ہو سکے" in the footnote on the same page; and entry no. 63's
Abd al-Rashid Jalandhari also has a father named **Sayyid Ashraf** (p. 110, folio 98) — "two men of the
same name a generation apart, not identified with each other by the book" (chunk 010). Chunk 011 finds
the same shape again: Alim Allah Jalandhari's father is Sayyid Atiq Allah, who is entry no. 66's saint,
"consistent; but entry no. 71's genealogy runs to Zaid bin Imam Hasan while chunk 010's entry no. 66
says only 'سادات صحیح النسب جالندھری'. **Not contradictory, but the two entries carry different
amounts of pedigree for the same family**".

**C29. Abd al-Wahhab Bukhari Dehlvi has two pirs on one page and the editor does not say they can both
stand.** Text: "سید صدر الدین بخاری سے ان کی **بیعت** تھی". Editor: after Multan he went to Delhi and
"شیخ عبداللہ بن یوسف قرشی ملتانی سے **فیض** حاصل کیا". "*Bai'at* and *faiz* are not the same relation
and the two may both stand, **but the editor does not say so**" (p. 176, folio 174 as printed; chunk
016).

**C30. Nur al-Din Wali's second master, and Bulbul Shah's mosque.** Text: "**میر سید حسین سامانی**";
editor's note 2: "**سید احمد سامانی** بن کمال الدین بن محمود". "Husain or Ahmad — **the footnote does
not present itself as a correction**, so the discrepancy stands unexplained" (p. 204, folio 192; chunk
019). On the same page-run, the text has the Hazrat building the lofty mosque himself and the editor
has Rinchan building a mosque near the khanqah for his own prayers: "these may be two mosques or one;
**the chunk does not say**, and the editor does not mark a correction" (p. 203). The raja's identity is
given three ways — "نرسنجن [OCR?] دیو" (text), "رنچن" (editor), "مہاجن بن مہاجن ڈنگوس گرب [OCR?]" as
the "correct and full name" — with a Muslim name of Sadr al-Din: "**use the editor's dates and leave
the name as a set of variants**", and note that a Kashmir reign ending in 1323 CE and Bulbul Shah's
death in 727 AH "are not reconciled anywhere in the chunk".

**C31. Two prefatory pieces twenty-five years apart, and a plate later than both.** The *muqaddima* is
signed **12 December 1974** (p. 35, folio 23) and the *pesh lafz* **6 November 1999** (p. 14, folio 2).
"The introduction is therefore the 1974 original carried unchanged into the 2000 edition; **a claim
made in the introduction — for instance that the companion volume of sources will 'soon' appear
(p. 24, folio 12) — is a 1974 claim and must not be read as a statement of 1999**" (chunk 002). The
facsimile attestation is then dated **25 June 1975 [OCR?]**: "the plates and their captions were
therefore added after the introduction was written, and **a caption is not evidence of what the 1974
text says**" (p. 79; chunk 007). And the edition carries **a third present**: the editor obtained
material "**اپنے قیام کراچی ۴، ۳ دسمبر ۱۹۷۵ء کے دوران**" (p. 310, folio 298; chunk 029).

**C32. `data-darbar`'s two presents must not be collapsed.** "بارہ برس" and "۸۲۷ برس" are the
**author's**, not the editor's, "and this is a 1974 edition of a nineteenth-century text. The dome's
date, the Auqaf note and the weekly vigil therefore belong to **two different presents**: the vigil
and the dome to the author's time, the mosque's demolition and the Auqaf plan to the editor's (1974).
**An entry must not write that the Thursday vigil, the Friday milad or the dome's condition are
current**, and must not present the 1292 AH figure as the book's own statement of its date — the chunk
gives 827 and 465 and does not add them" (pp. 194–195; chunk 018). The same caution applies to Mian
Wada's langar and Quran school and their named holder Hafiz Ahmad al-Din (p. 188), and to the relics
"اب تک قلعہ میں موجود ہیں" / "بقبضہ سرکار انگریزی" where **which Faqirs were alive when the author
wrote cannot be settled from the syntax** (pp. 256–259; chunk 025).

**C33. An attributed corpus questioned three times in two chunks.** The editor doubts that two of Abd
al-Karim Lahori's listed works exist — "**ان کتابوں کے وجود کے بارے میں ہمیں ہنوز کوئی اطلاع نہیں
ہے**" — against the text's statement that they are "مقبول خاص وعام" (p. 104, folio 92, and حاشیہ 2;
chunk 009). Chunk 020 records the third: **the editor says he does not know whether *Tashrif
al-Shurafa* exists — the book that supplied `sakhi-sarwar`'s whole pedigree in chunk 019.**

**C34. The book holds both an eleventh-century rival to `data-darbar`'s primacy and a footnote that
abolishes him.** Shaikh Ismail Muhaddith is the first caller to Islam in Lahore and dies **448 AH**;
Data Ganj Bakhsh arrives and dies **465 AH**. "Move Ismail to **980 AH** and Data becomes the earlier
figure by five centuries. **The book therefore contains, unreconciled, both an eleventh-century rival
to Data Darbar's primacy and a footnote that abolishes him. Record both; do not let an entry present
either as settled.**" The Ghausi notice the editor prefers is itself badly transcribed, flagged in four
places, "though the year itself is legible at two separate points in the footnote" (pp. 191–192, folios
189–190 as printed; chunk 018).

**C35. Four deaths the editor destabilises without endorsing a replacement — the archive is left with
no usable date.**
- **Shah Kaku**: text 880 AH, the author's own **882 AH** elsewhere, and the editor's demonstration
  that the underlying tazkira gives **no year at all**, with 882 traced to Nami's inference from a
  description of an aged man. "**Shah Kaku's death year should not be carried at all.** Only the burial
  place, Lahore outside Delhi Gate, is uncontested here" (pp. 99–101, folios 87–89; chunk 009).
- **Shaikh Sondha**: the editor shows the 1119 AH in circulation is groundless **but does not affirm
  the text's 24 Jumada al-Awwal 1129 AH**. "Carry the text's date only with this note" (p. 111, folio
  99; chunk 010).
- **Shaikh Daud Gangohi**: text 5 Ramadan **1095**; editor "یک شنبہ رمضان **۱۰۷۳ھ**", Sunday, Ramadan
  1073, **with no day number**. "The day of the week and the month survive the correction but the day
  of the month does not. **The archive cannot state a full date either way**" (p. 108, folio 96; chunk
  010).
- **Muhammad Abid**: text 18 Ramadan **1160**; Faqir Muhammad Jhelumi **1166 AH**; "**the footnote
  breaks off without a verdict** and points to p. 297". The year in the text also carries an
  unexplained trailing word, "ہجری القدس [OCR?]" (pp. 142–143, folios 130–131; chunk 013).

**C36. Seven further flat overturns of the author by his editor, recorded for the pattern.** Each is a
death year the editor replaces, and in five of the seven a figure on one side is OCR-flagged.
Mulla Shah **1069** (text) / **1072** (editor), with a dated meeting in 1071 AH as the hinge (p. 69,
folio 57; chunk 006). Muhammad Sadiq Gangohi **18 Muharram 1058** (text) / **1039 AH[OCR?]** (the
author's own first edition) / **19 Muharram 1051** (editor's conclusion from *Hadaiq-e Daudi*) (p. 106;
chunk 009). Abu Said **1049[OCR?]** (text) / **1039 AH** (editor) — "ten years, and the flagged numeral
is the author's" (p. 105; chunk 009). Abd al-Ahad **1142** (text, numeral and words agreeing) /
**1126 AH** (editor) (pp. 141–142; chunk 013). Muhammad Masum **1080** (text) / **1079 AH** (editor,
on a bracketed and OCR-flagged authority, "(رکن چہار علویہ[OCR?])", which "cannot be identified from
the chunk") (p. 136; chunk 012). Abd al-Rahman Pak **1153 AH** (text) / **4 محرم ۱۱۱۵ھ [OCR?]**
(editor's family sources) — 38 years, with the mauza name flagged too (p. 81; chunk 007). Mir Muhammad
Yaqub **4 Muharram 1160 AH** (text) / the editor's ruling that 1160 belongs to **Fazl Ali Lahori** and
that the author elsewhere gives **9 Safar 1179 AH [OCR?]**: "**two of the three figures in the editor's
correction are OCR-flagged**, as is the name 'فضل علی یکدل [OCR?]' and the *Khazina* volume reference.
**A human must read this footnote from the PDF before either year is used**" (p. 216; chunk 021).

**C37. Sadr al-Din Arif's footnote is internally confused and cannot settle his dates.** "It opens by
saying the disagreement is about the **death** year, then treats **609 AH** as the value the 'family
record' confirms. **But 609 AH precedes his father's death in 661 or 666 AH, so 609 can only be a
birth year** — in which case it competes with Simnani's 611, not with 677 or 684, and the death-year
dispute (text 684, Firishta 677, Simnani 684) is left unresolved. **All five numerals in the footnote
are OCR-flagged.** The text's 23 Zi al-Hijja 684 AH is the only date with a day and a month, and it
agrees with Simnani" (p. 161, folio 149, حاشیہ 1; chunk 015). See N47.

**C38. The author's two books differ by 184 years and by 104 years on the same two men, and the
editor's dating of his own source depends on the figures the text contradicts.** Entry no. 136: Sayyid
Aziz al-Din Makki Lahori enters Lahore in **574 AH** and dies in **612 AH** (p. 201) — while chunk
018's footnote cited, from *Khazinat al-Asfiya* 2/255, "سید عزیز الدین **کی** لاہوری **ف ۷۹۶ھ
[OCR?]**", with the same unusual "کی" in the name, "and used him as an example of an eighth-century
figure". Entry no. 139: Miran Badshah dies **786 AH** (p. 203) against "سید اسحاق گازرونی لاہوری
**ف ۸۹۰ھ [OCR?]**". "**A gap of 184 years and a gap of 104 years between the author's two books, and
the editor does not remark on either here.** Both are worth surfacing because **chunk 018's editor used
exactly these two entries to date *Tuhfat al-Wasilin***: his argument depends on the 796 and 890
figures, and this chunk prints 612 and 786." The epithet "شیخ کی [OCR?]" is flagged in this chunk too
(chunk 019). Bears on **`peer-makki`** — see B21 and N58.

**C39. The `ranmal-sharif` disciples — four flat corrections in one chunk, two of the editor's own
preferred years flagged.** "مفتی صاحب کا مندرجہ سال وفات **۱۱۳۱ ھ غلط ہے**"; Shah Abdullah's death is
**1131** (author) or **1092** (editor); the editor's preferred years are themselves flagged in two of
the four cases — "**۱۰۹۴ ھ [OCR?]**" (p. 242) and "**۱۰۹۷ ھ [OCR?]**" (p. 243) — and his spelling of
the first disciple differs from the author's heading ("شیخ **مطا [OCR?]**" against "شیخ **مٹھا**",
pp. 240–241). Nanu's burial village is flagged, "موضع **کلاسکے [OCR?]** چیمہ ضلع گجرات"; **only
"ضلع گجرات پاکستان" is secure**. The chunk dates Naushah's own death only relatively — "ایک روز ان کی
وفات میں باقی تھا" when Shah Abdullah, "منصبدار ہفت [OCR?] ہزاری دربار عالمگیری", took bai'at — and
"**a human must decide which tradition the row follows before any of the four disciples' dates enter
it, and should check the row's 1654 against the 'Alamgiri court' placement of the deathbed bai'at. The
chunk itself does not date the court**" (pp. 240–243, folios 228–231; chunk 023). See N79, N80, N81.

**C40. Rajan Qattal — dates from two hands, a laqab from two etymologies, and a narrative with no
source.** Death 16 Jumada al-Akhir **827 AH** is given "بقول صاحب معارج الولایت", on the authority of
*Ma'arij al-Wilayat*, **not as the author's own** (p. 171, folio 159); birth 20 Shaban **760 AH / 1359
CE** is the editor's (p. 169, folio 157). "760 → 827 is 67 lunar years, which the chunk does not
state. The name is '**راجن قتّال**' in the heading, '**راجو قتال**' and '**راجن کتال**' in the
footnote." The conversion-and-execution case — Nawahon's status "عامل شاہی", the deathbed speech, the
Delhi hearing and the beheading — "is narrated **without a source citation in the text**; the editor's
six references at p. 170 are given for Rajan Qattal generally, not for this episode. **Treat as the
book's account, attributed**" (chunk 016).

**C41. Shaikh Mahmud Shah Lahori's death year differs between two chunks and neither figure is
flagged.** Text: **1280 AH** (p. 158, folio 146; chunk 015); the editor's footnote in chunk 014 gave
Sayyid Muhammad Latif's **1866 CE / 1283 AH** (p. 157, folio 145). "**Three years, and neither figure
is flagged.**"

**C42. A sentence that says two numerals are the same and prints them differently.**
"جس کا سال کتابت **۹۷۵ھ** ہے … اس نے شیخ اسحاق کا سال وفات اس کے ترقیمے میں **۹۷۴ھ** ہی درج کیا ہے
گویا سال کتابت و سال وفات مولف ایک ہی ہیں" — "**the sentence says the year of copying and the year of
death are the same, but prints 975 and 974. Unflagged. Carry both numerals as printed and do not
reconcile them**" (p. 344, folio 332; chunk 032).

**C43. Sher Shah's 947 and 948 are not the same event, and the editor's source title is flagged.** The
author: he "قلعہ دہلی کو سال نو سو سینتالیس میں **ویران** کیا"; the editor: he
"دہلی علائی اور کوشک سیری کو برباد کر کے … **۹۴۸ھ / ۱۵۴۱ء** میں ایک شہر **آباد** کیا". "Destruction
and founding; **compatible, but the editor's source title is flagged** ('اخبار الصنادید [OCR?]') and
his Persian quotation has 'بکجا شنیدن [OCR?]'" (p. 237, folio 225, and حاشیہ 2; chunk 023).

**C44. Two coincidences the chunks would not quote as facts.** Ghulam Murtaza Qasuri's **death** year
and his son Ghulam Muhi al-Din's **birth** year are both **1202 AH**, on facing pages — "possible, but
the text also makes Ghulam Murtaza the **grandfather**, so the coincidence is between a grandfather's
death and a grandson's birth. **Worth verifying rather than quoting as a fact about either**" (p. 152,
folio 140; chunk 014). And Bibi Awliya's death is "**چھ سو پچپن ہجری**" (655 AH) with
"**سلطان محمد تغلق بادشاہ دہلی**" named as her great devotee, while the editor's footnote instead
tracks her descendants to 999 AH: "the chunk gives no dates for that sultan. **The pairing needs
checking against a chronology before it is repeated**" (p. 252, folio 240; chunk 024).

**C45. Nine sentences whose own syntax the workers would not resolve.** Each was flagged because the
natural reading and the possible reading assign a fact to different people.
- **Who boasts and who stills the chairs** at Multan: "the passage uses 'حضرت' for both parties…
  the most natural reading is that **Baba Farid boasts and Baha al-Din Zakariya stills the chairs** —
  but the sentence 'اتنی بات حضرت کی زبان سے نکلی ہی تھی' can also refer to the speaker of the boast.
  **Do not attribute either speech to a named saint in an entry without checking the printed page**"
  (p. 160, folio 148; chunk 015). Touches **`shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar`** and
  **`shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya`**.
- **Baba Farid's father, or an unnamed nephew:** "ان کا باپ جمال الدین سلیمان ان کا بھانجا سلطان محمود
  غزنوی کا سلطان شہاب الدین غوری کے وقت کابل سے لاہور میں آیا" — "whether the father or an unnamed
  nephew is the subject of 'came from Kabul to Lahore', and whose nephew is meant, **cannot be settled
  from the transcription**" (p. 86, folio 74 as printed; chunk 007).
- **Farid's errand as stated does not match its execution:** "شیخ نجیب الدین متوکل کو **اپنے بھائی** کے
  لانے کے واسطے ہانسی کو روانہ کیا" followed by "**والدہ** کو ہمراہ لے کر اجودھن کو روانہ ہوئے", with
  Najib al-Din himself identified as Farid's brother two entries earlier. "**Whether 'اپنے بھائی' is
  an error for the mother is not resolvable from the chunk**; no `[OCR?]` is flagged on it" (p. 250,
  folio 238; chunk 024).
- **Who kills whom in the Khalji anecdote:** "…آئندہ چاند رات کو **وہ خسرو شاہ اپنے مصاحب کے ہاتھ سے
  مارا گیا**" — "the sentence can be read as the sultan being killed at the hand of his courtier
  Khusrau Shah, or as Khusrau Shah being killed; **the chunk does not disambiguate**, and no date is
  given" (p. 251, folio 239; chunk 024).
- **Where Shah Inayat is, or where Bulleh Shah must go:** "تیرے نصیب کا … شاہ عنایت قادری کے پاس ہے
  **تو بمقام قصور جائے گا تو حصہ پائے گا**" — "it can mean that **Shah Inayat is at Qasur**, or that
  **Bulleh Shah must go to Qasur**… The archive places Shah Inayat's shrine at Mozang Chungi, Lahore
  and Bulleh Shah's at Kasur, **so the reading matters. The chunk does not settle it and neither
  reading should be published as the book's.** One word in the clause is flagged ('بجزہ [OCR?]'),
  **which is exactly where the sense of 'portion' sits**" (p. 219, folio 207; chunk 021). Touches
  **`mazar-of-bulleh-shah`** and **`shrine-of-shah-inayat-qadiri`**.
- **Khawand Mahmud's arrival — entreaty or order:** the text's "**التجا**" against the editor's
  "**حکم**", "with a sectarian cause supplied only by the editor. **Any entry using this must give
  both; the editor's version has two Mughal chronicles behind it and the text's has nothing cited**"
  (p. 133, folio 121; chunk 012).
- **`bibi-pak-daman`'s burial clause does not parse:** "تاریخ وفات حضرت کی مادہ خورشید دین محمد سے
  حاصل ہوتی ہے۔ اور **مدفن گورستان بی بی پاکدامنان کی مادہ تاریخ ہے**" — "the second clause runs the
  burial place and the word 'chronogram' together in a way that does not parse. **The burial place is
  not in doubt… but the clause should be re-read from the PDF before it is quoted, in case a phrase
  has dropped out**" (p. 233, folio 221; chunk 022).
- **Mir Surkh Bukhari's age at the funeral miracle:** "ایک روز **مکان کی عمر** میں لڑکوں کے ساتھ کھیل
  رہے تھے" — "'مکان کی عمر' makes no sense as printed **and is not flagged, so this is a probable
  transcription fault rather than an OCR doubt**. He was plainly a child" (pp. 162–163, folios 150–151;
  chunk 015).
- **The Mughlani Begum kinship clause, and Pir Balkhi's epitaph.**
  "مغلانی بیگم احمد شاہ کی بہن اور **محمد شاہ کے بیٹے** کی شادی **اپنی بیٹی تیمور** کے ساتھ کر کے" —
  "'بیٹی تیمور' pairs a feminine noun with a masculine name, and 'محمد شاہ کے بیٹے کی' leaves the
  relation unfinished. **Whether the book means a son or a daughter of Durrani, and of Muhammad Shah,
  cannot be settled from the transcription**" (p. 256, folio 244; chunk 025). And "**(۱) یہ ایک کا
  مزار ہے**" — "'this is the mazar of one —', with the noun apparently missing (the epitaph says
  'الشھید'). Unflagged; **carry as printed**" (p. 351, folio 339; chunk 032).
